# Decisions taken

> Every settled decision with its reason and accepted price. Large: grep it, do not read it whole.
>
> Moved verbatim from CLAUDE.md (§13) on 2026-10-07. The § numbers are kept as stable anchors: `docs/README.md` maps every § to its file.

## 13. Decisions taken

**Each decision carries its status** in front of it (since 2026-10-10, issue #31): *[in force]*, *[superseded in v…]* where a later decision took its place — the later one names it —, *[partly superseded in v…]*, *[retired in v…]* where what it decided was removed. [`decisions-index.md`](decisions-index.md) lists them by theme, each by the beginning of its bold sentence, which is what to grep here. Splitting this file by theme was weighed and not done (D-11 of spec `2026-10-10-audit-issues-26-a-33`): a decision often settles two themes at once, a § reference names this file, and the index gives the way in a split would give without moving 450 entries; the file is the one in `docs/` allowed over 100 KB, by `tests/test_docs_coherence.py`.

- *[in force]* **A check's verdict is a key of -1, named by its utilisation, and printed only where named, settled (issue #58, 14.10.2026).** The engineer: «Le résultats pour les vérifications sont le verify et non pas le sigma. Le verify n'apparait même pas dans le volet de droite» and «le résultat doit également pouvoir recevoir le niveau 0». He chose one key per verdict -- `eta`, `eta_s`, `eta_y`, `eta_z`, `eta_m`, `eta_m_t`, `eta_m_c` -- over one key `verify` (D-01: one verdict can be hidden and another kept), and a verdict left out of `shown:` not printed, as any key (D-02). **It supersedes «A check's result is always printed at -1» (issue #39)** and the `1 | 2 | 3` of issue #41: the stresses are ordinary keys, nothing is locked in the window, and `lib/shown-variables.json` has no `results`. *Price*: more switches, named `eta_*`; a report written at -1 before, which counted on its verdict being printed, prints none until its `shown:` names it -- silently. Spec `docs/requirements/completed/2026-10-14-verdict-niveau-manuel.md`.
- *[in force]* **The «Formules» tab shows a domain as one text, each command a pill where Typst prints it, and keeps a blank line beside a command, settled (issue #40, 11.10.2026).** The engineer: «lorsqu'une commande est validée, elle doit s'afficher de manière réduite avec le nom de la variable, la valeur et l'unité … si la commande autorise dans Typst l'affichage en ligne … cette commande doit également s'afficher en ligne dans le volet central». The middle pane is one `QTextEdit`, a command an object character drawn as a pill (`formula_tab_widgets.PillPainter`, a `QPyTextObject`); where a pill stands is **derived** from its call, never stored (D-03): inline unless `note: "line"` on a `#var`, `#var-unit` or `#eq` -- `#val`'s `note: "line"` and `#verify` stay in their sentence, which a compiled test holds (`calc/domains/formula_layout.py`, `tests/test_formula_domain.py`). The file keeps its form, one command a line; **a blank line beside a command is now kept** as the paragraph it is for Typst (`formula_domain.tidy_items`, D-05, D-07: one at most, carried by the text, an empty text between two commands) -- until then `split_items` dropped it and two paragraphs ran into one. Enter beside a pill writes that blank line. A pill opens as its row of fields on a line of its own, the text parted round it (D-04); a drop inside an equation, a raw block, a call, a heading, a list's item or a comment is refused (D-06). *Price*: an equation's letters and numbers are no longer in the pane, only its answer (D-01); a block added at the end of a heading stands apart from the sentence after it, a blank line written between them.
- *[partly superseded in v0.118]* **The level -1 «Sélection manuelle» is four joined buttons per variable, `0 | 1 | 2 | 3`, the variables side by side, settled (issue #41, 11.10.2026)**, reversing issue #20's box and drop-down. The engineer: «le choix du niveau doit se faire non pas par un menu déroulant, mais par quatre petits boutons côte à côte fusionnés ensemble … la case à cocher n'est plus nécessaire». `display_view.LevelControl` puts a `chrome.SegmentedSwitch` (its `soft` variant: the button down in `accent-soft`, the bar's switches keeping their ink) per variable in a `flowbar.FlowLayout`; 0 is the variable left out of `shown:`; a check's result (issue #39) offers `1 | 2 | 3`, its 0 left out (the engineer's answer) -- *superseded by issue #58: every line, the verdicts included, offers `0 | 1 | 2 | 3`*. The call written is unchanged.
- *[in force]* **`#eq` prints inline by default, as `#var` does, settled (v0.117, issue #19), reversing v0.87's `note: "line"`.** The engineer: «#eq s'imprime en ligne par défaut, comme #var depuis v0.87, de sorte qu'il peut suivre un #var sur la même ligne ; note: "line" garde la ligne à part. Conséquence : l'aspect de chaque #eq déjà écrit change». `note: "footnote"` means inline for the four commands, written or not. The engine's checks keep their lines through one alias, `timber-eq` (his answer of 10.10.2026: «Elles gardent leur aspect»). **Accepted price**: every `#eq` already written without `note:` -- or with `note: "footnote"`, which until then kept a line of its own -- changes its look, and consecutive ones run on in one paragraph; the «Formules» tab writes the default.
- *[partly superseded in v0.117]* **The level of detail -1 lists what is printed: `level: -1, shown: (A: 2, W_y: 1)`, settled (v0.117, issue #20).** *A member check's result is always printed since issue #39 (11.10.2026, «A check's result is always printed at -1» below): «a result not chosen not printed» holds for the connectors and the polar moment only.* The engineer's form, with `shown:` for `show:`, a keyword of Typst (10.10.2026). Per call only, like 0; the title and the verdict always printed, a heading where a key under it is chosen, a figure where a key is chosen at 2 or 3, a result not chosen not printed; one list for `show-check-member`'s member and check, a key both print being the check's. **What a command may name is `lib/shown-variables.json`'s**, read by Typst (a key it does not print refused) and by Python (the window's boxes) -- a superset of what one call prints, a key it does not apply to being accepted and ignored; `show-calc`'s keys are the export's. The window never asks for a name: a box and a level per variable. **Accepted price**: a `verify-*` printed at -1 shows its verdicts as at level 1, and a member's compact level-2 lines have no -1 form.
- *[in force]* **The «Formules» tab writes what a line is in «Calcul» and how it is printed in «Affichage», into the same call, settled (v0.117, issue #19).** The engineer's answer of 10.10.2026: the arguments set in «Affichage» are written into `Formules/<id>.typ`, the include recompiled; one declaration per command, each option tagged with its view (`Option.view`); the two views merge, neither erasing the other's options. The call to copy stays the domain's `#include`.
- *[in force]* **The look of a project is a form: a folder `Template/` at the Typst root, `config.typ` keeping only the language and the engineer's own adjustments, settled (v0.114), reversing v0.112's styles.** The engineer, on seeing the BFH template's body text and tables in Libertinus Serif — Typst's own default — beside headings in Lucida Sans: «on parle beaucoup de templates sans comprendre si on parle d'un template de forme ou de fond». A look had to be separable from data, hand-editable, and switchable without losing what he adjusted; his answer, of the places put to him, was a folder `Template/` at the Typst root — a Typst function called from `config.typ`, a `form.json`, a `title.typ` dedicated to each form — with `config.typ` keeping the language and his own `set`/`show` rules, which win. Chosen over the style as one monolithic `config.typ` replaced wholesale on a switch — which lost his hand edits and kept the language and the look in one file — and over a Typst package, which a project's own folder does not need. Accepted price: a form is three files that must agree (the tokens `template.typ` exports, the `title.typ` that imports them, the call in `config.typ`); `Template/template.typ` and `title.typ` are replaced at every switch (the old ones archived), so an edit made in them is lost to the next form — his adjustments belong in `config.typ`; and what was edited by hand inside a monolithic `config.typ` does not pass into the new one, which the question says before it is asked.
- *[in force]* **The project templates are dissolved into one form and three contents, settled (v0.114).** The engineer: the BFH project template is only a form, and the Standard project template is dissolved into a form (Neutre) and content templates; a project is made from one layout template and, for each of its three documents, one content template. The project template of v0.94 mixed the look and the sections in one skeleton, which is what made «template» unanswerable; it is chosen against keeping it beside the two new kinds, which would have been three kinds where he wanted two and the one that caused the confusion kept. «Modèle» is no longer a word of Woodcore, for either kind: FR «template de forme» / «template de fond», DE «Formvorlage» / «Inhaltsvorlage», EN «layout template» / «content template», their places «Intégrés», «Partagés» (the office's folder) and «Mes templates». Accepted price: a project template the engineer saved before forms is neither a form nor a content template — it is read only as legacy (`--template`, the old creation route) and keeps its own look unless a form is asked for — and the Settings page no longer lists project templates.
- *[in force]* **The report has content templates, and switching any document's content is a comparison, settled (v0.114).** The engineer: the report gets content templates too, with the same comparison as the convention and the base; changing a document's content template after the project exists is rare and must be possible, and must never cost what he wrote — a tree of the document against the template, sections to keep, take or drop, nothing deleted but archived. Chosen over replacing the document with the template, which loses his writing; over copying only the sections the document lacks, which can neither take a template's version of a section that differs nor drop one; and over a choice object by object, which the objects' ids make possible and a reader could not follow. Accepted price: the grain is the section — the objects under it are shown, greyed, to say where two versions part, and are not chosen; `take` replaces a differing section's text wholesale (the document's file archived); the report's sections are matched by their name without their number, so a section renamed by hand is a `document_only` and a `template_only` and not one section; and the engineer reads a two-sided tree to decide.
- *[in force]* **The BFH form sets every piece of text in Lucida Sans, settled (v0.114).** The defect that began it: `config.typ` set Lucida Sans on the headings and nothing on the body, so the text and the tables came out in Libertinus Serif, Typst's own default, and the BFH page read as two typefaces. His answer: all of it — the body, the tables, the captions, the outline, the headings, the header and the footer — as the BFH's Word template does; chosen over the body alone or the headings alone, each of which keeps a second face. Accepted price: the formulas keep Typst's math font (`New Computer Modern Math`) and code its monospace, which no text face replaces; Lucida Sans is not bundled — `Segoe UI`, then `Arial`, stand in where it is not installed, so a PDF compiled on a machine without it differs; and a project's pages reflow once the BFH form is applied.

- *[in force]* **Positions are Woodcore's own JSON, and Positionen.xlsx is imported once, settled (v0.114), reversing v0.94's writer and §7's workbook.** The engineer: «le fichier Excel des positions ne sera plus utilisé … son export à l'enregistrement est uniquement un JSON». His answer of the options put to him: a one-time import at the first opening, the workbook **moved** to `_Archiv/`, and an «Importer depuis Excel…» button kept for an old project. Chosen over a clean break, which would have had him retype his positions, and over keeping Excel as a second source, which is the duplication §2 rule 1 forbids. Accepted price: the drop-down lists his workbook carried (types, materials, grades) are built in now — STE's types, a short list of materials, SIA 265's and SIA 263's classes, plus what the project uses; and a test never migrates his own project (`test_the_export_is_reproducible` skips until it has its `positions.json`).
- *[in force]* **Only id and type are required of a position, settled (v0.114).** «Tout le reste ne doit pas être nécessaire … ne doit pas produire une erreur critique.» A blank section, strength class or moisture class is a warning at the export and a reason in `#member`, the values written in the call; the record is schema 1.4. Chosen over requiring a section for a timber element, which would have stopped the blank positions the plan tab now creates.
- *[in force]* **CAD through ezdxf, DWG through the ODA File Converter if installed, settled (v0.114).** His answer of three: nothing proprietary ships with Woodcore, and a DWG is converted by Open Design Alliance's free converter where the engineer installed it; LibreDWG (GPL) was the alternative. Accepted price: a DWG needs an install of his, and a CAD sheet is scaled by calibration alone.
- *[in force]* **A hidden PDF layer is stripped from the report's page, settled (v0.114).** Measured first: PDFium honours `/D /OFF` and Typst's PNG render does too, but Typst's PDF export warns «PDF contains optional content groups» and may draw them. So the window renders a copy with the default configuration changed, and the report gets the page with the hidden content removed (`flatten_page`) — the one way the deliverable is certain to hide what the engineer hid. Accepted price: 1–2 s to flatten a large page, paid once per export of a changed page.
- *[in force]* **The storey designation and the numbering character are two fields, settled (v0.114).** His answer: the designation free and of any length with a list per language, the character (STE: U, 0, 1 …) proposed from it and editable — an attic takes the highest storey number plus one. Chosen over deriving the character by a fixed rule, which a «Mezzanine» defeats, and over dropping it from the numbering.
- *[in force]* **Export sheets live in `<typst_root>/Plans/`, a `.typ` the source of truth, settled (v0.114).** His answer: a visible folder of the project, beside the three documents, opened in VS Code as they are; Woodcore edits tagged objects and follows his edits (§13 v0.112's rule). Every sheet command takes named arguments only, `typst_objects` parsing nothing else.
- *[in force]* **A plan is saved when left, and asks only when the save is refused, settled (v0.114).** «La sauvegarde doit se faire de manière automatique et il ne faut pas confirmer.» A rename still asks — it breaks the calls naming the old id.
- *[in force]* **No page-picker popup: a document is cut into plans in the centre, settled (v0.114).** «Il n'y a pas une fenêtre pop-up … c'est dans le volet central.» A page ticked is a plan; a plan without a crop shows its whole page, one with a crop its crop alone.
- *[in force]* **A convention's and a base's headings are written at the level they print at, and a chapter is a file, settled (v0.113), reversing v0.111's offsets.** The engineer, reading a section file in VS Code: «dans le document type, il doit y avoir deux égales», and a chapter's title «dans un fichier .typ, éditable soit à la main soit dans Woodcore». v0.111 had every file write `=` and the entry give it its level with `set heading(offset: …)`, which made a file independent of its depth and showed the engineer a level that is not the one printed. Chosen over keeping the offsets, which is what reads in VS Code as a mistake, and over writing the chapters' titles in the entry, which is generated and must not be edited by hand. Accepted price: a section moved to another depth has its headings rewritten (outside its shared blocks), and a shared section's title stands **before** its shared block, each document writing its own at its own level — so renaming it is no longer shared. Projects made before are converted on a button, never silently.

- *[in force]* **A choice and a checklist print the answer alone by default, settled (v0.113).** The engineer: only the selected choices are printed, the right pane offering all. The default of `only-selected` and `only-checked` went from `false` to `true`; a document that wants every option says `false`. Accepted price: every existing choice and checklist of a project prints differently once its engine is updated, which the version's notes say.

- *[in force]* **An object taken out is not asked about, and can be put back where it stood, settled (v0.113).** The engineer: no alert; the object stays visible as removed; a right click reintegrates it right after the end tag of the object before it, tolerating what was written between. The copy was already archived before removal (v0.112), so nothing is lost without a question; what changed is that its place is remembered, in `Sections/archives/removed.json`, by **neighbours' ids and not by line numbers**, which do not survive an edit in VS Code. A section is still asked about.

- *[in force]* **The title page is a `title.typ` shared by the three documents, settled (v0.113).** The engineer's choice of «title.typ + .woodcore»: the rows — authors, client, architect, addresses —, how each is set (inline, bold, one per line) and rows of his own are written in a file he edits in VS Code, the values defaulting to the project's details; `config.typ` keeps the look alone. It writes its words in the document's language, where the BFH title page printed French whatever the language.

- *[in force]* **The settings' templates are one page of three places, and a template is saved from a project, never written in the settings, settled (v0.113).** The engineer: merge «Modèles» and «Modèles de document» with a switch per document; Intégré, Partagé, Mes modèles; import a folder into «Mes modèles»; no creating or editing a template «en code»; one «Enregistrer» left of «Fermer». The template's sections editor of v0.94 is gone with it.

- *[in force]* **A long operation blocks the window behind a loading card, and hover still works, settled (v0.113).** The engineer's choice of «bloquée avec l'icône» over a busy cursor alone: the window was freezing for 5 to 9 s while its tabs were built (measured: `MainWindow` 8.6 s, `reload` 5.2 s, `rebuild` 4.7 s). `busy.py` puts a card in the middle, holds input with an application-wide filter that lets hover and tooltips through, and pumps the event loop between tabs (`ExcludeUserInputEvents`); Qt-free work runs in a thread. Accepted price: the build is a little longer for the pumping, and a click during it is dropped, not queued.

- *[in force]* **A recent project whose file is gone is forgotten, and «Convertir un ancien dossier» leaves the start page, settled (v0.113).** The engineer's «Effacer» and «Le supprimer»: a greyed row nobody can open is noise, and the conversion only served folders laid out before Woodcore, of which none is left on his machines; `woodcore-cli convert <folder>` keeps it for whoever needs it.

- *[in force]* **A document is edited in one tab, and no Typst code is ever shown in Woodcore, settled (v0.112), reversing the first build's «Sections» tab.** The engineer's third round of 01.10.2026: one tab, «Structure du document», in the three documents; the «Sections» tab with its Typst code editor disappears; no Typst code is ever shown in Woodcore, and the engineer edits code in VS Code. The first build had two tabs — the structure beside a Typst code editor coloured as the Python tab's, with its own preview — which was the first round's design. What makes the rule livable is that the tab **follows the disk**: a file the engineer writes in VS Code is read again, the pane of the object chosen included, so that Woodcore and VS Code are two views of the same files and never two copies (§3.3octodecies, §10quater). Chosen over keeping both editors, which is two editors for one text. Accepted price: **a file Woodcore cannot read — tags that do not pair — is not repaired in Woodcore**: it is refused by name and corrected in VS Code, and an object written in a form Woodcore does not write is rewritten after a warning rather than shown as code.

- *[in force]* **Every heading, every paragraph and every table is an object, and the report's files are tagged on request, settled (v0.112).** The first build tagged tables and the like; the rework made a heading and a paragraph objects too, so that a paragraph can be dragged to another section, shared, imported and previewed alone, which a tree of files and tables could not do. The templates are regenerated so (ids `title`, `h2`, `p1` …, the same in the three languages, so that a shared section has the same ids in both documents). ⚠️ **The report's existing files are not tagged automatically**: Woodcore shows their headings, and «Baliser ce fichier» tags one file on request — tagging being a write into text the engineer wrote, which no action of his asked for. Accepted price: two comment lines round every paragraph of a CU or a BP, which VS Code shows, and ids — `p3` — that say nothing of what a paragraph is; the tree names a paragraph by its first words.

- *[in force]* **A paragraph is a plain text box holding its markup as written, settled (v0.112).** The proposed minimal editor was accepted: no toolbar, no syntax colours, the document's own words in the window's font, `TextModel` refusing as it is typed what cannot be one paragraph (a blank line, a heading line, an unclosed bracket) and keeping the last valid body. There is no rich editor. Accepted price: markup — `*bold*`, `$q_k$` — is visible as typed.

- *[in force]* **The tree is chapters › sections › objects, several chosen at once, and moved by drag and drop, settled (v0.112).** Sections and chapters anywhere in the tree, objects within their file or into another, above or below another object; a drop is **never Qt's own move**: it says what was dragged and where, the model moves it, and the tree is read again from the files, so that what is on screen is always what is on disk and a drop whose write fails leaves nothing half moved. Sections and objects are not moved together, an object is not put into a chapter, and a heading of a file with no tags is not moved alone — each refusal said, and the way out named. The report has no chapters: its tree is its `#include` lines and the headings of each file, with the `.typ` mark that says which are files of their own (the indicator §14 had listed).

- *[in force]* **The preview is the page of what is chosen, not of the document, and not a live preview, settled (v0.112), replacing the first build's page of the whole document scrolled to the section.** The rework: a chapter shows only that chapter, a section only itself, an object only that object, nothing chosen the document — compiled as a **small snippet** in about half a second, and not a live Tinymist-style preview. The first round's design was the compiled PDF in the centre with a click scrolling to the heading (`structure.align`, which stays, and the whole-document compile of «Compiler l'aperçu»). A snippet is compiled **from standard input** against the project's folder as the root, so nothing is written into the project to render it, and with the object's file preamble and the section's heading offset, so that it prints as it will in the document. Accepted price: the snippet has no page context — no page number, no break, no running figure number — and an object relying on a `#let` written after the first tag of its file is previewed without it.

- *[in force]* **The right pane offers the options of what is chosen, by its kind, settled (v0.112), where the first round offered a section's title and whether it is enabled and nothing else.** The first round's rule — no page-break option, nothing else, the engineer asking for more as he needs it — stands for a section, whose pane offers its title, its file, whether it is printed and its sharing; the rework adds, per kind of object, the options that make it editable without code: a table's rows (add, remove, up, down) and the fields of the row chosen, each column shown or hidden but **kept**, its caption and notes; the live loads' category in a drop-down of Tabelle 8 and the two values overridable in orange; a choice's and a list's options; the signatories; the norms to name, from the catalogue and the project's `references.bib`; a plan's and a picture's arguments. Hiding a column keeps it in the call, which the engineer's own example needed: in the live-load table he hides the first column, selects the first data row, writes the use in the column «Nutzung» and picks the category in the right pane, and the `kN/m²` and `kN` columns fill themselves.

- *[in force]* **What the documents share stays in step by orange, import and ignore, never by overwriting, settled (v0.112), reversing the first build's alert and overwrite.** The first round's rule: a block edited in Woodcore lists in an alert the documents whose copies differ and will be overwritten, Woodcore then writes its version into every copy, and an outside edit is not synchronised at all. The rework replaced it: **in a document, a shared file or object is orange when its copy in another document changed since this document's copy was last synchronised, red when both changed, and «Importer les modifications» takes the other version between the tags only — a conflict asking which version to keep —, «Ignorer» being remembered per document and per file or object until «Réactiver»**; and **Woodcore's own right-pane edit follows the same mechanism**, writing this document's copy alone, so that the other documents turn orange and import when the engineer decides, as an edit made in VS Code does. What overwriting would have meant is a write into three documents, two of them possibly being edited in VS Code, on the strength of a dialog, and an edit in VS Code and an edit in Woodcore being two different things. ⚠️ Two hashes per relation and not one base per block (`own` and `seen`), because «keep mine» means *seen and kept*, which one hash cannot say; the first reading of a project that was never synchronised puts equal copies in step and differing ones in conflict, for want of a record to say who changed; two copies found equal are in step whatever they held before. Accepted price: documents may stay out of step for as long as the engineer does not import, a `sync.json` per document that Woodcore alone writes, and — for now — no JSON schema for it (§14).

- *[in force]* **The record of what is shared is a `sync.json` beside each document's entry file, settled (v0.112).** A document's folder is what its archive holds and what a migration moves, so the record travels with the copies it describes; a template never carries it (`templates.SYNC_FILE`), a document made from one having no copy of another document yet; and each document's record is its own view, which is what «ignored» is. `shared_with` in `structure.json` says that a file is shared and not where it stood, and stays what the tags say, written down for the tree (`reconcile`): **the tags are the truth**.

- *[in force]* **A section imported from another document is linked unless the engineer says otherwise, settled (v0.112).** «Importer depuis un autre document»: the document chosen dynamically, a tree of its sections and objects with a box each, and **«copie indépendante»**, unticked; what is imported stays linked (shared), a whole section arriving at the bottom of the tree and **objects imported alone arriving in a new section «Importé»** at the bottom, which the engineer then fills by drag and drop. A section ticked with some of its own objects is the section; an object standing in a larger shared block is already linked through it and is refused linked rather than shared twice. ⚠️ **An object's id is the same in every copy of its block**, the synchronisation comparing objects by id, so an object that meets an id of the destination takes a fresh one in the source and in the copy: `title` is in every section.

- *[in force]* **ψ is never shown for a live load, settled (v0.112), reversing the first build's optional columns.** The first round asked for ψ0, ψ1 and ψ2 of SIA 260 Tabelle 2 as optional columns of the live-load table, to be transcribed from the norm — and they were, with three variables on every instance of the domain. The rework: **never shown for live loads**, removed from the table, from the instances and from the templates, **Tabelle 2 staying transcribed** in `calc/norms/` for the load combinations to come (§3.5). A hidden column would have been a field nobody can see the effect of (v0.70's own argument for dropping `plates.thickness`). Snow's ψ stays **the formula in h0 as the norm prints it** — the engineer's round-two answer — and is never evaluated, the norm giving no lower bound and no Swiss site lying below 60 m a.s.l. Accepted price: the base de projet's `reduction-factors` section still prints Tabelle 2 as a hand-written table, which the engineer is to rule on (§14).

- *[in force]* **A table cell never carries a footnote, whatever the report's level: `doc-value`, settled while building (v0.112), not asked of the engineer.** The first round's rule was that a variable inserted into a table cell is written `#val(…, domain: …)`, so that the value follows the calculation; `#val` puts its label, description and citation into one footnote at level 3, and `uniform-level` forces that level everywhere, so a table of ten values would carry ten footnotes. `doc-value` prints the value alone, with no unit, no label and no footnote, unaffected by the level, and registers its norm for the bibliography; the live-load table's `auto` cells and a variable put in a cell are written with it. Accepted price: a second command for one value. To be confirmed by the engineer (§14).

- *[in force]* **A shared section keeps one id in both documents, settled (v0.112).** The round-two answer: shared sections keep the same id in CU and BP — the live loads are `live-loads` in both —; they have identical titles and content, and **the same id means the same section**, so a shared section cannot be renamed and a copy in the report takes the file's stem without its number. A shared section is copied as a file and a common file name is the natural spelling; distinct ids (`cu-live-loads`, `bp-live-loads`) would have needed a `shared_id` to record that they are one section. Consequence classes CC1 to CC3 are kept in `reliability-differentiation` with their source (SN EN 1990 Annex B), SIA 260 leaving the differentiation of reliability to the engineer.

- *[in force]* **The two documents come as complete templates, with no client data, and Musterprojekt's skeletons are replaced, settled (v0.112).** The round-one rule: **one template per mode, as complete as possible, in French, German and English, built from the examples and from SIA 260, generic text, no client data** — no name, address, figure or date of the BFH's and the office's examples, the repository being shared. The catalogue is the maintainers' and is **not shipped**, its sources lying outside the repository; the generator is deterministic and the templates are committed. The union of the examples is ordered as SIA 260 orders its contents, each section carrying the clause it answers; **the Technischer Bericht is not a mode** and went into the base's structural concept and ground. Building physics, acoustics and fire protection stay in the base, as in the BFH's template, from norms, enabled by default, their interpretation to be confirmed: thermal protection SIA 180:2014, energy SIA 380/1:2016, sound insulation SIA 181:2020, room acoustics SIA 181/1:2026 and the VKF directives. **Musterprojekt's two skeletons**, unmodified, were replaced by the full templates on the engineer's word (the old ones are in `_Archiv/squelettes-261001-20h59`), which is why `tests/projet-exemple` carries them too. The style is **neutral and the project's own `config.typ`**; another style is chosen at creation and — if it was simple, and it was — switched mid-project, keeping the document's language; no list of the project's participants is added to the project file, the title page using the details it has; the signatures are a parametric block.

- *[in force]* **The norms the library lacks are found online and cited with the page they were read from, settled (v0.112).** The round-two answer: SIA 262, 264, 266, 267, 118, 180, 181, 232/1, 271, 414/2, 500, 380/1 and 181/1 — and the VKF directives, 19 of them in the library in French only — are found with their **current** reference and edition and entered in `calc/norms/norms.json`, each with a `_source` naming the page: SIA 262 is the **2025** edition, the 2013 one superseded since 01.11.2025 and given no alias on purpose, so that a citation of it is one that needs a look; SIA 181/1 is 2026. Where a revision is announced and not in force — SIA 232/1, SIA 500, the BSV 2026 — the entry names the edition in force. Accepted price: eight SIA entries and the VKF's German titles rest on web pages and not on a PDF of the library, so §2 rule 13 is met by a page that can be turned to on the web and not on the shelf (§14).

- *[in force]* **A document template is saved from a document, in three places, and offered when a project is created, settled (v0.112).** The round-one phase 6: a choice of sections shown or hidden with the values the user pre-filled — «Template — Convention d'utilisation courte» —, available when a project is created, and in the three languages. Saved as the document **as the engineer wrote it**, its enabled flags as they stand, without its archives, PDFs, journal, pictures and `sync.json`; usually in one language, offered only to a project in that language, and saving again in another language adds it; **a template replaced or deleted is moved to `archives/`, never deleted**, as every «Supprimer» of this window is. Three places, as a project template has: builtin (read only), the user's and the office's shared folder.

- *[in force]* **A new project opens in the convention d'utilisation, and no console window may flash, settled (v0.112).** The round-three answers: a project begins with its agreement, so the drop-down starts there, an existing project keeping the document last shown; and a terminal that opens for a fraction of a second whenever Typst compiles is a defect, solved in one place — `calc/proc.py` — and **held by a test that reads the sources**, because a start added next month would bring it back and nothing would say so.

- *[in force]* **The report is the third of three documents, and nothing is renamed, settled (v0.111).** The engineer's rule of 30.09.2026: nothing of the report is renamed, it is simply the third mode. What is called a convention d'utilisation (SIA 260's service criteria agreement) and a base de projet (its basis of design) joins the report; the report keeps its entry file, `Statischer Rapport.typ`, its PDF and its `layout.report` and `layout.pdf`, so every caller of `report_typ` and `report_pdf` — and Tinymist, which names the PDF after the `.typ` — goes on meaning the report, and every route that names its one deliverable (§12) is unchanged. Chosen over renaming the report's entry per language and over symmetrical keys (`report_rs`). Accepted price: the two new documents are `report_cu`/`pdf_cu` and `report_bp`/`pdf_bp` beside `report`/`pdf`, a little asymmetric, which a reader of the `.woodcore` learns once.
- *[in force]* **The Typst root is the project's own folder, and the three documents share one `_woodcore/`, settled (v0.111).** Chosen over one root per document — which would copy the engine and the export three times, or reach them by `../../` paths that change with the depth — and over a root above the project. What the three share goes up to the project's folder (`config.typ`, `commands.typ`, `references.bib`, `Images/`, `RFEM-Export/`, `_woodcore/`, the engineer's own: one style, one engine, one export); every file writes its imports **from the root**, `/commands.typ` and `/Images/…`, valid at any folder depth, equal to what Tinymist sees and — the reason that will matter — **independent of the document a section lies in**, so a section copied into another document needs no edit. `project: "references.bib"` is read from the root by the engine (`read("/" + path)`), so a `references.bib` at the top also ends the disagreement between Tinymist and the command line that a report's own folder had. ⚠️ Accepted price: the root now holds the workbooks, the architect's plans and `_Archiv/`, so **nothing may walk `typst_root` any more** — `archive_export` and `variables` did, and no longer do (`members_of_document`, `stamps.enumerated`) —, and VS Code has to open the project's folder (§12).
- *[in force]* **`DEFAULT_LAYOUT` stays as it is, and one key says which layout a project has, settled (v0.111).** Musterprojekt and the example project stated no layout and read the old one from that constant, so renaming a default would have lost both (v0.100's lesson, `DEFAULT_LAYOUT` is load-bearing). `layout.typst_root` marks a project laid out in three documents, **all seven keys of `MODE_KEYS` or none** — `Project.__init__` refuses a partial set naming what is missing — and a new or migrated project states its layout **in full** (`project.full_layout`, written by `write` and `update`), so its `.woodcore` reads nothing from `DEFAULT_LAYOUT` and says where every part lies. Chosen over a `mode` or `version` key, a second thing that could disagree with the layout, and over changing the default. `tests/test_project.py` pins `DEFAULT_LAYOUT` as a literal, so the day somebody moves it the test says why not.
- *[in force]* **A project is migrated by renaming, proved against a reference taken first, and undone automatically, settled (v0.111).** The engineer's rules: never delete, move; and the verification v0.82's step 4 made — the export and the PDF's text identical to a reference taken before the first move. Chosen over copying into a new tree — which doubles a 30 MB project, leaves two to keep in step, and hides the mistakes a rename shows at once — and over a migration checked by eye. **His two loosenings**, both accepted: «`data/` identical» means identical **modulo the old→new RFEM path prefix** (the export records the RFEM file's path, and that is what moved), and the same for the PDF's text; the day the title page prints is also excluded, being the day. The backup and the write-ahead log are written **before** the reference, so a rollback is a true undo of everything the migration did, the reference's export and engine sync included; `--rollback` refuses where the engineer changed something since. **The real project is migrated on his go and never by a test** (§11ter). Accepted price: about a minute and a 40 MB `before.zip` for a project like Musterprojekt, offered once, «Plus tard» remembered, the project working as one report meanwhile.
- *[in force]* **A document is compiled only if its Typst changed — by a digest of its content, from Typst's own list of what it read, settled (v0.111).** The engineer's rule. Chosen over modification times — NextCloud dates files, the export rewrites identical JSON, an editor saves without a change: each a ten-second compile for nothing —; over a hash of a set of files enumerated by hand — a second list of what a document reads, which would age the day a command reads a new file —; and over comparing the PDF's bytes, which carry a creation date. `typst compile --deps` (0.15.1) is what makes the list exact, and the enumerated set is the **fallback**, said in the stamp. Accepted price: a stamp costs 0.25 s; **a file saved while Typst compiles is not caught** — the `unsettled` rule catches what is modified after the PDF and cannot tell that from what was there before; a Typst other than the stamp's recompiles every document once; and `stamps/` is the machine's own, gitignored and left out of every archive, since an archive holds no PDF to compare it with.
- *[in force]* **One journal per document, and a convention's revision keyword is read from its own journal, settled (v0.111).** The engineer's decision: each document prints its own «Historique des versions». Chosen over one journal with a document field, which a reader of one document would have to filter and which would make every journal edit a change of every document. The keyword of the report's PDF stays in `data/project.json`, as it has been since v0.99; a convention's and a base's come from `data/journal-cu.json` and `data/journal-bp.json`, because a revision of another document in `project.json` would make the report look changed every time the convention is — and the export-only-if-changed rule would recompile it for nothing. `journal-entries(document: "rs")` defaults to the report and not to `auto`: a function returning an array cannot ask a state.
- *[in force]* **The document is a drop-down that hides rows and does not rebuild the window, settled (v0.111).** His design: a drop-down above the group «Projet»; the report every tab it had; the two others a subset. Chosen over a group of tabs per document, three windows, and a sidebar section each: the same modules — positions, plan, variables, commands — serve the three, and only the journal and (later) the structure are a document's own. **No `rebuild()`**, which carries each tab's `view_state` through and would carry a journal entry half written into another document's journal; `MODE_BOUND` tabs are asked `confirm_leave` first. The document shown is remembered per project. Accepted price: a tab that holds a document's own state implements `on_mode`, and the drop-down is not shown where a project has one document.
- *[in force]* **Each document is archived apart, RFEM-Export included, settled (v0.111).** His answer: the RFEM exports are in every archive. One zip per document compiled, `<timestamp>-<mode>.zip`, chosen **by name** — the document's folder, the shared files, `_woodcore/` less `out/` and `stamps/`, the pictures, the RFEM exports, the sources, the three journals — and never by walking the Typst root, which would sweep in the other documents, the workbooks and the plans. Chosen over one archive of the whole project, which would make the archive of a convention carry the report's every revision. Accepted price: an export of three documents writes three zips, each a little alike.
- *[in force]* **The three folders are the engineer's exact forms, named at creation, settled (v0.111).** `1_Convention d'utilisation`, `2_Base de projet`, `3_Rapport de statique`; `1_Nutzungsvereinbarung`, `2_Projektbasis`, `3_Statischer Bericht`; `1_Service criteria agreement`, `2_Basis of design`, `3_Statical report` — numbered so that a file manager lists them in the documents' order, in the project's language, offered in «Nouveau projet» and editable there, and stated in the `.woodcore`, **never recomputed from the table** afterwards. They replace v0.100's one question about the report's folder, and `templates.REPORT_FOLDERS["en"]` changed with them. The standard template **stays report-shaped on disk** (`templates/projet/`): the folder a template keeps is an implementation detail every project renames, and a report-shaped skeleton is laid out as it is copied — each part straight to where it goes, the shared files to the top and the report into its new folder, never copied and then renamed (`_copy_skeleton`) — so that nothing of the existing template, its tests or the engineer's own templates had to move. The names are written out rather than composed from number and name: they are the engineer's words, and `tests/test_documents.py` holds the two agreeing all the same.
- *[in force]* **Tinymist's root is stated, and a probe decided it, settled (v0.111).** `"tinymist.rootPath": "${workspaceFolder}"` — Tinymist refuses a relative root, and the VS Code extension substitutes the variable. The engineer accepted it as a safety net beside «VS Code opens the project's folder». What decided it was a probe of Tinymist 0.15.8's language server on a migrated copy (§12): the project's folder as the root resolves `/commands.typ` from a section in a document's folder and a document's folder as the root does not — so a document's folder opened alone, which is what a workspace at the wrong level gives, is precisely the failure the stated root prevents. Accepted price: VS Code reads the file only when the project's folder is the folder it opened, so the safety net is itself one the engineer has to have opened the right folder to have (§14).
- *[in force]* **The decisions of phases 2 to 6, taken with the engineer and recorded before they are built (v0.111).** Each lowest-level section of a convention or a base is **its own `.typ`**, named by a **stable English id** with no number prefix (`live-loads.typ`), and the order, the nesting and the enabled state live in a `structure.json` beside the entry file, which generates the entry's `#include` lines between two tags (`structure.sync_entry`); a disabled section keeps its file. **The report has none**: its sections may hold several headings in one file and its order stays in its `#include` lines. **The `.typ` is the source of truth** of a parametric object, which stands between a START and an END tag so that Woodcore finds it, parses it and writes it again — and **warns before overwriting what was written by hand**. **The unit of sharing is the whole file**, copied into the other documents' folders; **only what lies between shared tags stays synchronised**, each copy being free outside them, and an edit made in Woodcore lists the copies that differ before it writes. **One template per mode, as complete as possible, in the three languages, with no client data**, the repository being shared; the style is neutral, the project's own `config.typ`. Norms the library lacks are found online and cited with a `_source`. **The right pane offers a section's title and whether it is enabled, and nothing else** — the engineer will ask for more as he needs it — except the norms section, where the user selects the norms shown. What phase 1 built of it, because the migration and the creation need a document to exist: `calc/structure.py` (the file, the generated block, the tags' reader), `calc/doc_templates.py` and `templates/documents/` with a two-section skeleton in three languages. The rest was §14's and is built in v0.112 — with three reversals, the «Sections» tab, the overwriting of every copy of a shared block and the optional ψ columns, and the changes the first items of this section record.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **An RFEM structure is a domain instance, and its extremes are read rather than enveloped, settled (v0.100).** The engineer's own «Chaque Structure est un domaine», answered with the shape §3.3 already defines, so `#val`, `#find`, `#show-calc` and the summary tables read it with nothing added — and in a file of its own, `data/rfem.json`, as a connector's and a connection's are. What is read is RFEM's **result-combination** tables, which already carry the extreme *and* the combination that reached it: enveloping the 48 combinations here would be a second opinion on a number the solver settled, and it would cost 11.9 s where 4 tables cost 6. Chosen over a domain in `calculations.json` (whose `action` is a load's notion, permanent or variable, which a structure is not) and over dedicated commands with no domain (which would have put `find`, `val` and the summaries out of reach).
- *[retired in v0.117]* **The design situation is part of the variable's name, settled (v0.100).** `SP1_T001_1_M_y_max`. The engineer asked that one always state which situation or combination is being looked at; with one instance per structure — which he chose — a bar holds four maxima and only the name can tell them apart. Chosen over an instance per structure *and* situation, which would have made `#show-calc("rfem", "VU-Regelfall-Stuetze")` impossible to write, and over a `situation:` argument on `#val`, which is generic and must not learn about RFEM. ⚠️ Accepted price: the key is long, and it is a key rather than a symbol — the page still prints `M_(y,"max")`.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **A bar is one RFEM member and its index is always written, settled (v0.100).** The engineer's own choice of three (one member = one bar; contiguous collinear members of one position = one bar; the index written by hand in the comment). The index is written even where the position occurs once, which he did not ask for: `T001` alone would be shorter today and would silently become wrong the day a second member of that position joins the structure, and every call naming it would then name nothing.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **A quantity that is identically zero on a bar is left out, and said out loud, settled (v0.100).** A plane model carries no V_y, M_T, M_z, u_y, φ_x or φ_z at all — **4955 of 7696 vars**, and `data/rfem.json` fell from 2.4 MB to 1.05, a file Typst reads on every compile. Dropped per bar and **across every situation**, never in one and not another, which would make a name exist in SP1 and not in SP2; and `zero` names them, so «this structure carries no M_z» and «this export was read without M_z» stay two statements rather than one silence.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **The curve is reconstructed from the export and the sign of the relation is measured, settled (v0.100).** The engineer's answer (c) of three — reconstruct *and* use the stations. It is the one place the RFEM import does arithmetic, and it is bounded: it integrates values RFEM wrote down, between two of its own stations, and every endpoint is RFEM's own number to the digit. ⚠️ Which shear is the slope of which moment, and with which sign, is a **convention**, so both are tried over every segment of a table and the one that reproduces the exported moments wins; where neither does, nothing is curved. A diagram this module cannot verify is a diagram it does not touch. ⚠️ And the values *written* on the figure stay the stations' own, so no number beside a diagram was computed here.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **An imported model stays generated and its presentation is a file beside it, settled (v0.100).** The engineer's own answer B of three. The v0.98 rule stands — a model he did not draw has no business in the folder he draws in, and RFEM is the source — and what is his, the three size factors and which diagram is drawn for which loading, lives in `Woodcore-Datei/statics/views/<id>.json`. ⚠️ **In a subfolder**, because `statics.source_paths` globs `*.json` at the top of the folder and would read a view as a model: the `details/` trap of §3.3decies, met a second time and avoided the same way. ⚠️ A view **replaces** the drawing's three factors where an *entry* of a drawing states a factor **multiplying** them: there is one drawing and one engineer. ⚠️ A view whose model is gone is kept and reported, never deleted — an export renamed or a workbook not synced yet is not a reason to throw away a page of settings.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **The diagram is drawn for a combination and not for a design situation, settled (v0.100).** An envelope has no single curve but a **band**, a max and a min at every station, which is a different object and a different drawing. SP is where the *numbers* come from and CO where the *curve* comes from; the engineer's own rule — «toujours préciser quelle situation ou combinaison» — covers both, and the band is §14's.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **A third command beside the two the engineer named, settled (v0.100).** He asked for a table and a combination's formula, and for «(1) l'effort maximal et la combinaison qui l'a calculé» he was told `#val` would serve. It does not: `#val` prints a value and cannot print what produced it. `rfem-max` is that half, and it is a command rather than an argument on `#val`, which is generic and must not learn about RFEM.
- *[in force]* **A file another program holds is not a report that does not compile, settled (v0.100).** The whole project sits in NextCloud, whose sync client holds a file open while it uploads it, and Typst then fails on Windows' own error rather than on a diagnostic of its own (`os error 32`, ERROR_SHARING_VIOLATION). Reporting that as «the report does not compile» would be a **false statement about the engineer's work**, so such a failure is tried once more after a moment and, where it persists, said as what it is (`file_busy`, naming NextCloud). ⚠️ Nothing else is retried, and the line matters: «file not found» is a real defect of the report — a picture not put there yet, which is exactly what the engineer's own `Sections/005` has — and retrying it would only say it twice. A test holds all three paths, the third by asserting the process ran **once**. 👉 The same lock hits the suite: `tests/test_commands.py` was seen to fail once on `os error 32` over `examples/lib/formula.typ` and pass alone on the next run, so a compile failure naming a *file* rather than a Typst error is worth re-running before it is believed.
- *[in force]* **The bar says what is on screen and the banner says what went wrong, settled (v0.100).** A failed reading keeps the values of the last good one listed, so a bar repeating the failure would spend the one line that can say where those values come from; it carries the failure only when a **first** reading failed and there is nothing else to describe. And the banner carries **Typst's own message** under the reason, because Typst names the file and the line and nothing in the window could — «le rapport ne compile pas» alone leaves the engineer to find it himself.
- *[in force]* **Nothing in a reading is a wall-clock value, settled (v0.100).** The `Registry` carries its domains and its `stamp` and no duration and no timestamp; `save()` writes neither. A time in a record is a value that differs between two runs of an unchanged project, which is why `data/project.json` carries no date (§12) — this cache is outside the project and outside that promise, so the field would have been harmless, and it is left out anyway because a field nobody reads is a field that acquires a meaning nobody remembers (v0.70's `plates.thickness`). A test asserts the dataclass has exactly the two fields. 👉 The same reasoning caught a real one next door the same day: the scripts tab exported the `seconds` a run took, and two exports of an unchanged project therefore differed byte for byte.
- *[in force]* **`style:` on `var`, `var-unit` and `val` wraps the whole line, not the value, settled (v0.100).** The engineer asked for it in those words -- «ajoute à #var l'argument style comme pour #eq» -- and the one decision inside it is *what* gets set apart. `#eq` hands its `style:` to its own eval's **scope**, so it lands on the **answer** and the formula before it is untouched (v0.85); the three commands that print one value wrap **the whole of it** through `apply-style` -- `R_k = 7 kN` comes out bold, underlined or doubly ruled entire, symbol and unit included. That is what he asked for, and it is the honest reading of «comme pour #eq» for a command whose output *is* one line rather than a formula ending in an answer. ⚠️ **The two cannot be one mechanism**, and the reason is a decision already taken here: `#eq`'s tail is a math **source string** it builds itself, while the other three build a line of **content** whose `=` is held by no-break spaces (`~=~`) -- which is what keeps a symbol from being parted from its value at a line break (v0.87). Rebuilding that line as one source string to hand to an eval would put a relation exactly where the line may break, and leave `R_k =` at the end of one line with `7 kN` at the start of the next. ⚠️ **Its accepted price is written into the argument's own documentation**: a styled line is re-evaluated in math mode, so it is set with math's own spacing around its `=` and comes out a little wider than the same line without it -- two `#var` calls one above the other, one styled and one not, do not align on their `=`. ⚠️ **`var-line` and `verify` do not take it**, which is right rather than an omission: a row of declarations sets its items apart with its own separators and a relation already carries `verify`'s double rule as its verdict, so a `style:` there would be a second mark competing with the one that means something. 👉 The code, its `///` blocks, its examples and its French and German are another session's (v0.100, task A); this entry is the reference document's side of the same work, written by the session that read the surface rather than by the one that built it -- which is why it records what the surface *is* and the engineer's own words for why, and claims nothing about intent beyond them.
- *[in force]* **The PDF carries the report's own variables, settled (v0.110).** The engineer's choice among the three §14 put to him, and the one that costs nothing: **(a)** read on demand, which is 8-10 s the first time a report changes; **(b)** have the export write a JSON, which doubles every *Exporter PDF* from ~10 s to ~20 s; **(c)** have the PDF carry it, which is free because the PDF's own compile is the one that pays. He chose (c) knowing the price stated with it -- **every delivered PDF now embeds a JSON of the report's values**. ⚠️ That price is arguably a gain rather than a cost, and it is worth saying which: §1 makes everything computed in Typst **untraced**, and this is the one artefact that now travels with those numbers, so a PDF handed to a checking engineer carries what produced it. What it is *not* is a second source (§2 rule 1): nothing reads it back into `data/`, and the report goes on computing its own values at every compile. ⚠️ **Both routes give the same registry**, which is not a hope but the same `var-registry.final()` through the same `json.encode`, held by a test that compiles the example report and compares the attachment with a `typst eval` field for field. So `best()` chooses a price, never an answer -- which is what made the choice reversible: had he picked (a) or (b), `from_pdf` would simply not be called, and the tab would not change by a line. ⚠️ **Three facts were measured before it was written**, each of which would have sunk it: an attachment adds **no page**; it is **silently ignored for a PNG or SVG target**, so `build_examples.py`'s 306 renders and the website's SVGs are untouched; and it is deterministic, so an unchanged report still writes an unchanged PDF -- the same reason `data/project.json` carries no date (§12).
- *[in force]* **Python asks Typst what a state holds, by naming that state from outside, settled (v0.100).** The values the report computes live in the Typst state `var-registry` and in no file (§1), so a window that lists them has to ask a compiled report. `typst eval --in <report> 'state("var-registry", <default>).final()'` is the route, and it was chosen after the two alternatives were tried and refused. **Parsing the `.typ` sources** for `#var(` and `#eq(` calls reads what is written and not what is computed: an `#eq`'s answer is in no source, and a `#var` reading `var-of(...)` has its value, its unit and its symbol in the export rather than at the call. **Reimplementing the registry in Python** is a second unit algebra beside `lib/formula.typ`, which is the duplication §2 rule 1 exists to forbid, and it would drift the first time a formula's evaluation changed. ⚠️ What makes the route possible is a fact worth writing down: `typst eval --in` evaluates in the **standard library's** scope and not the document's, so the document's own `var-registry` binding is invisible (`unknown variable: var-registry`) — and a state is keyed by a **string**, so it can be named again from outside. `typst query`, the obvious older route, is deprecated in 0.15.1 in favour of `eval`. ⚠️ Accepted price, and it is the whole of the design's shape: a reading is a **full compile**, 8–10 s on the example project against 9.8 s for `typst compile` of the same report.
- *[in force]* **The tab reads once, not on every opening, settled (v0.100), against the engineer's stated preference.** He wrote «si nécessaire, ajouter un bouton "Importer Typst", ou, mieux encore, automatiquement importer depuis typst à l'ouverture de l'onglet», and the measurement contradicts the «mieux encore»: automatic on *every* opening is ten seconds of a hung tab per switch. So the intent is kept and the mechanism is not — it reads **automatically the first time the tab is opened**, in a thread with the tab usable, and then reads its cache until the report, the engine or the export has moved under it, with a banner saying so and *Relire* to read again. It is the argument §10quater already makes for the RFEM tab, whose `on_shown` reads nothing, and it is put to the engineer rather than settled behind him (§14). ⚠️ Chosen over two other ways of making the first opening instant, each of which is his call because each touches something outside the window: the **export** writing the JSON after its own compile, which doubles every *Exporter PDF* from ~10 s to ~20 s; and the **PDF carrying it** as an attachment (`pdf.attach` + `json.encode`, read back with the pypdf already pinned — verified end to end), which is free because the PDF's own compile is the one that pays, and which changes what he hands to a checking engineer.
- *[in force]* **The registry's default is written down twice and held equal by a test, settled (v0.100).** `variables.REGISTRY_DEFAULT` restates `lib/calc-engine.typ`'s own `state("var-registry", (stack: ("",), domains: (:)))`. It is a copy, and §2 rule 1 would rather it were not — what makes it unavoidable is that the caller is Python and the declaration is Typst, with no file between them. What makes it safe is that the cost of a mismatch is **not** a wrong default here but a **report that stops compiling**: a state named with another default hands that default to the document's own `update` functions, and the error surfaces inside `lib/formula.typ`, reading as a defect of the engine. `tests/test_variables.py` reads the declaration out of `calc-engine.typ` by balancing its parentheses and compares, and a planted mismatch fails it naming both — the rule §11 already applies to the files `lib/distance.typ` names.
- *[in force]* **The cache carries no level and no language, settled (v0.100).** Measured rather than assumed: level 1 and level 3 give **byte-identical** JSON, a level choosing what is printed and never what is stored, so one reading serves all three reports of §3.7. The language reaches **four labels of ninety** — the ones the glossary composes, `eta_w`'s «Feuchteklasse 2» — and no value and no unit, so a reading passes no `--input lang=` at all and shows the report's own language, the window's chrome following the window. That is the loads tab's rule for the labels the workbook wrote (§4bis, §10quater), and the price is stated: in a French window the four glossary labels read in the report's language, not the window's.
- *[in force]* **The PDF is not in the stamp, settled (v0.100).** What a reading is compared against is the count, the total size and the newest modification time of every `.typ` of the report and everything under the engine copy, `data/` included — because `var-of(...)` reads the export. The PDF is left out, and `out/`, `.probe/` and `archives/` with it: the PDF is written *by* the very compile a reading pays for, so counting it would make every reading stale the moment it was taken, and again after every *Exporter PDF* — which is the one moment an engineer would most expect the tab to be right. ⚠️ Accepted limit, written into the test that holds it: a max-mtime stamp cannot tell two writes inside one tick of the clock's resolution apart, so a file rewritten with the same bytes in the same tick is a change it can miss. The count and the total size are what make the ordinary cases certain.
- *[in force]* **The variables tab is a browser and has no `dirty`, settled (v0.100)** — the rule stated above, applied: it lists what a reading found and saves nothing, so it carries no `dirty` and owes the window no row marks, as the loads, RFEM and commands tabs do not. ⚠️ It does keep a `confirm_leave`, which every other tab has in order to *refuse* a close: here it only waits for a reading's thread and always answers True, that being the one hook the window offers a tab on its way out.
- *[in force]* **`#var` is offered for a named domain only, settled (v0.100).** `#var` reads its value from `domain:` or from a positional beside the key, so `#var("R_d")` alone can never work whatever is declared around it — `var-record` asserts a number. `build_reference.call_var_registry` therefore **refuses** a call with no domain rather than writing one, and a variable of the report's own default domain is offered `#val` alone, having nothing to be carried out of. Chosen over emitting it and letting Typst refuse it: §14 already records that the tab's copied calls may name variables a report declares first, which is the engineer's own decision, and that is a call that *can* be made to work — this one cannot.
- *[superseded in v0.117]* **The GitLab token lives in the environment, and each publication is asked for, settled (v0.99).** The engineer asked whether he could hand his personal access token over, so that a session could publish and push by itself, and two things came apart in the answer. **Pushing needs no token**: `git push` uses his own git credentials, and a session already pushes. **Publishing does**, and the token goes into `WOODCORE_GITLAB_TOKEN`, which `packaging/publish.py` already reads (`token()`), and **never into the conversation**: a session's transcript is written to disk and sent onward, and a token of scope `api` reads and writes the whole private project. ⚠️ `publish.py`'s third route, `getpass`, is reachable only on a tty, so a session's own shell is refused by name rather than left waiting for a prompt nobody can answer — which is what makes the environment the one route here. ⚠️ **The token is the means and not the permission.** Publishing is outward-facing and hard to take back: the installer becomes downloadable and, once `version.json` stands beside it, every installed Woodcore offers the update at its next start (§10quater). So each publication is **asked for** — « je publie 0.99.0 ? » — and the three steps of §12 then run in one go. Chosen over publishing of a session's own motion once a version is built and its suite green, which the token alone would allow: the engineer keeps the one sentence and loses the manipulations. ⚠️ Accepted price: a version the post-commit hook has already built waits for that sentence before it reaches anyone. ⚠️ The token's own scope and expiry are the engineer's to set, and **`write_package_registry` is worth trying before `api`**: the upload is a PUT on the generic-packages endpoint, so a narrower key may well serve, and it is a much smaller thing to leave lying about. ⚠️ **Since v0.117 the token is GitHub's** (the entry below): `WOODCORE_GITHUB_TOKEN`, a fine-grained token limited to the releases repository, *Contents: read and write*; `GITHUB_TOKEN` is not read, being in many shells a token for everything. The rest of this entry stands: the environment, never the conversation, and each publication asked for.
- *[in force]* **The three factors are written down once, and an entry's own is a factor multiplying them, settled (v0.99).** The engineer asked for a thickness of its own on every entry of a drawing. `calc/domains/figure_style.py` holds the three names, the range, the arithmetic and the table of which entry may state which (§3.3quaterdecies); the detail schemas gain the three the plans and the static models already had. Chosen over a fourth copy per module, which is how the plans' and the static models' came to be two lists that happened to agree, and over an entry stating a **size**: a schema is a detail at its real size in millimetres and a static model a scale-free sketch normalised to 1000 figure units, so one number would mean two things in the two, and neither would survive the drawing being redrawn at another extent. On a **plan** a size could have been stated — its coordinates are the sheet's, points or pixels — which is what made the choice worth taking rather than inheriting: it would be a length in the units of whichever background the plan happens to be drawn over, so the same number would mean one thing on a PDF at 1:50 and another on a photograph, and it would not survive the crop being redrawn, the symbols' unit being the crop's longer side over 850. A factor means the same in all three. ⚠️ Accepted price: two levels to read before knowing how large something is drawn, which is why the export records both; and an engineer who wants a number 3 mm high on paper says «×2» and reads the result rather than typing 3. ⚠️ A factor an entry is not drawn with is **refused naming the ones it may state** rather than silently ignored — a field nobody can see the effect of is a field that ages (v0.70's own argument). ⚠️ No `schema_version` moved: a field whose absence has a meaning is additive, the v0.69 rule.
- *[in force]* **A `style` key a drawing does not know is refused, settled (v0.99), tightening v0.34.** Until v0.99 `check_source` read `style.line`, `style.point` and `style.text` and ignored anything else the object held, the schema declaring no `additionalProperties`. With a factor of its own on every entry, a misspelt `lines` is a factor the engineer set and nothing draws — the `plates.thickness` argument of v0.70 — so `figure_style.check` refuses it, on the drawing's own three as on an entry's, naming what a factor may be called. Accepted price: a source carrying a key nobody reads no longer exports; none of the engineer's does, and the message says what to write. ⚠️ A factor that is not a number at all is refused by the **schema** first, which names its path (`plan-source.elements[0].style.line`) rather than the entry — two readers of one field, the earlier one winning, which is the arrangement `check_source` has always had.
- *[in force]* **`number_frame` applies the element's own factors itself, and is idempotent by construction, settled (v0.99).** A position number's box is asked for from three places with the **plan's** sizes — Woodcore's canvas hit-tests a click on it, `plan.warnings` asks whether a moved number falls out of the frame, and the export's `label_at` frames a detail round it — and from three more with the **element's**, `_number`, `_leader` and `_slab` asking while they draw it. Scaling in the callers would have meant six call sites to keep right; scaling inside `number_frame` alone would have had the drawing's own three scale twice. So `Sizes` carries the plan's own sizes (`Sizes.drawing`), `entry_sizes` starts from them whichever it is handed, and asking twice for one entry gives the same answer rather than the factor squared — the v0.74 shape of «the import graph is acyclic by construction», applied to a number. It was worth doing by construction rather than by care: the defect it forecloses is a box 1.69 times its size with a correctly sized number inside it, which no page says out loud, and planting it fails three tests of `tests/test_plan_style.py`.
- *[in force]* **A load's own `line` carries its arrow's length, settled (v0.99).** The one place a factor changes more than a weight, and said out loud in the schema, in the drawing and in a test: two loads of equal magnitude draw different arrows where one of them states a factor. It is an exception to «within a kind, equal magnitudes give equal arrows» (v0.51) and it is what the engineer asked for — that *one* load stand out among its neighbours. Chosen over scaling the stroke alone, which leaves the thing he wanted to stand out exactly as long as its neighbour, and over a second field for the length, which would be a length in a figure that has no lengths. What a load stating nothing gives is unchanged: the arrows are in proportion to their magnitudes, and the notes still name what was held up to the minimum.
- *[in force]* **The break a release opens at a node keeps the drawing's own factors, settled (v0.99).** The accepted price of the cut belonging to the **node** and not to one member end (`statics_svg._release_cuts`, v0.55): both ends meeting there are drawn back by the same amount, and a cut taken from each member's own `line` would open a break wider on one side of one node than on the other — the very thing v0.55 repaired. A member or a release drawn heavier therefore stands in a break sized for the drawing; what a heavier release does widen is its own symbol, whose extent the frame takes in and a halo covers. A spring's zigzag amplitude keeps the drawing's `point` for the same kind of reason, a member stating no `point` at all.
- *[in force]* **A halo grows with the part it marks, settled (v0.99).** A ring thinner than the symbol under it marks nothing, so a halo's nominal width stays in the drawing's own `point` unit and is carried by the factor that widened what it marks — `line` for a member, an arrow or a dimension line, `point` for a symbol on a node, which a support and a release may state themselves. A point takes the drawing's, drawing nothing of its own to mark.
- *[in force]* **A text of a schema is set over its own box, translucent, settled (v0.99).** The engineer, on a schema: the halo makes a cloud round every letter. It is `statics_svg.py`'s own repair of v0.77 made here, on the four kinds of text at once. **Translucent and not opaque** (`LABEL_GROUND_OPACITY`, 0.85, one constant): a schema's label sits *on* the piece it names — the middle of the longest stretch no other piece covers (v0.75) — so an opaque ground would take a bite out of that piece's own fill, which is the whole reason he asked for three answers rather than one. Chosen over moving the labels off their pieces, which would move every figure already drawn.
- *[in force]* **A line gives way to a text, and not the other way round, settled (v0.99).** A shown construction line is cut at the boxes of the labels it crosses, and a dimension line under its own value. It is the answer the ground behind the text cannot give: the aids are drawn **over** everything, the labels included (v0.77), so no ground under a text can mend a line drawn over it. Chosen over drawing the aids under the labels, which would undo v0.77 — a construction line is what the eye reads the detail against and must not disappear behind a number — and over leaving the line across the text, which is the defect. ⚠️ The canvas cuts them too, since it draws the lines itself: the window and the report would otherwise show one detail two ways.
- *[in force]* **«Reprendre» is one word in every tab, settled (v0.99).** «Zurücknehmen», which the connectors' and the schemas' tabs have used since v0.70 and v0.75 — §4bis's one word per notion, applied to the button three tabs now carry.
- *[in force]* **The summaries are three commands, without a level, settled (v0.99).** The engineer: «des tableaux récapitulatifs des charges (kN/m2), des charges linéaires (kN/m), puis finalement, des résultats principaux, le tout par domaine», and on the levels «un tableau et un tableau». **Three commands and not one with a `kind:`** is his choice: a summary of loads and a summary of results are two things a report prints in two places, and a call that says which by an argument reads as a command doing three jobs. ⚠️ **And so none takes a `level:`**, which is my decision and not his: a summary is the one place in this report whose whole purpose is to be complete, so there is no level at which it would show less — and an argument that changed nothing would be a lie in the signature, which an editor offers, which `build_reference` catalogues and which a reader tries. They are the only three display commands of this engine without a level; the accepted price is that exception, and a `#show-load-summary(level: 1)` written out of habit is refused by Typst naming the unknown argument. **To be contradicted if the engineer prefers a level that chooses the columns** — the result summary, for instance, could leave its *Bezeichnung* column at level 1.
- *[in force]* **A line written by hand enters a summary by a mark, and its unit says which, settled (v0.99).** The export carries no line load at all and the engineer answered «pour l'instant rien. Mais le user doit manuellement pouvoir ajouter des lignes eq ou var dans typst». `summary: true` on `#var` and `#eq` is the opt-in, and **the unit sorts it**: kN/m² to the area loads, kN/m to the line loads, any other refused naming the two. Chosen over **gathering by the unit alone**, with no mark: an intermediate of a calculation is often in kN/m² — a layer's load, a pressure on its way to a force — and a table that listed them would be a summary of the working. Chosen also over an argument that *names* the table (`summary: "line"`), which would say the same thing twice, the unit having said it already. ⚠️ Nothing is read from `data/statics.json`: a static model's line loads are a drawing, they compute nothing, and the engineer said «pour l'instant rien» (§14).
- *[in force]* **The unit is read where the variable ends up, not where it is declared, settled (v0.99).** The refusal is therefore raised where the table is printed. Chosen over a check at the call, for two reasons that are facts of the engine and not preferences: a marked `#eq` often states **no** `unit:` at all, its answer coming out in whatever unit the algebra gives its operands; and `#var-unit` may carry a variable out of its unit on the line after, so a check at the call would refuse a variable that is right by the time it is read. Accepted price: a `summary: true` in a report that prints no summary is never refused — nothing listed it.
- *[in force]* **The η are found by walking the registry, not by registering, settled (v0.99).** A `verify-*` publishes its η in a var-domain named `"<member>-verify-<check>"` (§3.3undecies), and that name **is** the contract: it is what `#val("eta", domain: "S001-verify-compression")` writes. The *Nachweisübersicht* therefore reads the domains that carry it — a name in `-verify-` **and** a numeric `eta`, both, each alone gathering either an engineer's var-domain that declares an `eta` or a check domain that declares none. It is the choice the bibliography made between a walk and a registration (§10bis), and for its reason: a ninth check added to SIA 265 4.2 appears there by itself, where a registration is a line somebody must remember to write — and `lib/timber.typ` did not have to change by one line. Accepted price: a check given a `name:` is listed only if that name keeps the shape (§14).
