- *[in force]* **A group is named by a bold row of its own, settled (v0.99).** `render-table`'s `group` names a run of **columns** (§3.3) and not of rows, so a domain's name goes through a body row whose first cell carries it, set bold by `bold-rows` — the argument `render-table` already takes for a row that speaks louder than the others, and the way a printed table names a block. **Every group gets its row**, even where it is alone: for the line loads that row is «Im Bericht berechnet», and what it says — that these numbers are the report's own and are traced nowhere (§1) — is the first thing a checker wants to know. Chosen over one table per domain (the engineer said «le tout par domaine», one grouped table) and over a *Domain* column repeated on every row.
- *[in force]* **A result is what the record calls a result, settled (v0.99).** What a calculation's results *are* is the data's to say (§3.3), so the summary asks `results` and decides nothing — which spares it judging which of a record's declared results is «really» the main one, a judgement the data has already made. One visible consequence: a polar moment shows its `I_p`, which its record calls its result, **and** the `F_max` the report published, which is the answer its reader came for (§3.3quinquies). And **a result whose value is a word is left out**: a sentence has no place in a column of values, which leaves SIA 261's category E out of the tables — its only «value» is the sentence 8.2.2 states in place of one, and the report refuses to print that category anyway (§3.3septies).
- *[in force]* **Two tables for the results, a `blocks:` to choose them, settled (v0.99).** A value with its unit and a utilisation against 1 with its ✔ share no set of columns, and a set carrying both would carry neither well — the mark would have nowhere to go. `blocks:` is `show-bibliography`'s argument, for its reason: a block with nothing in it is left out, and where everything asked for is empty, one line says so. It is not a `kind:` by the back door: the three commands stay three, and `blocks:` chooses what *one* of them prints.
- *[in force]* **`lib/summary.typ` imports `calc-engine.typ` and nothing else, settled (v0.99).** The chain is `calc-engine → summary → formula`, and **no import inside a command's body was needed** — unlike `lib/timber.typ`, which needs `var`, `eq` and `verify` (§3.3undecies) — because the mark is a pure function of the registry's dictionary rather than a state of its own: what it writes, `var` and `eq` write for it, in the update they already emit. `formula.typ` imports the three commands and `summary-mark` by name, as it imports `distance` (§10bis point 3), so `commands.typ` — a file of the *project* — did not change.
- *[in force]* **A citation stays what it is and the bibliography is a second list, settled (v0.99).** The engineer's own choice of two put to him: the right-hand column goes on printing «SIA 265 §4.2.1» exactly as it did, the norm is *registered* at the same time, and a command prints the list at the end. Chosen over Typst's `#cite`/`#bibliography` with a CSL style, which was the other half of the question: those set a citation as a key in the text and let a style decide its form, so every citation in this report — a clause with its §, a table with the report's own word, a document cited whole — would have had to be expressed as a CSL key and set by a style that knows nothing of §10's column. **The acceptance criterion he set is that no existing page moves**, and it is held two ways: a test compiles one sentence with three registrations in it and one without and compares the pages to the text and to the millimetre, and the 276 frozen example pages were rendered before and after the change, every one of them byte-identical. Accepted price: a citation is not a link, and a reader who wants the full title of a document cited on page 12 turns to the end of the report.
- *[in force]* **The catalogue is JSON in `calc/norms/`, and a project's own file is BibTeX, settled (v0.99).** The engine's thirteen documents are `calc/norms/norms.json`, with `calc/norms/norms.py` checking it before anything is taken out — the arrangement every one of the forty other files of that folder has (§11), and what makes «read off the edition's own title page» a rule with teeth rather than an intention. A project's own documents are a **`references.bib`**, which is the engineer's own word and the form he will have on hand for a manufacturer's approval. So the two are read by two readers, which is the accepted price: one in Python and Typst over a checked JSON, one in Typst alone over a BibTeX subset. Chosen over one format for both — BibTeX for the engine's own catalogue would have put a second parser between an export and a norm's name, with no schema to check it.
- *[in force]* **The BibTeX reader understands the format or refuses, naming the line, settled (v0.99).** `@type{key, field = {value}, field = "value", field = 1234}`, braces balanced and the inner ones dropped as BibTeX drops them; `%` and `@comment` passed over; `@string` and `@preamble` **refused**, since a string macro changes how every value after it is read and passing one over would misread the file; any other `@type` refused with the list; and a field the reader does not name — `author`, `url`, `isbn` — **ignored**, so a .bib written for something else can be used as it stands. That is the line: the reader understands the *syntax* completely or says where it stopped, and reads the *fields* it prints. Chosen over a lenient reader that skips what it cannot parse, which is how a bibliography comes to be missing an entry nobody notices. ⚠️ A value is a string and is printed exactly as the file writes it: no TeX is interpreted, so an en dash is typed as one and not as `--`, and nothing an engineer types into a title can change the page around it — `render-prose`'s own promise about an Excel description, one step further.
- *[in force]* **`title` is required; `edition` and `publisher` are printed where given, settled (v0.99).** The engineer's four fields are what an entry *carries*, and the engine's own thirteen carry all four — `norms.py` requires them, where the data is the engine's to get right. A project's document may genuinely have no publisher (a course handout), and refusing it there would send it to the third block, which says less than a line without a publisher. One line of `norms.py` tightens it if the engineer wants the four required everywhere.
- *[in force]* **The entry says its block, and a citation without an entry gets a third, settled (v0.99).** The engineer's rule was «tout ce qui est nommé par l'argument `norm` est une norme», and his own data contradicts it: bauforumstahl's Arbeitshilfe is a connection's `DEFAULT_NORM` and is cited with `norm:` (§3.3quinquies), and «Sherpa / ETA-12/0197» is written with `norm:` in `Sections/005`. So an entry carries `kind` — `norm` or `literature` — and what neither the catalogue nor the project's file names goes to **Weitere Dokumente / Autres documents / Other documents**, printed as it was cited. Chosen over putting every `norm:` under «Normen», which would file a steel handbook and a manufacturer's approval as norms, and over refusing a citation with no entry, which would stop a report over a document the engineer has not written down yet.
- *[in force]* **The data is walked, and what the report writes by hand registers at the call, settled (v0.99).** Two mechanisms rather than one, and each for what it is good at. The **walk** covers the five `show-*` commands that print a citation out of `data/` — `show-calc`, `show-input`, `show-polar-moment`, `show-connector-resistance`, `show-live-load` — with **no edit to any of them**, which is what kept the change to `lib/calc-engine.typ` at nothing. The **registration** covers what is in no file: a `norm:` written at a call. Accepted price, stated: the walk does not know which calculations the report printed, so a norm cited only by a calculation the report leaves out is listed all the same. That is narrower than it sounds — every project's data cites SIA 261 and SIA 265, which every report prints — and `show-bibliography(data: false)` is the exact answer for a report that wants only what it registered. The other way round (a registration at each `show-*`) is five more edits to `calc-engine.typ` and is what to do if over-listing ever bites.
- *[in force]* **A registration is emitted before its command's context, settled (v0.99).** `bib-note` returns content — a `state.update` — and every caller emits it outside every context and outside anything that is `measure`d: `var`, `eq` and `var-unit` beside the registry update they already emit, and `val`, `var-line` and `distance` as `bib-note(norm) + context { … }`, which is the one change their signature line needed and which keeps their bodies untouched. Registering from inside `norm-text` — the one function that renders a citation, and the tempting place — was refused twice over: a state written inside a context is not read back by the folded chain an update function reads (§3.3quinquies's panne of v0.85), and `norm-text`'s result is *measured* by `row-cell` and `chain-rows`, so an update inside it would be evaluated during measurement, where nothing it writes belongs.
- *[in force]* **A norm follows the record, not the level, settled (v0.99).** A `norm:` written at a call is a statement that the line rests on that document; whether the clause is printed is a matter of level. So a `#var(..., norm: "SIA 265", level: 0)` registers SIA 265 — the value it declares is printed by the formula under it — and the client, engineer and checking reports of §3.7 cite the same documents for the same numbers. Chosen over registering only what prints its citation, which would have made the bibliography depend on the level.
- *[in force]* **The block names are bold lines and not headings, settled (v0.99).** §4bis settles that the headings of `Sections/` are the engineer's own words, written by hand, so a command that emitted `= Normen` would put a glossary word in the outline. `heading: <int>` sets them as headings for an engineer who wants them there. The three names are `calc/i18n/strings.json`'s, in three languages, as every other word the engine prints.
- *[in force]* **A block is sorted case-insensitively, and a norm comes before its supplement, settled (v0.99).** Codepoint order gets two things wrong that a reader notices at once: `/` (0x2F) sorts before `:` (0x3A), which put «SIA 261/1:2020» above «SIA 261:2020» and «SIA 265/1:2018» above «SIA 265:2021»; and a lowercase initial sorts after every capital, which put «bauforumstahl» after «STE Compact 02». The sort key is therefore the designation lowered, with the colon that opens the edition mapped below every printable character, so that what two designations share ends at the colon and the shorter one wins. Two lines, and they are what makes the list read as a bibliography rather than as a dump.
- *[in force]* **The edition is left off the line where the designation already ends on it, settled (v0.99).** «SIA 261:2020 — Einwirkungen auf Tragwerke. Schweizerischer Ingenieur- und Architektenverein, 2020.» says the year twice; the VKF directives, whose designation is a number and whose edition is a date, keep theirs. One line of code, and it is what makes the four fields read as a bibliography rather than as a record dump.
- *[in force]* **A title is the document's own, in the languages the document prints it in, settled (v0.99).** An SIA title page prints its title in German, French, Italian and English, so an SIA entry carries three titles and three publishers and a report prints the one it is written in. The VKF directives and STE Compact 02 exist in the library in French only, so they carry one title, printed as it stands in a German report too — §4bis's rule that what no norm gives in a language is left as it is. ⚠️ SZS C5's pages carry no English, so an English report prints its German title.
- *[superseded in v0.118]* **`SN EN 14592+A1:2012` is named from its successor, and says so, settled (v0.99).** *Superseded by «A Stabdübel's f_u,k is its steel grade's of SIA 263 Tabelle 1» (12.10.2026): the file now reads SN EN 14592:2022 itself.* The edition the engine's f_u,k is credited to is not in the library (§11, `sn-en-14592-2012-fu-k-stabduebel.json` already says so of its clause), so its designation and its three titles are read off the title page of `EN-14592-2022-DE.pdf`, which states «Ersetzt SN EN 14592+A1:2012» and prints all three. - *[in force]* **bauforumstahl's Arbeitshilfe is named from its own metadata, and says so, settled (v0.99).** Its printed pages cannot be read as text — the fonts carry a scrambled mapping, which is why §11bis counts it among the scans — so the title is the one `connection.DEFAULT_NORM` already cites, confirmed word by word in the garbled page, and the designation, the publisher and the year are the PDF's own `/Title` («Arbeitshilfe 02-9»), `/Author` («bauforumstahl e.V., Duesseldorf») and `/CreationDate` (2013-03-09). ⚠️ The year is therefore the file's and not a printed edition statement, which the entry's `_source` says.
- *[in force]* **The bibliography never names the library's file, settled (v0.99).** The engineer refused it explicitly. Every entry carries a `_source` naming the edition its fields were read off — which is what makes §2 rule 13 checkable per entry, and which `norms.py` requires — and `lib/bibliography.typ` does not read that field at all, which a test holds by refusing the word in the Typst source.
- *[in force]* **The project's file is named as a path from the report's folder, settled (v0.99).** `project: "references.bib"` is read as `read("/references.bib")`, an absolute Typst path resolved from the root — the arrangement `lib/calc-engine.typ` used for `json("/data/...")` before v0.23. A relative path would have resolved against `lib/bibliography.typ`, where the call is not written, which is surprising in exactly the way a path should not be. And the default is `none`: `read()` on a file that is not there is a report that does not compile, and Typst has no way to ask whether a file exists, so a project that has written no bib must be able to say nothing rather than ship an empty file it does not know about.
- *[in force]* **The two commands are «Le rapport et sa langue», settled (v0.99).** A bibliography is a part of the document, like its language and its metadata, and not a calculation of the export — so `show-bibliography` and `cite-norm` are in `build_reference.CATEGORIES`' `document`, beside `woodcore-document` and `set-report-language`, rather than in `display`, whose name is «Afficher un calcul de l'export». A category of their own is one line if the engineer wants the reference to list them apart.
- *[in force]* **`cite-norm`'s copied call is written out, settled (v0.99).** Its one argument is the document it cites, so the call `build_reference` would derive — `#cite-norm()` — is a call that refuses, and there is no default a citation could have. It joins `WRITTEN_CALLS`, where a binding whose call cannot be derived from its signature already lives (§10ter).

- *[in force]* **The report's revision journal is one file, written by hand, settled (v0.99).** The engineer asked for a module that records what was changed in the report; his own answers settled the rest. **One `journal.json` and not one file per entry**, against the arrangement of every other source here (§3.3quinquies, §3.3decies): a journal is read as a whole and in order, nothing addresses one revision by an id, and a folder of files would have brought a `group`, an id and a file name to keep in step for no reader. **Written by hand** (his decision 6.2), against an entry appended when a source is saved: an afternoon in the schemas tab would leave twenty entries, and «what I changed in the report» is not «which files I touched». **One field beyond his six, an index** (6.3), against a scope and a Woodcore version: a revision block on paper carries a number, and the number is also what the journal is ordered by. **The short form is typed at every entry** (6.4), against a setting in *Paramètres*: a journal may carry entries by several offices; Woodcore prefills it from the entry before it, which is a convenience and not a setting. Accepted price: nothing here notices when two entries spell one office two ways.
- *[in force]* **A journal is ordered by its index and never by its date, settled (v0.99).** The date is typed, so it may be wrong or two entries may share one; the index is unique by construction. A journal that reordered itself around a typo would print a revision under the wrong one, and what reads the order — `show-journal`, which never sorts, and `journal.latest`, which the PDF's keywords name — would follow it. `validate_journal_business` holds the order rather than trusting it. Accepted price: an entry typed with the wrong index is in the wrong place until its index is corrected, which the list shows at a glance.
- *[in force]* **A gap in the numbers is never refilled, settled (v0.99).** `next_index` is the largest plus one, `positions_workbook.propose_id`'s own rule: Rev. 2 deleted out of 1, 2, 3 leaves 2 unused. The newest deleted gives its number back, which is right for one typed by mistake and taken out at once. Chosen over a high-water mark kept in the file, which is one more field to carry for a case nobody meets.
- *[in force]* **The levels govern the three texts and nothing else, settled (v0.99).** The engineer's 6.5, «au level 3, tout afficher», read with 5.6: a table is a table. So the number, the day, the name and the short form are printed at every level and only the label, the short description and the long one come and go. Chosen over hiding columns that identify a revision at level 1, which would leave a table whose rows could not be told apart.
- *[in force]* **The engineer's own words go into the table as content, settled (v0.99).** His 6.9: the label and the two descriptions are never translated. The glossary's own rule — translate a string if the glossary carries it — is right for everything the export carries and is not safe for free text: `match-template` matches on the literal segments around a template's holes, so «Element 3 ergänzt» matches «Element {n}» and a French report prints «élément 3 ergänzt» (§14's `tl-of` risk). `table-cell` therefore passes **content** through untouched — one additive branch, as a column's own `label` already allowed — and `show-journal` hands its three texts over that way. Chosen over trusting the glossary not to collide, which is a promise nobody can keep about text somebody will type next year, and over fixing `tl-of` itself, which is §14's and reaches every reader of the glossary. Accepted price: one more branch in a function three sessions have touched this month, and content cells that no `value` column may use.
- *[in force]* **A narrow prose column is hyphenated, in the journal's table alone, settled (v0.99).** `render-table` sets its cells ragged (v0.87), and Typst hyphenates only what it justifies, so a German compound in a three-centimetre column runs past its edge. `show-journal` sets `hyphenate: true` around its own table and nothing else does, so no other table of the report moves. Chosen over justifying the cells, which v0.87 removed for a reason, and over setting the journal smaller than the text around it, which `render-table` deliberately does not do.
- *[in force]* **The latest revision reaches the PDF through `data/project.json`, settled (v0.99).** The engineer's 6.8. `woodcore-document` is a `set document` rule at the top of a document and cannot ask whether a second file is there, so reading `data/journal.json` from it would stop every report of a project whose engine was updated without being exported again. The export puts the newest entry into the project's record instead — optional, so a record written before v0.99 compiles with a default — and `project.record` is *given* it rather than reading it, so `project.py` knows nothing of the journal. The keyword is «Rev. 3 (2026-09-25)», left in that one form in every language: a keyword is a search term in a file's properties, not a word on the page, and the rule runs before the report's language is set.
- *[in force]* **The journal's page is the last section of the template, settled (v0.99).** A revision journal is often read at the front of a report; putting it there would renumber six sections of every template and of the two tests that hold a section's number. It is `008-journal.typ`, after the literature, and the sections editor of *Paramètres* moves it forward in one click (v0.94). Accepted price: a reader looking for the revisions finds them at the end until the engineer moves the section.
- *[in force]* **The journal's list is flat, settled (v0.99).** No `grouped_list`, no folder, no `identity.py`: a journal is chronological and an entry is named by its number. Accepted price: a project with fifty revisions scrolls, which the search box answers. ⚠️ It is a `QTreeWidget` with no root decoration — positions_tab's own list, the model this tab was written from — and not a `QTableWidget`: what the window reads off a list is the dot of unsaved changes, an icon in column 0, and `PositionList.rows()`'s shape, which `tests/test_window.py` already reads for the other tabs. A second list widget to keep in step would buy nothing.
- *[in force]* **A date is checked and never inferred, settled (v0.99).** `journal.parse_date` refuses «27.09.2026» and «2026-02-30» alike: the shape is ISO and the day must exist. Chosen over accepting any text, which would put a date the report cannot format on the page, and over a date picker in the tab, which would take the day from the clock — the one thing the engineer's own field is not (a revision is dated by when it was decided).
- *[in force]* **What the schema already says is not restated in Python, settled (v0.99).** `journal.check_source` validates against the contract first and then checks only what a JSON schema cannot express — an index that is taken, a day that does not exist, a name that is blank. The «missing required field» and «expected integer» messages are the walker's, naming the entry by its position; a second loop saying the same thing one line later was written and removed.

- *[in force]* **A table's head is measured to its ink, settled (v0.97), replacing v0.32's push on the first formula.** The engineer: «il n'y a pas assez d'espace dans le moment polaire entre F_i^* et le tableau». The cause is one fact about Typst and it had been patched on one side only: a built-up formula reaches well outside the line box its text edges give it — 5 pt above and 5 pt below, measured at 11 pt on the superposition's fractions — and that box is what the grid row and every gap around it are measured against, so the 1 em between two formulas left 1 pt of white and the 0.4 em under the last left 3 pt before the table's own rule. v0.32 had met the same fact at the top and answered it with a push, moving the *first* formula down by how much taller than an ordinary line it was: the one side a push can reach, and measured against the line box rather than the ink, so it over-corrected there and said nothing about the other three gaps. Setting the head to its ink (`top-edge`/`bottom-edge: "bounds"`, the instrument `row-cell` has used for a wrapped row since v0.87) answers all four at once and retires the patch. ⚠️ **Chosen over adding space under the last formula alone**, which is what the engineer named: that is the same patch on a third side, and it would have left the formulas themselves 1 pt apart on the very page he was reading. ⚠️ **The title is inside the block**, so the 0.45 em under it is real white: the title's ink hangs about 3 pt below its own line box, which is what had made v0.32's push necessary in the first place. ⚠️ **Accepted price**: the two kinds of table that carry a `formula` move — the polar moment's superposition head grows by about 20 pt and each of the wind load's two by about 10 pt, so §004's pages reflow — and their frozen example pages were re-rendered. A table without a formula, which is every other table in the report, is untouched.

- *[in force]* **The release is numbered after its newest version label, settled (v0.96).** The engineer: «la prochaine version doit être la version la plus à jour (v0.97.0 ?)». v0.91 to v0.96 stood on `project.VERSION` 0.90.0, each a label of the work and none a number of its own; the installer that carries them is **0.96.0**, the highest label, so that the number an installed Woodcore reports names the newest work it holds. Chosen over 0.97.0, a number no version of the work carries, and over one number per label, which would have meant releasing five installers nobody asked for. From now on a version label and the release that carries it are the same number wherever a release follows the work.
- *[in force]* **«Katalog» becomes «Woodcore», settled (v0.96).** The engineer: «je souhaite renommer le katalog en un mot qui est plus commun au fr, all et de (Katalog et plutôt allemand). Fais-moi des propositions (simplement "woodcore-gui" ?)». Woodcore is the program's own name, already in the window's title since v0.83, and reads the same in the three languages; «Woodcore's window» where the window is set against the command line or the export. Chosen over a second name for the window beside the program's, which is one more word to learn. The texts, the code files and the class were renamed together (`woodcore_gui.py`, `MainWindow`), the history left as it was written: CHANGELOG, the audits, this file's status lines.
- *[in force]* **What files already written say is read and migrated by use, settled (v0.96).** A `.woodcore`'s `katalog` is read where `gui` is absent and replaced at the first change of folders; the archive's route `katalog` is recorded as `gui`; the settings folder `typst-katalog` is still taken over. Chosen over a pass that rewrites every project, which would write into the engineer's projects on no action of his. The key is `gui` — not `woodcore`, which would repeat the file's own name, and not `window`. Accepted price: an older Woodcore (≤0.90) opening a project v0.96 has migrated shows no empty folders, reading `katalog` alone; nothing else is lost (§14).
- *[in force]* **The old file name is a shim, settled (v0.96).** `calc/tools/catalog_gui.py` runs `woodcore_gui.py` as the program (`runpy.run_path(..., run_name="__main__")`), so the launchers installed with 0.83 to 0.90, which start the engineer's sources by that path, keep working. Chosen over no shim, which would have made «Ouvrir les fichiers .woodcore avec ces sources» fail for every launcher already installed, and over an import-and-call shim, which would not have behaved exactly as `python woodcore_gui.py` does. Nothing imports it, which a test holds.
- *[in force]* **An address the site moves keeps working, settled (v0.96).** The tutorial's step on the window is `tutorial/window/`, a noun as the other steps' slugs are, and `website/site.json`'s `moved` writes a redirect at `tutorial/katalog/`. Chosen over keeping the old slug, a German word in every language's address, and over no redirect, which breaks a link somebody kept. The next move is one line.
- *[in force]* **The source of a value typed on a connector is `woodcore:`, settled (v0.96).** `katalog:connectors/<id>.json` until v0.96. Nothing reads the prefix back, so the change is one of words; the first export after it rewrites those strings in `data/connectors.json`, and the frozen data keeps the old ones until frozen again.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **What Woodcore reads of RFEM is its table export, not its API, settled (v0.98).** *Fichier → Exporter → Tableaux* writes an `.xlsx` (or a folder of `.csv`) and that file is what `calc/rfem_import/` reads (§3.3duodecies). The API was tried first and reads the same model in about a second over gRPC — and **it requires a Dlubal API key**, which not every office has and which a report's reproducibility must not depend on. So the file is the route, and the API can only ever be an accelerator that writes that same file (§14). Accepted price: the engineer exports by hand, which the dialog's saveable template makes two clicks, and an export goes stale without saying so (§14).
- *[retired in v0.117]* **The metadata's sigil is `@`, one sigil for every key, settled (v0.98).** The engineer says what an RFEM object *is* in RFEM's own comment field, `@position T001` (§3.3duodecies). ⚠️ **`#` was the first design and is impossible**: RFEM reads a `#word` in a comment as one of its own tags and the word is **gone** from the exported comment — a convention built on it would lose his work silently, which this project treats as the worst kind of failure. It is recorded here so it is not proposed again on the grounds that `#` reads better. One sigil rather than one per kind (`@position` a reference, `&model` a name) was chosen over the sigil carrying the meaning: with four sigils a line written with the wrong one is not a line at all, and the keys already say what they are (`REFERENCES`). A line written with another sigil is matched and **reported** rather than read as prose, which is what makes the single sigil safe.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **A `@position` is only on a member or a surface, settled (v0.98).** The engineer's own rule (`MEMBER_OR_SURFACE_ONLY`), enforced in `validation.py`, which knows an object's kind, and not in the parser, which does not. A node, a support or a load carrying one is a warning naming the object rather than a silent reading: a position is realised by a thing that has a section, and a support is not one.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **Nothing the RFEM reader finds refuses, settled (v0.98).** Every finding is an `engine.Note` the window writes in its own language (§4bis, the v0.79 rule). The export is the engineer's file, read and not validated by him: a comment he has not finished typing, a position he has not added to the workbook yet, a section he is about to change must not stop the report from building. That is the same line §3.6 draws for every other editor — a defect in a file the pipeline **writes** blocks, a gap in a file it **reads** warns.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **An imported static model goes to `data/statics.json` and never to `Woodcore-Datei/statics/`, settled (v0.98).** The engineer's decision, and the one the whole controller is arranged around (§3.3duodecies). `Woodcore-Datei/` is what he draws; a file written there by an importer would be a drawing he did not draw, open in a tab that would write it back and lose whatever RFEM said next — the copy-that-goes-stale §2 rule 1 exists to prevent. So the records are handed to `statics.prepare(generated=…)`, checked by the same `check_source` as a drawn one, and **regenerated at every export**. Accepted price: an imported model cannot be adjusted in Woodcore at all — the adjustment is made in RFEM, which is where the model is. A drawn model and an imported one of one id is refused naming both, rather than one quietly overwriting the other, which is what keying `data/statics.json` by id did.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **Only a named structure is imported, settled (v0.98).** A structure with no `@model` and no `@label` has no id to be addressed by, so `models_of(..., named_only=True)` lists it and imports nothing. Chosen over naming it after its lowest member number, which would put `24` in the report's calls and change the moment a member was renumbered in RFEM. The window and `--list` say how many are unnamed, so the engineer sees what he has not named yet.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **The member forces are read behind a flag, settled (v0.98).** Measured: the model alone is 1.1 s out of his workbook and its result sheets are **105 s**. So an export reads the model at every import and the forces when asked (`--results`, `prepare(read_results=True)`). Chosen over reading them always, which would make every *Import données* two minutes longer for numbers nothing in the report reads yet (§14), and over never, which would leave the reader written and unexercised.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **XLSX stays the format, on a measurement, settled (v0.98).** The engineer asked whether CSV would be faster. It is — 0.24 s against 0.92 s end to end — and the difference is not where it looks: opening the 490-sheet workbook is 1.48 s of which reading the 19 sheets Woodcore needs is 0.07 s, those 19 being 36 kB of 13.3 MB. One file to move, to name in the project and to hash is worth 0.7 s, and **the lever is which tables are read, not which format they are in**. A folder of `.csv` is read all the same, by the same reader, for an office that prefers it.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **The sheet and column aliases are measured, never guessed, settled (v0.98).** The engineer exported the same model in French, German and English so that the three could be read against each other, and six aliases written from memory were wrong. They are in `tests/fixtures/rfem_model.py`, which builds the fixture in all three languages, and `tests/test_model_tables.py` asserts the three read to the same model. What RFEM itself translates — a material's name, a load's type and distribution, a design situation, a load case's own abbreviation — is carried opaquely rather than mapped: nothing computes on it.
- *[in force]* **A position's section is read once, settled (v0.94).** `calc/domains/section.py` reads a dimension for its section type at the export and writes the record's `section`; `#member`, a schema element and the window read that and never the text again (§7). Chosen over a reader in each consumer, which is what v0.75's `schema.parse_section` beside v0.88's own reading in `lib/timber.typ` had become — two readers of one text, free to disagree. Accepted price: a record exported before v0.94 carries no `section`, and `#member` asks for an export again.
- *[partly superseded in v0.118]* **The steel families are codes and names only, settled (v0.94).** *The day came with issues #50–#52 (12.10.2026): the profile tables are transcribed (see «SZS C5's profile tables are transcribed from the book…»); a position's section still carries its designation and no property.* The engineer: «voir tous les profils de SZS-C5» — the 24 families of SZS C5:2018's designations (p. 154), and no profile table: a steel section states its designation and carries its numbers, and nothing computes a steel member. Transcribing C5's tables is for the day a steel check is asked for (§14).
- *[in force]* **`show-position` is removed with no alias, settled (v0.94).** The engineer: «est-il possible de remplacer show-positions par member ? Avec un argument de member, il serait possible d'afficher exactement la même chose». `#member(id, level: 1)` prints the very line, `description:` and `axes:` included, and a command a report no longer needs is one name fewer in every section's scope; an old report stops on «unknown variable: show-position», which the «Nouveautés» page says how to rewrite, as every rename here since v0.32 has done.
- *[in force]* **Round timber is computed, without lateral torsional buckling, settled (v0.94).** A circle's section values, its shear 4/3·V/A from mechanics and footnoted as such, and no Kippen: a round section is as stiff about either axis, so the page says the check does not apply and (37) takes k_m = 1.
- *[in force]* **η_w stays on the stiffness, settled (v0.94).** The engineer first wrote that the E-modulus must not be reduced by η_w — «c'est la résistance qui est diminuée, jamais la rigidité»; SIA 265 3.2.1.3 converts «die Tragwiderstände und die Steifigkeitswerte» of class 1 and Tabelle 4 gives a factor for each, which was put to him with the page, and he chose to change nothing: E_0,05 times η_w for the stiffness stands (§3.3undecies, v0.88).
- *[superseded in v0.114]* **Woodcore writes Positionen.xlsx, settled (v0.94), reversing v0.34.** v0.34 had Woodcore copy the rows a plan lacked, for pasting, and never write the engineer's workbook; the engineer asked that a position be added from the window. `calc/positions_workbook.py` writes it with openpyxl and puts back afterwards the one part openpyxl drops, the x14 extension carrying his drop-down lists — chosen over editing the sheet's XML by hand, which would have been a second writer of the xlsx format to keep right where openpyxl already writes everything else. Everything is kept or the write is refused by name, atomically, and never while Excel holds the file. Accepted price: the shared strings are written as the cells' own text, which Excel reads the same.
- *[in force]* **The Positions tab re-imports after every write, settled (v0.94).** A row written and not exported would leave `data/` and every tab on the old row; the window's own `reexport`, *Importer Excel*'s, runs after each write, and a failed export leaves the row written and says why. Accepted price: an export per write, some seconds on the engineer's project.
- *[in force]* **Templates in three places, settled (v0.94).** The engineer's answers: the built-in «Standard», the user's own under `%APPDATA%`, an office's shared folder; a template a skeleton folder and a `template.json`; edited in a form, a project saved as one, its sections edited in place. A template is named `<place>:<folder>`. The languages of `template.json` are the source and are written into the files a project starts with, which would otherwise be a second place for them.
- *[in force]* **Typst is checked at every start, from the sources, and pinned in the same step, settled (v0.94).** The engineer's answers: a banner and the pinned version updated, winget, and no check in Woodcore.exe, which ships its own Typst. A modal only where Typst is missing or not the pin — the report was verified with the pin — and a banner for a newer one, which is an offer. Installing and pinning are one action, the dependencies' own rule (v0.59): installing without pinning recreates the mismatch from the other side.
- *[in force]* **The commands tab reads the website's data, settled (v0.94).** One file of words and categories (`calc/i18n/reference.json`), one order (`build_reference.CATEGORIES`), one decision on an example's levels (`command_docs.example_levels`), read by the tab, `build_examples.py` and the site — the engineer's rule that a command's description and examples are not written twice. The examples are shown open, as on the site, reversing v0.64's arrows; French and English pages are compiled on demand and cached, not committed, the committed pages staying German (v0.86). Accepted price: the first look at a command in French or English compiles its examples in the background, about a second each.
- *[in force]* **The website's sidebar follows the kind of page, settled (v0.94).** A command page the commands, a tutorial page its steps and the reference, a page of changes the versions, the gallery none — the engineer's own lists.
- *[in force]* **The static models' and the schemas' bars are icon-only and as tall as the plan's, settled (v0.94).** The engineer's words: the icons understood without a name, a tooltip after 0.5 s with the name and the use, the plan tab's height, and «séparateurs fins, sans titres». The height is measured from the plan's own tool (`flowbar.tool_height`) rather than written down, so that a font changes both alike; the tooltip is timed on these tools only, Qt's application-wide delay left alone. Accepted price: the members' icons are drawn for the bar (`MEMBER_ICONS`) rather than taken from the figure's swatch, a small departure from «the bar shows what it draws», the swatch being a hair at 20 px.
- *[in force]* **At every commit, the «Nouveautés» and the commands' documentation follow, settled (v0.94).** The engineer: «lors d'un commit, il faut toujours log les changements sur le site internet, modifier la documentation des commandes si besoin, build le .exe». The rule is §15's: the installer is built by the post-commit hook when `VERSION` changes (§12), and publishing stays the engineer's to ask for.
- *[in force]* **A card is a `QGroupBox` the style sheet dresses, settled (v0.93).** `cards.card(title)` sets a property on a box rather than building a frame and a title label: a tab's code, its `form_layout(box)`, and every test that finds a box by its `title()` go on working, and a box made a card is one call. Chosen over a `QFrame` with a label, which would have meant rewriting what reads a box's title, and over restyling every `QGroupBox` of the window at once, which would have reached the tool bars and the connectors' lit boxes other sessions are rebuilding. The title is semibold through the box's own font — a style sheet's font on `::title` is not honoured — and does not reach the fields, a style sheet's font not propagating to children.
- *[in force]* **A column of cards is never narrower than its cards, settled (v0.93).** `cards.Column` asks for its body's width as its minimum; the splitter then never squeezes it below its cards, whatever the language or the font. Chosen over a fixed minimum width, the forms' arrangement until then — 320 px for the polar moments' form, which was narrower than its own contents and so scrolled sideways and cut its buttons — and over clipping silently. Accepted price: the form's pane can no longer be dragged narrower than its widest line (a radio button's text, which cannot wrap), so a form's choices are laid out one under the other, the rotation centre's first.
- *[in force]* **Cards read side by side reflow, settled (v0.95).** A connector's «Pièce 1» and «Pièce 2» are side by side where the column has the room and one under the other where it has not (`cards.Reflow`), as the site's grid sets its cards on a narrow screen. Chosen over keeping them side by side and letting the column scroll sideways — which a column of cards does not (v0.93), and which is what hid the second piece's fields at a window of 1440 px — and over always stacking them, which gives up the classeur's side-by-side reading where there is room for it (v0.70). The turn is made on a resize, at the two cards' minima.
- *[in force]* **A card's labels go above their fields where they must, settled (v0.95).** `cards.form()` is a `QFormLayout` with `WrapLongRows`: a long label beside a slider set the plan's properties pane as wide as the two together. Chosen over narrower wording, which would have been three languages rewritten for one layout.
- *[in force]* **A pane's room is measured in the window's own fonts, settled (v0.95).** `test_panes`' slack is taken in a process told where Windows' fonts are: in cards a pane is as wide as its words, and the off-screen default font doubles them. Chosen over lowering the threshold, which would weaken the guard for the defect it was written for, and over narrowing the plan's cards until the doubled font fitted, which would have shaped the window after a font nobody sees.
- *[in force]* **A light card on a soft ground, a lighter card on a darker ground, settled (v0.93).** The column is `bg-soft` in both schemes; a card is `card` — the site's white in the light scheme, `#1d1d27` in the dark, the one token added to theme.json. A raised surface is lighter in a dark scheme, and `bg`, the dark window's own, would have set the cards darker than their ground.
- *[in force]* **The calls are coloured labels, not line edits, settled (v0.92).** A `QLineEdit` cannot colour its text, and the site sets a call as a coloured code block; a `QLabel` in rich text can, selectable by the mouse, with the call in its tooltip and `text()` giving back the call. Chosen over a `QTextEdit` of one line, which scrolls and takes the focus for nothing, and over leaving the calls plain. Accepted price: a call wider than its pane is clipped rather than scrolled, the whole of it being in the tooltip and on the clipboard.
- *[in force]* **A symbol is painted as mathematics by a delegate, and the item keeps its source, settled (v0.92).** The loads tree's *Symbole* column shows q with its subscript and the item's text stays `q_(k,"max","b·h")`: the search, the copy and the tests read the source, and only the painting changed. Chosen over putting HTML into the items, which a search would then have to strip, and over a column of its own.

It lives beside theme.py for the same reason theme.json does: the program ships `calc/` whole and the site's build already imports from there.

The navigation is drawn over a QTabWidget whose bar is hidden rather than replacing it with a stack: every module, test and call that brings a tab forward, reads its title or sets its mark works as it did, and the two are kept in step both ways. Its groups are the site's arrangement and its words the norm's where there is one — *Actions* for the loads and their combinations is SIA 260's, the report's Einwirkungen. Accepted price: the navigation takes about 224 px of the window, so the widest editor tabs are that much narrower until it is folded; the push buttons' side padding went from 12 to 10 px so that the plan tab's panes keep more than 400 px of play (`tests/test_panes.py`).
- *[in force]* **The start page's actions are cards and its recent projects too, settled (v0.91).** As the site sets its features: an icon on a soft ground, a title and one line saying what it does. The recent list keeps its QTreeWidget and its five columns, one painted over the row, four hidden: what a test and the context menu read of a row is what it was.
- *[in force]* **Woodcore is free software, GPL-3.0-or-later, its source published in Woodcore-Publish, settled (v0.118).** The engineer, 14.10.2026, distributing the code for free: «License: GPL», version 3 or any later version chosen among GPL-3.0-or-later, GPL-3.0-only and AGPL-3.0 -- v3 because typst.exe, which the installer carries, is Apache-2.0, compatible with GPL v3 and not with GPL v2; «or later» so that a later GPL needs no one's consent again. The official English text is `legal/licenses/GPL-3.0.txt` (already there for Qt, the FSF's to the byte) and the repository's `LICENSE`, the same bytes, which GitHub reads -- `test_legal` holds the two equal; `legal/LICENSE-fr.txt`, `-de.txt`, `-en.txt` present it in nine clauses alike, only the GPL's own text binding: what the GPL permits and asks, where the source is, the user's projects and reports outside it and the engine's copy in a project under it, a preliminary version, the engineer responsible for what he signs, the GPL's own disclaimer (sections 15, 16) with Art. 100 CO reserved, the third parties, the update check. The GPL asks that whoever receives the program can obtain its corresponding source: every version is published in `When-I-hit-the-wall/Woodcore-Publish` under its tag, the build scripts included, and the licence, *À propos de Woodcore…* and the site's legal page say where. *Price*: a version once published under the GPL stays free -- the choice cannot be taken back for it; a contribution from someone else would be theirs and the GPL's, so relicensing later would need them. A licence can only be granted by whoever holds the rights: the engineer states he alone owns the code (14.10.2026, §14). ⚠️ Still his (§14): the SIA's rights in the transcribed norm values of `calc/norms/`.
- *[superseded in v0.118]* **Woodcore is proprietary for now, © 2026 Florian Lamprecht, settled (v0.91.1).** *Superseded by «Woodcore is free software, GPL-3.0-or-later…» (14.10.2026).* The engineer's choice, sharing it with close colleagues while it changes fast: a short licence of his own in three languages rather than an open-source one, which could not be taken back for a version once published; the choice between staying proprietary, a non-commercial source-available licence and an open-source one (MIT/Apache, GPL/EUPL) is left for when the program is stable. The clause that matters most for a structural tool is the user's: the engineer who signs remains responsible, and liability is excluded as far as Art. 100 CO allows. The third-party notices are an obligation of distributing the installer, not a choice, and are therefore checked by the build rather than left to be remembered. ⚠️ Accepted, and stated to the engineer: ownership against his employer (Art. 17 LDA) and the BFH, the terms of use of the BFH's GitLab, the SIA's rights in the transcribed norm values shipped in `calc/norms/`, and the protection code written with an AI actually gets, are questions a licence file does not settle (§14).
- *[in force]* **Every colour of Woodcore is written once, for the site and the window, settled (v0.90).** `calc/tools/theme.json` holds the site's CSS custom properties and Woodcore's roles, light and dark; `website/build.py` writes it into `site.css` and `theme.py` builds the window's palette and style sheet from it (§10quater, §12). Chosen over Woodcore reading `site.css` -- the installed program ships no website -- and over two copies held equal by a test, which is §2 rule 1 kept by a guard where it can be kept by construction. It lives in `calc/tools/` because the program ships `calc/` whole and the site's build already imports from there. Accepted price: the site's style sheet in the repository is not the one served until built; its `/* @tokens */` line says where they are.
- *[in force]* **Fusion, dressed by one style sheet, replaces Windows 11's own style, settled (v0.90).** A style sheet reaches Windows 11's style unevenly and the prototype showed it; Fusion takes the palette and the sheet whole, looks the same on Windows 10 and 11, and is what the off-screen tests build. Accepted price: the window no longer takes Windows' accent colour -- an orange on the engineer's machine, which a selected tool shared with the orange of a value written over -- nor Windows 11's own controls. What a global sheet breaks had to be put right where it broke: the bars measured to the pixel since v0.87 keep their measures because a tool button's padding is 2 px, a symbol in a 30-px button is `compact`, and Fusion's faint check boxes are pictures of the scheme's.
- *[in force]* **The typography is the site's as a browser resolves it, settled (v0.90).** The engineer asked for «la typographie exacte du site internet» provided indices, exponents and mathematics show; measured in the browser, the site loads no font and shows Segoe UI and Cascadia Code on Windows, «Inter» and «JetBrains Mono» being named first and loaded by nothing. Put to him, he chose that -- nothing downloaded, nothing bundled -- over bundling Inter and JetBrains Mono (about 3 MB in the program, 0.4 MB on the site, and the site changing for everyone). `theme.installed` takes the first installed family of each stack, as a browser does, so an engineer who installs Inter sees the site and the window change together. Segoe UI's coverage is Segoe UI Variable's to the glyph for what a calculation is written with (`tests/test_theme.py`); the mono stack gained «Cascadia Mono», Cascadia Code without its ligatures, which the site turns off anyway. Accepted price: Cascadia is Windows Terminal's, shared with every program; without it the code is Consolas, on the site as in the window.
- *[in force]* **The scheme follows Windows unless the engineer chooses, per user; the density is compact, settled (v0.90).** His three answers to the proposal: Comme Windows by default with Clair and Sombre in *Fichier*, over following Windows alone and over always light; and a compact density, the rows keeping about the height they had, over the site's air, which showed some 24 rows of the loads tree where the window shows 36. The orange of a value written over stays the report's in the light scheme (4.7:1 on white) and is `#f0a050` in the dark one (8.8:1): the report is printed on white whatever the window's scheme, so only the light value is held equal to `lib/calc-engine.typ`.
- *[superseded in v0.117]* **The installer is published on GitLab Pages, through the Package Registry, settled (v0.89) -- superseded in v0.117 by the entry that follows.** The engineer's choice of two: a link of his own (NextCloud, SWITCHdrive) pasted at each version needs no token and no size budget, but is one more manual step and a second place a version lives. The project being private, a file of its registry cannot be read by anyone but a member — which is why the `pages` job fetches it with its own job token and serves it from the site, public once the Pages visibility is «Everyone». Accepted price: **Pages holds one site of at most 100 MB** by default, so only the newest installer is served, and the site's example pages had to become light (SVG, minified); an older version is downloaded from the registry by a member, or not at all.
- *[in force]* **The site is on Cloudflare Pages and the installers in a public GitHub repository, settled (v0.117).** The repository moved from the BFH's GitLab to GitHub, where it stays **private** (the engineer), and GitHub Pages is closed to a private repository on the free plan: the site went to **Cloudflare Pages**, project `woodcore`, https://woodcore.pages.dev/ (the `*.pages.dev` domain for now, his choice), built and published by `.github/workflows/site.yml` with wrangler. **It supersedes the entry above on the Package Registry.** Cloudflare Pages takes no file over 25 MiB and the installer is 95 MB, so the installer is no longer in the site: it is an asset of a Release of the **public** repository `When-I-hit-the-wall/woodcore-releases`, which holds nothing else (`website/site.json`'s `releases_repo`), and the site links to it. The registry's price, that only the newest installer was served, is gone: every Release stays. The workflow downloads the installer from its public address, with no token, and checks its size and SHA-256 against `website/releases.json` before it writes `version.json`; **where the check fails the build exits with 1 and nothing is deployed**, because a Cloudflare deployment replaces the live one and a site without its download and its manifest would erase theirs. ⚠️ **The program's rule for an installer's address is loosened, at a price, the engineer's answer of 07.10.2026.** `updates.validate` accepted an installer only on the manifest's own host, because a manifest pointing elsewhere is not one this program was built to trust. It now also accepts exactly `https://github.com/When-I-hit-the-wall/woodcore-releases/releases/download/…` (the host and the start of the path fixed in `updates.INSTALLER_HOST` and `INSTALLER_PREFIX`, checked on the parsed address: no port, user name, query, fragment, `..` or encoded separator), the SHA-256 and the size of the manifest being what make the file the right one. Lost: «the manifest and its file come from one server». A compromised Cloudflare account, which serves the manifest, could name another installer; a compromised GitHub account alone could not make this program accept a file. GitHub's redirect to a signed address of its own is followed and its hosts are not pinned (they were renamed once already), but never out of HTTPS. ⚠️ **No bridge** (the engineer): a Woodcore installed before v0.117 reads `FEED_URL` from the program itself, at the old address, and will never be told of a version published on the new site; the GitLab site stays online and is no longer updated. Whoever has one installs 0.117 by hand from the new site, which the page of what changes says (`website/content/<lang>/changes/0.117.0.md`). The ten installers published before are recorded `hosted: "gitlab"` in `releases.json` and are neither fetched nor offered; the site has no download until 0.117.0 is published (the engineer: wait for it, not republish 0.116.0). ⚠️ **Order of the commits**: the installer of 0.117.0 is built by the post-commit hook at the commit that changes `VERSION` and freezes `FEED_URL`, the rule for an installer's address, `legal.SITE` and the licence: the new address is committed before it, or 0.117.0 would look for updates at the old site for ever (`tests/test_site.py`, `test_no_live_file_names_the_retired_host`). ⚠️ **Two things recorded, not decided here.** The installer, which carries the SIA tables as readable JSON, is now in a public repository, more durable and easier to find than a page of the BFH's site: the engineer's call, as the site already masks the tables it prints in full (§13, v0.89). And the entry of v0.91.1 on the terms of use of the BFH's GitLab no longer concerns hosting; the same question for GitHub and Cloudflare is in `docs/open-questions.md`.
- *[in force]* **The list of the positions keeps to the page, and a table's columns may state a width, settled (v0.117).** The engineer's request of 08.10.2026: the columns of the table are chosen and given a width — a length or a percentage —, «le tableau ne doit pas dépasser dans la marge de droite», it runs over several pages, and nothing is printed where no plan is ticked. ⚠️ **It amends a written rule**: `lib/calc-engine.typ` and docs/domain/calculation-domains.md §3.3 said that a column «states what it holds and never how wide it is», so that every table read from the data is laid out by one declaration (`column-width`). The amendment is the narrowest that serves: **only `position-list-table` sets a column's optional `width` and the table's `fit: true`**, `render-table` reads them and builds no second `table(...)` — the three rules, the repeated heading and the page break stay in one place — and a table read from the data carries neither, and is laid out as before. Chosen over a second table in `positions.typ`, which would have duplicated the three-rule style. The cost, measured: nine columns do not fit an A4 portrait page at their least (their least sum is 574 pt of the 453 pt of text), so the old table **passed the right margin** — by 5 pt on three positions, by 64 pt on 59 positions of long values — and the new one narrows the widest columns first and hyphenates (a long heading, a long word), which reads worse than a table that fits: choose fewer columns, or a width. The refusals follow the command's convention — a width that is not one, or a column that is not one, **stops the report naming it** — and a budget that cannot be met (valid widths that leave the others less than 2 em) is a note in the report, in orange. **`show-position-list` with no plan ticked prints nothing** where it used to be refused (the window leaves `plans:` out), the refusal having been deliberate and its test the opposite one.
- *[in force]* **«Charges» gives way to «Variables», which lists every value with its provenance, settled (v0.117).** The engineer's issue 13: «Supprimer l'onglet *Charges* (`LoadsTab`) une fois vérifié qu'aucune de ses variables ne manque dans *Variables*» — which settles what the entry below left «to that day»: of the two tabs that would have listed the loads, the one that goes is the loads tab, and the six tabs of «Actions», when they have a content, will hold what «Variables» lists rather than a third copy of it. *Variables* is laid out in three panes — domains, the flat list of the chosen domain's variables, the detail — and lists **every value of the project**: the documents' registries, read from Typst as before, and the four files of `data/` that carry `vars` (`calculations.json`, `connections.json`, `connectors.json`, `scripts.json`; `calc/variable_sources.py`), read from disk at every showing. **Each value says where it comes from — Excel, Woodcore, or Typst and its document — and that is decided where the values are assembled and stored nowhere**: a value of a document's registry is Typst's whatever its `source` says (`#var(…, domain: (…))` copies an export's var, `excel:` source and all), and every other is what its `source` prefix says, through one table (`variables.PROVENANCE`) that a test holds against every source of the example project. The engineer chose: a **provenance column** in place of the document column (whose `Export · CU · BP · RS` it replaces), a box **«Tous les domaines»** to carry the filter over every domain, the loads tab's **traceability and calls** carried over (level, norm, cell, formula, choices, *Ouvrir le classeur*; `show-input`, `var-of`, `value-of`, `show-calc`, `show-live-load`, `show-check`, the loop, the two summaries), **«Résultats seulement» off by default**, **no copy by double-click or right-click** (one copies from *Affichage*), and the domains of `data/positions/<id>.json` left out while the export writes them empty. **What it costs**: a case's `tables` are no longer shown as text in the window — the report prints them —, and a copy is one click more than it was. `tests/test_variable_sources.py` (`test_no_variable_of_the_loads_tab_is_missing`) holds that none of the loads tab's variables is missing (spec `2026-10-08-onglet-variables-trois-colonnes`).
- *[in force]* **«Actions» has two sections, and the display options are kept in memory, settled (v0.117).** The two sections of «Actions» — permanent and variable — are **sub-titles inside one group** (a third kind of row of `chrome.SideNav`; `woodcore_gui.NAV_SECTIONS` says which tab each stands before) and not three groups, so that `NAV_GROUPS`, which a dozen tests read as `(title, (keys...))`, keeps its shape and the group's single title stays; their six tabs are `FutureTab` with their own names, and **«Charges» and «Combinaisons de charges» stay** below them after a thin line, until the new tabs have what they hold. ⚠️ **They will list what «Charges» lists** (`dead_load`, `live_load`, `snow` and `wind` are domains already): until they have their content they duplicate none of it, and when they do, one of the two is to give way — a decision left to that day, and taken on 08.10.2026: «Charges» gave way to «Variables» (the entry above). «Surcharge» is the engineer's word for the permanent load that is not the structure's (the glossary's «Poids propre non-structural», SIA 261's *Auflast*). **The options of an «Affichage» panel are kept while the window is open** — every export ends in a rebuild of the tabs that gave each panel its defaults back — **in memory, in no file**: the call pasted into the report is the durable form (rule 15), and a file would have been a new data contract with its schema, its version and its migration (the engineer chose this over a per-user setting and a per-project file). **«Surfacique» keeps its three modes with a position's side imposed**, and records no which-side-was-fixed: a position whose dimension changes later leaves the polygon as it was while the element's `h` follows — known, accepted, `schema_version` unchanged. **The saving of a position takes 12 to 15 s on the example project**, two thirds of it the window's rebuild of its 32 tabs on its own thread: measured and explained in the specification (§13), five remedies proposed, **the first of them chosen by the engineer and done** (the entry below).
- *[in force]* **After a position is saved the window is not built again; each tab is built again when it is next shown, settled (v0.117).** The engineer, 08.10.2026, chose the first of the five remedies proposed for the 12 to 15 s a position took to save: «rafraîchir que les onglets qui les lisent». Measured (§13 of the specification): two thirds of that time was the window building its 32 tabs again on its own thread — Qt attaching widgets to a window that is shown (`setCentralWidget`, `addTab`, `addWidget`: five of the seven seconds of a rebuild), not the tabs' own work. **How**: a position saved imports **lightly** (`positions_tab.reimport` → `reexport(light=True)`); when that export ends well `MainWindow.refresh_lightly` reads the window's data again, brings up to date the line beside the project's name and the banner of workbooks changed since the export, marks **every tab stale** except the Positions tab — which holds what it has just written — and builds again at once the one in front if it is another; every stale tab is built again **when it is next shown, before it is seen** (`refresh_if_stale` in `on_tab_changed`, `refresh_tab`), with what it kept through a rebuild (`view_state`, its display's options). Everything else — «Import données», a PDF asked for, a reload, a language, an RFEM import — is a full rebuild as before, and a light refresh that goes wrong is one. **Chosen over a list of the tabs that read positions**: that list would have to be kept in step with the code of every tab — and with what an export derives from a position (a schema's b and h, the connections that read them) — and a tab missing from it would show, silently, what the export no longer says, the defect this project fears most; the tabs already re-read the disk when shown (`on_shown`), and this extends the rule to the whole tab. The cost: **the first visit of a tab costs its construction** (0.5 to 1.1 s, measured: the plan 0.8, the schema 0.9, the fire plan 1.1) where the engineer used to pay it all at once; a light refresh itself takes 0.01 s with the Positions tab in front and 0.7 s with another, against the 10.2 s of the rebuild; a tab nobody opens is never built again, and one holding unsaved work keeps it through `view_state`, as through a rebuild.
- *[in force]* **A window at the start, once per version, and «Installer» installs, settled (v0.89).** The engineer's choices over a banner — which can go unseen, the dependency check's own form (§10quater) being an offer to a developer rather than news to every user — and over sending him to a web page. «Plus tard» is **a day and not a start**, because each project opens in a process of its own (§13, v0.81): three projects opened in a morning would otherwise be three questions. The installer is **run, not silenced**: its own wizard, in its own language, is what the user expects of an update, and it opens the new version at its end. ⚠️ **What makes the download safe enough to run**: HTTPS, the installer on the manifest's own host, its size and its SHA-256 checked before anything is started, and the file written beside its name and moved into place only once it checks. The SHA-256 comes from the same site as the file, so it guards against a corrupted or cut download and not against a compromised site; signing the installer is what would (§14).
- *[in force]* **The update check lives in the installed program only, settled (v0.89).** A checkout is updated by git, and a window offering to install an older-looking or newer-looking installer over the engineer's sources would be noise at best. `WOODCORE_UPDATE_CHECK=1` asks from the sources all the same, to try it.
- *[in force]* **The website is generated from what Woodcore reads, settled (v0.89).** §2 rule 1 applied to a third reader of the documentation: Tinymist's hover, the commands tab and the site read the same `///` blocks, the same translations and the same fences. What the site writes itself — its pitch, its tutorial, its download page — is prose no other reader has, in `website/`. Chosen over a static-site framework, which would have been a new toolchain (Node, or a Python package pinned beside the three) for a site one Python file builds with the standard library and the openpyxl already pinned.
- *[in force]* **The norm tables are not published, settled (v0.89).** The engineer's choice: a site anyone can read is not the report of a project, and SIA 261's Tabelle 8 or SIA 265's Tabellen 24 to 41 set in full is a reproduction of the norm rather than a citation of it. The rule is a list of globs in `website/site.json`, because «sets a table in full» is a property of the page an example prints, which nothing in the data says. ⚠️ `show-connector-resistance` is masked too, its level 1 printing Tabelle 24 for its diameter as `#distance` does — the one entry of the list the engineer did not name, and one line to remove.
- *[in force]* **0.89.0, bumped for the update check, settled (v0.89).** The first installer carrying the check has to be installed by hand, whatever it is numbered; a new number is what makes it the one to publish first, and what makes the engine copy of every project older, which the program asks about (§12). `lib/` did not change.
- *[in force]* **Every separating dash the report prints is a long one, settled (v0.88).** The engineer: «tous les tirets (également entre une valeur et un label) doivent être des longs tirets, pas des tirets courts». Between a value and its label it already was (`#var`, `#eq`, `#val` set `---`, U+2014); what was a short dash is now long: a member check's heading «Biegung mit Druck — S001» and «Stabilität — Kippen», a connector figure's caption and a Figur 45 plate's «Bauteil 1 — Seitenholz» (dowel.py writes the German, which is the glossary's key), an empty cell of the polar moment's table, the notes under a static model's figure (`b_e = 3.6 m — description`), and every spaced dash of calc/i18n/strings.json, keys and values in three languages. ⚠️ **Kept short, deliberately**: a range, «Verbindungsmittel 1–4», «Öffnung A–D», where the short dash is the typographic rule and not a separator; the title of a document cited as it is written (bauforumstahl's «Tragwerksplanung im Stahlbau – Gelenkige Anschlüsse»); and the window's own sentences, which are not the report. An export written before v0.88 still carries the short dash in the strings it holds until it is exported again.

- *[in force]* **The member checks are commands of the report, a member written down once, settled (v0.88).** The engineer's option B of three (§3.3undecies): A, a command per check taking the section and the material each time, repeats them; C, one command choosing the checks from the actions given, decides for the engineer and is left for later, on top of B. The prefix is `verify-`, which the checks share with `#verify`, and not `check-`, which `show-check` would have made read like the export's. Every clause of 4.2 in the first version («il faut inclure tous les cas»), Anhang C and SIA 265/1's materials excepted (§14).
- *[in force]* **A check is a run of `#eq` and `#verify`, not arithmetic of its own, settled (v0.88).** One unit algebra, one set of levels, one way of stacking a wide formula, one verdict mark: a check prints the way a calculation written by hand prints, and a change to how `#eq` prints reaches every check. Accepted price: a check at level 3 is long, every formula being printed in letters and in numbers, and every check is computed with states and contexts, which a report of fifty members has not yet been compiled with (§14).
- *[in force]* **The exact relative slenderness, with Anhang B's values, and E_0,05 times eta_w, settled (v0.88).** The engineer chose the exact form over the simplified (35)/(36)/(43)/(44), and asked for eta_w on E_0,05 as well as on the strengths, where the answer proposed was «on the strengths only», λ_rel being a ratio of characteristic values. Two consequences, stated: the Bemessungsbeispiele's glulam examples, which take λ/60 and 0.06·√(a·h)/b, are not reproduced (the fixture says why), and in Feuchteklasse 2 or 3 the checks come out a little more severe than the book's method. E_0,05 is Tabelle 47's or 48's, never 2/3 or 5/6 of E_0,mean — the reference templates take the footnote's ratio, 7333 where the norm gives 7400 for C24.
- *[in force]* **k_h applies to glulam bending by default, settled (v0.88).** 3.4.2.3 allows it («darf») and the Bemessungsbeispiele apply it on every glulam beam under 600 mm; `k-h: false` takes it off. It reaches f_m in bending and in lateral torsional buckling alike, the book's own practice.
- *[in force]* **A table is cited «SIA 265 Tabelle 8», without §, settled (v0.88).** `norm-text` treats a `norm_ref` starting with «Tabelle » as it treats an annex, as `distance-cite` already did; no export carried one, so no page moved.
- *[in force]* **A bare number of `#verify` keeps the unit it is written with, settled (v0.88).** `eta <= 100 "%"` prints «100 %», where the unit table made it «1»; the comparison is made on the base values as before.


- *[in force]* **Every key is accepted, settled (v0.87, second half), reversing v0.79's refusal (periodic audit 260916, B19 and AUD-7-425).** The engineer: «Une clé comme M2 reste refusée». The first half of v0.87 had left it open as a question of what `M2` means — M with the subscript 2, or one upright word — and the answer is the one a symbol written by hand already had: `key-symbol` reads such a key through `quoted-words`, so a letter and its digits are that letter and those digits (`M2` is M and 2, as `gamma_(M1)` gives M and 1) and any other word is upright (`FEd`, `Mx`); M with the subscript 2 is written `M_2`, as it always was, and a key that starts with one letter or a Greek name is printed exactly as before. One rule for a key and for a symbol typed beside it, rather than a second reading to learn. `atom-symbol` keeps such a symbol one atom in a formula's letters line, so `M2 / a` is a fraction of M2. What stays refused is what a formula would misread: a `-`, a leading `_`, and an operation's name — `dot`, `max`, `sqrt` — which would be substituted into `max(a, b)` (§10quinquies). Accepted price: the printed form of such a key is the reader's and not a choice of the engineer's — `FEd` is one upright word, where F with the subscript Ed is `F_Ed` — and a key whose printed form matters is given its symbol as the second argument, as before.
- *[in force]* **A formula too wide for its row is stacked at its `=` signs, not shrunk and not left to overflow, settled (v0.87, second half).** The engineer: «Une longue fraction qui ne peut pas passer à la ligne déborde dans la colonne des citations». Overflowing is the defect; shrinking the line, or setting its fractions inline so that it can break, would overrule his own `show math.frac` rule in `config.typ` — which is his to make — and set one line unlike the lines round it. Stacking is how a calculation too long for a line is set on paper: the letters, then `= <numbers>`, then `= <answer>`, the signs one under the other (§10quinquies). ⚠️ **Decided by measurement, and by nothing else**: the one-line form is measured against the width its row has, and a row that fits goes through the code it always went through, so no page that was right moved — the snow, wind, dead-load, live-load and polar-moment pages came out pixel-identical. The citation stands at the end of the first line, on its baseline, rather than centred in a row a tall fraction makes taller. Accepted price: every row carrying a formula is measured, and a single segment wider than the whole text is still left as Typst sets it (§14).
- *[in force]* **The engine is checked when a project opens, in the very box the export asks with, settled (v0.87, second half).** The engineer: «Est-il possible que, lors de l'ouverture d'un projet qui tourne sur un ancien moteur, un message demande si le user veut uploader le moteur à la dernière version ?». It is the export's comparison (`project.engine_state`, §12) made as the window comes up, and each state gets what an export would do with it: nothing where nothing is to be done, a silent copy where an export copies without asking (absent, stale), the question where an export asks (older, unknown, edited), and where an export refuses (newer) the whole window read-only, the engineer having said yes to closing the one door an export's refusal left open — a drawing saved from a tab; a project opened read-only, which this window may not write, is asked nothing (§10quater). «Plus tard» writes nothing and keeps nothing, so the question comes back at the next opening. ⚠️ **One box for the two places** — `engine_box` and `ask_engine`, the export keeping only its own two buttons — so the question at the opening cannot come to say something the export's does not; and «Voir les différences…» is offered for an older and an unknown copy as well as an edited one, a difference being what the engineer weighs before letting a report print otherwise. Found on the way and repaired: the export's up-front check named *older* and *edited* and let *unknown* through, which wrote `data/` and then stopped at the engine; it refuses all of `project.ASKED` now (§12).
- *[in force]* **The installer is rebuilt by a `post-commit` hook, from a clean worktree of the commit, in the background, settled (v0.87, second half).** The engineer: «L'installateur devrait également automatiquement être reconstruit à chaque nouvelle version». **A hook**, because a version is made by a commit — `VERSION` bumped in `calc/project.py` and committed — whatever made that commit, a terminal, a session or the engineer; the hook asks git whether `VERSION` changed and does nothing else, and a clone opts in once (`auto_build.py install`), a hook being git's to run and not the repository's to impose (§12). **The commit, not the working tree**: several sessions edit the working tree at once, and a build of it would freeze whatever they had half written, where the commit is a state somebody declared finished; the installer is then exactly the version its commit names. **Detached**: a build is some eight minutes, and a hook that waited would hold the commit — and every terminal or tool reading its output — for all of them; git ignores what a post-commit hook returns, so a build that fails cannot fail the commit either, and its log in `dist/` says what became of it. Accepted price: **only committed files are built**, so a version's commit must carry its new files; an amend rebuilds nothing, the installer being there (`start --force`); the build takes `python` and `typst` from the PATH; and it is per clone, each clone activating it once.
- *[in force]* **`#var` is inline, and where its extras go is an argument, settled (v0.87), reversing v0.61's line of its own.** The engineer: «de base, #var doit s'afficher inline (afin de pouvoir afficher plusieurs var les uns après les autres). S'il y a deux retours à la ligne, alors ça fait un nouveau paragraphe.» Asked where the label, the description and the citation then go, he chose neither of the two fixed placements put to him but **an argument**, on `var`, `var-unit`, `eq` and `val` alike: «un argument permet de régler si la note vient en ligne (comme actuellement) ou en note de bas de page». `note: "footnote"` gathers the three into one footnote, `note: "line"` sets them on the line (§10quinquies). The defaults are each command's own shape: `"footnote"` for `var`, `var-unit` and `val`, which are words of a sentence, `"line"` for `eq`, a formula on a line of its own with a citation column beside it, so no `#eq` page moved. Chosen over one fixed placement for all four, which would have put either a footnote marker on every formula or an em dash in the middle of every sentence. ⚠️ **Accepted price**: a report written before v0.87 with consecutive `#var` calls now reads them as one paragraph — the example project's `Sections/005` among them — and needs a blank line, or `note: "line"`, where a line was meant (§14).
- *[in force]* **The PDF is written beside its `.typ`, settled (v0.87), reversing v0.23's place one level above, beside the workbooks.** The engineer's choice between the two places put to him: «le pdf doit être créé au root et pas un dossier au-dessus». `project.DEFAULT_LAYOUT["pdf"]` is `Statischer Bericht (typst)/Statischer Rapport.pdf`, the Typst root, which is also Tinymist's default output — so the template's `.vscode/settings.json` sets no `outputPath` at all, one setting fewer to keep in step with the build (§12).
