Dimensions a calculation needs are entered manually in Typst at the point of calculation. Chosen over teaching the parser a second shape when a CLT deck (`D001`, thickness `180`) hit the `"<b>/<h>"` assumption: the column was the last part of a position record that pretended to be calculation input, which §7-§8 say it is not. A panel, a round column, or anything else now needs no special case, because nothing reads the string back as a number — with one exception since v0.75 (above): `schema.parse_section` reads a b/h `dimension` as the default section of a detail schema's element, leniently.
- *[in force]* **Neither Excel carries loads, settled (v0.18).** `Positionen.xlsx` lost its `Loads` sheet and `docs/typst-reference.xlsx` its generated one (§8, §10ter). Loads are calculated in, or from, the Belastungsannahmen workbook, so a `Loads` row elsewhere could only be a second source for the same number. The condition for letting `Positionen.xlsx` carry loads again is stated and narrow: a load calculated in Python from entries that have nowhere else to live. Accepted price: the sheet has to be deleted by hand — nothing here writes to the engineer's workbook — so `export_all.py` warns on every run until it is gone.
- *[in force]* **Two non-overlapping Excel workbooks, settled (v0.10).** `Positionen.xlsx` (hand-edited) and `docs/typst-reference.xlsx` (generated) used to both carry a `Positions` sheet with near-duplicate content, plus a `Sections` sheet on the generated side that added nothing over `Positions`. Fixed by removing `Positions`/`Sections` from the generated workbook entirely (§10ter) rather than merging the two files into one physical workbook — position ids valid for `show-calc`/`show-check` remain discoverable there via `Domains`/`Steps`/`Checks`/`Vars`.
- *[in force]* **Axes moved out of the data, settled.** Plan axes are no longer stored in `data/positions/*.json`; they are supplied as an argument (to `calc-block()` since v0.12, or to `show-position()` directly) and treated as a presentation detail, not calculation data.
- *[in force]* **The calculation block is the report's unit of work, settled (v0.12).** Calculations are grouped by `calc-block(positions, ...)[ ... ]`, which declares the position(s) the group concerns, prints their descriptive summaries as its header, and frames the body (§5). Attribution runs block → position and is presentation only — it writes nothing to a position and creates no position↔load link, so it does not reopen §8. Chosen over heading-based grouping (couples report structure to data) and over the v0.4-v0.11 implicit banner on `show-calc`'s first mention of a position (invisible, order-dependent, and unable to express one block covering several positions). Because the block knows its positions, `show-calc`/`show-check`/`show-input` are scope-checked against it and panic on a position the block does not declare.
- *[partly retired in v0.11]* **First MWE built, settled (2026-08-25); calculation half retired (v0.11).** A single position (`B01`, later `T001`, a roof beam) exercised the full pipeline end-to-end: `Positionen.xlsx` (read via openpyxl) -> `calc/export_all.py` -> validated `data/*.json` -> `lib/calc-engine.typ` -> `Sections/003-baustoffe.typ` through `006-nachweise.typ`. As of v0.11 the surviving end-to-end path is Excel -> loads + descriptive positions -> `show-input` / `show-position` in `Sections/003` and `/004`; the `show-calc`/ `show-check` half (`Sections/005`, `/006`) is dormant, pending §14. Everything new is named in English (`snow.py`, `bending.py`, `combinations.py`, the `actions`/`bending` domains, `--input level=`), except the report's pre-existing German section headings, which are left as-is. Symbols render as real Typst math (§6bis) and norm citations render as right-margin notes via a `grid` layout (§10).

- *[in force]* **A command's documentation lives in its `///` block and nowhere else, settled (v0.64).** The summary, the arguments and the worked examples of every command are in `lib/`, and Tinymist's hover, `docs/typst-reference.xlsx` and Woodcore's commands tab all read that one text (§10sexies). Chosen over a catalog file written beside the window, which would have been quicker and would have been a second description of the same functions, ageing without anything saying so — §2 rule 1, applied to documentation. Accepted price: ~150 example fragments had to be written into the engine's own files, and they name this project's real ids (`"SL"`, `"Velo-Unterstand"`, `"SD_01-8mm"`), so the documentation is coupled to this project's export in a way it was not before. That is deliberate: an example the window can compile and a reader can paste is worth more than one that is true of no project in particular, and the test says so out loud when the data moves.
- *[in force]* **The category of a command is hand-written and held by a test in both directions, settled (v0.64).** `build_reference.py`'s `CATEGORIES` names the ~60 public commands in five groups; everything else falls to Internes by subtraction (§10sexies). Chosen over deriving a category from the file, from the `// ---` banner a `#let` sits under, or from "carries a `///`" — the last being the tempting one and the wrong one, since five documented bindings are internals and two public ones carried no header at all. Accepted price: a list to maintain. `tests/test_commands.py` is what makes that safe, failing on a category that names a command the engine no longer defines and on a public command with no header or no examples.
- *[in force]* **An example is compiled, one per document, settled (v0.64).** Each ` ```typ ` fence is its own Typst run, rendered at the report's own 16 cm measure (§10sexies). Batching a command's examples onto pages of one document is cheaper and incorrect: `#unit` sets a dimension's unit to the end of the document and `#var` writes into a document-wide registry, so one example would silently change the next. Accepted price: ~124 compilations in the test suite, about 28 s, and one arrow-click of latency in the window — against which the cache buys everything back, and the per-example run is also what lets the tab render only what was actually opened.
- *[in force]* **The preview is a PNG and the SVG route is closed, settled (v0.64), on a measurement rather than a memory.** One page of this report exported with `--format svg` carries 284 `<symbol>` definitions and 527 `xlink:href` uses: Typst writes every glyph as a `<use>` of a `<symbol>`, and QtSvg implements SVG Tiny 1.2, which has no `<symbol>` — the text would not appear at all. It emits no `clipPath`, mask, gradient or CSS, so nothing else was in question. Recorded here so it is not proposed again on the grounds that SVG would scale better, which it would.

- *[in force]* **The plan's tool bar wraps, and its two rows are laid out apart, settled (v0.67).** The bar is what made the tab's splitter undraggable (§10quater), so the question was what gives when the pane is narrower than the bar. Three answers were weighed and two rejected. **Scrolling sideways**, which is what v0.66 chose for the static models' bar, was written first and rejected on sight: that bar's height is draggable and this one's is not, so at the window's own default width six controls sat off the right edge — M, L, the variant combo, *Annuler*, *Rétablir* and *Ajuster*. **Clipping** hides a tool with nothing to say so, which is the rule v0.66 states and this project's worst failure mode. **Wrapping** costs height, about 26 px a row, and loses nothing; the drawing is what the width was wanted for, so height is what it pays with. Laying the two rows out **apart** rather than in one grid came with it and is not cosmetic: sharing the grid's columns is what had been eliding the labels, and it is what made the bar's minimum the sum of the columns' maxima rather than of either row. Accepted price: the flow layout is now read by two tabs, so it moved out of `statics_tab.py` into `calc/tools/flowbar.py` — one mechanism, one file. Its one change there is that an item whose widget is hidden takes no place, which the plan's variant combo needs and the static models' bar has no case of.
- *[in force]* **A short tool bar scrolls sideways rather than wrapping, settled (v0.66), reversing v0.51.** v0.51 chose wrapping over scrolling and §10quater said so in those words; the engineer asked for the opposite after using the tab on a 0.5 m bench, where the bar took four rows above a drawing area 400 px tall. Wrapping is still what a tall pane does — what changed is that a *short* pane is no longer a *clipped* one. Chosen over the two alternatives. A bar that simply clips loses tools with nothing to say so, which is the failure mode this project treats as the worst kind. And a **vertical** scroll bar, the obvious way to reach a wrapped row, is the same failure wearing a control: a tool bar scrolled up and down hides a group silently, where a horizontal bar is visibly saying there is more to the right. Accepted price: at a middling height the bar is both wrapped *and* scrollable, which is two things at once and has to be seen to be understood; and the layout is now computed twice over — once by `_lay` to draw, once by `wrap_width` to size — which is exactly where v0.66's own defect lived, and is why `_rows_at` is written to break on the same pixel `_lay` breaks on.
- *[in force]* **The worked examples are frozen against a snapshot of `data/`, settled (v0.65).** An example names real ids, so it read the live export and moved with it; it now reads `000-Python/examples/data/`, a copy refreshed only by `build_examples.py --freeze` (§10sexies). Chosen over a purpose-built toy project, which would have been smaller and cleaner and would have meant rewriting all 126 examples against invented ids and maintaining a toy workbook to generate them. Accepted price: 1.4 MB in Git, and a snapshot that ages — which is the point, not a defect. ⚠️ What forced the folder's shape is that the engine finds its data by relative path, so the only way to point it elsewhere is to put a copy of the engine beside the data. The alternative, a `--input data=…` knob in `calc-engine.typ`, was rejected: it is fewer files but puts a global switch in the engine for documentation's sake, and a typo would silently read the wrong data.
- *[in force]* **The MWEs are generated from the fences and committed, settled (v0.65).** The example is written once, in the `///` block; `build_examples.py` writes it out as a real `.typ`, versioned and never hand-edited — the `docs/typst-reference.xlsx` arrangement. Chosen over hand-written example files, which would have made the file the original and left the `///` block pointing at it: the editor's hover would have lost its examples, and §2 rule 14 would have had two texts to keep in step. Accepted price: a generator to run after editing a fence, which the test names when it has not been run.
- *[in force]* **The pages are pre-rendered and committed, settled (v0.65).** The tab loads a PNG and compiles nothing — twenty examples opened in 271 ms, and it works on a machine with no Typst at all. Asked for as a speed question; the answer is that the speed does not come from the format. **SVG cannot be displayed** (Typst writes every glyph as a `<use>` of a `<symbol>`; QtSvg is SVG Tiny 1.2, which has none) and **PDF buys nothing** (QtPdf would rasterise it at display time — still a render, where a PNG is a load). Accepted price: ~14.5 MB in Git, and a committed page that can lag the engine until somebody regenerates.
- *[in force]* **The documentation's French and German are a file of their own, matched on the English, settled (v0.86).** `calc/i18n/commands.json`, one unit per paragraph and per argument carrying the English it translates (§10sexies). The engineer chose it over two alternatives put to him: `/// fr:` and `/// de:` lines in the blocks themselves, one file per command but three languages in Tinymist's hover and lib/ tripled in documentation, and the summary alone translated, less to keep up and a tab half in English. Matching on the English rather than on the position is what keeps a translation from saying an old sentence as though it said the new one; the test is what keeps an edit to a `///` block from being finished without it. Accepted price: a second text per language, kept up by hand — 7 500 words each today — and a failing test in any session that edits a block's prose without translating it.
- *[in force]* **The examples are frozen from the example project, settled (v0.86).** Never from the project that is open: the snapshot is committed into the program's repository, which is shared with developers, and v0.82 already kept the architect's plans out of it (§11, §10sexies). `tests/projet-exemple/` is the anonymised Musterprojekt the suite works on, so documentation, tests and pages name one set of ids. Accepted price: an example names the example project's ids (`SD-10`), which the engineer's own project need not have (`VU-SD10`) — the copy buttons, which name the open project's, are what compiles in his report.
- *[in force]* **The example pages are rendered in German only, settled (v0.86).** The engineer's choice over French pages compiled on demand or a second committed set: the labels the data carries are German whatever the window says, and an example that wants another language calls `set-report-language` itself.

- *[in force]* **The report's folder is named at the project's creation, and `DEFAULT_LAYOUT` does not move, settled (v0.100).** The engineer: «Lors de la création d'un nouveau projet dans woodcore, le nom de ce dossier est demandé. Par défaut "Rapport de statique", dépendant de la langue choisie», and «sans (typst)». ⚠️ **The way it was built is the opposite of the obvious one, and had to be**: `project.DEFAULT_LAYOUT` names `Statischer Bericht (typst)` three times (`report`, `pdf`, `generated`) and **stays exactly as it is**, because `Project.__init__` does `{**DEFAULT_LAYOUT, **data.get("layout", {})}` and **both real projects state no `layout` at all** — checked, the engineer's own `Musterprojekt.woodcore` carries only name/company/author/gui and the example project's only name/company/author. Renaming the constant would have made the report folder of both unfindable at once. So it is now «the layout of a project created before v0.100», a new project writes its own `layout` into its `.woodcore` (only `report`, `templates.layout_of` deriving `pdf` and `generated` from it), and **nothing on disk is renamed in a project that already exists**. Accepted price: two projects of different ages have differently named report folders, which is what «named at creation» means. A consequence worth recording: `packaging/build.py` and `packaging/launcher.py` needed **no change at all**, since what they name is the constant and the example project.
- *[in force]* **`templates/projet/`'s own folder is deliberately not renamed, settled (v0.100).** It is still `Statischer Bericht (typst)`, matching `DEFAULT_LAYOUT` — because `stated_layout` compares a template's layout against that constant, so renaming the folder would make Standard *state* a report folder and `report_folder_offered` would never offer the language's word again. The folder is an implementation detail of the template: every project made from it renames it. A template that **does** name its report folder in its own `template.json` means it, and keeps it whatever the language.
- *[in force]* **Only the folder is renamed, never the entry file, settled (v0.100).** His answer C6. `Statischer Rapport.typ` keeps its name and so does the PDF, which matters because since v0.87 Tinymist states no `outputPath` and names the PDF after the `.typ` — `woodcore-cli`, «Exporter PDF» and Tinymist must all name one PDF (§12). The `.vscode/settings.json` comments that named the German folder are generic now, since they are copied into every project.
- *[in force]* **One language choice sets both, settled (v0.100).** His answer C9: the creation dialog and `woodcore-cli new --language` set the document's language (config.typ's `doc-lang`) and the engine's (`#set-report-language`) together, where the files keep them apart for the reason §4bis gives. Two settings, one question — and the report folder is offered in that language.
- *[in force]* **The folder's name is checked before anything is created, settled (v0.100).** ⚠️ The defect this was written for: the name was validated *after* the skeleton had been copied, so a refused one left a half-copied project and the next attempt then failed on «is not empty» instead of on the name. `check_report_folder` refuses a separator, `.`/`..`, what Windows refuses in a name, a trailing dot and a reserved device name — at the one moment the name is typed.
- *[in force]* **A script's result reaches the report as a named value, settled (v0.100).** The engineer's choice of three: named values exported and read with `#val(..., domain: ("script", "<id>"))`, over a full `{vars, steps, checks}` record printed by `show-calc` (more powerful, and every step and formula would have to be declared in the script) and over a scratchpad whose numbers are retyped (which is what his own D10 answer refused). Its best property is that it needed **no new Typst command**: `val`, `show-calc` and `find` already read a pair `(domain, instance)`, so only `find-calculation`'s branch was added.
- *[in force]* **numpy alone, settled (v0.100).** His choice over numpy + SciPy: `numpy.linalg` solves any linear system, and the installer stays near 100 MB rather than 130-150, which Pages' 100 MB budget beside the site could not have held (§12). Accepted price: a non-linear equation is solved by Newton or bisection written out, which the tab's own template shows.
- *[in force]* **A script is read-only on the project, and that is a guard and not a sandbox, settled (v0.100).** His choice of three. The guard replaces `open`/`io.open`, which covers nearly every accidental write including `Path.write_text`; `os.open` and `subprocess` get round it. ⚠️ It is documented as a guard **out loud**, because a promise of safety nobody can keep is worse than a guard somebody understands.
- *[in force]* **Every export runs every script, settled (v0.100).** Chosen over saving the results of the last run: a script's numbers must be the numbers the current data gives, which is §2 rule 1, and it is what every other domain already does. Accepted price: a slow script slows every export, which is why the record carries its `seconds` — and why a run is bounded by a timeout.
- *[in force]* **A script's run time is not exported, settled (v0.100).** The record carried `seconds` for one afternoon, which is a wall-clock duration and made `data/scripts.json` differ between two exports of an unchanged project — against §12's promise that an unchanged export stays unchanged to the byte, and for the same reason `data/project.json` carries no date. It is out of the record and out of the contract; `run` still reports it, and Woodcore's status line still shows it, neither being exported. ⚠️ It was invisible until the example project carried a script, which is what made `test_export.test_the_export_is_reproducible` say so — a reminder that a fixture holding none of a new kind of thing is a fixture that checks nothing about it.
- *[in force]* **A `dirty` declares a tab an editor, settled (v0.100).** `tests/test_window.py`'s `_editors` is every tab that *has* a `dirty`, and it calls `update_list_marker()` on each and asserts that each lists something — so in this window the flag is not merely a flag: it is what a tab owes the window's row marks by. The **Python** tab keeps it and gained the marker, being a genuine editor of files; the **«Formules»** tab dropped it, having no list at all and saving nothing of its own, so a dot would come on at its first keystroke and never clear. A tab that only browses carries no `dirty`, which is why `MainWindow.update_tab_marks` reads it with a `getattr` default.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **A deterministic parser and no AI, settled (v0.100).** His answers E12 and E13. A parser has a fixed grammar: same lines, same Typst, always; a line outside it is refused naming the line; no network, no key, no cost; and it cannot produce a command that does not exist. An AI tolerates any wording and in exchange can produce a wrong unit, an inexistent command or a plausible and wrong clause, differently each time — irreproducible, which in a report whose whole premise is traceability (§1bis) is the wrong tool *inside* the loop that writes the calculations. ⚠️ The right place for one, if ever: **in front of** the parser, turning free prose into the keyword lines the engineer then reads — never behind it.
- *[retired in v0.117]* **The keyword grammar knows each command's arguments, settled (v0.100).** A value the block states is handed to the checks that take it and to no other, rather than to all of them: `verify-shear` given a buckling length would refuse, and a translator whose output does not compile is worse than none — which is why the check that matters most in `tests/test_dsl.py` compiles the emitted Typst and reads the member's name back off the page.
- *[in force]* **A script's identity is in its own file and shown read-only, settled (v0.100).** A deliberate departure from every other editor tab, which offers Nom · Description · ID as a form: the identity is three `@` comment lines the engineer edits in the very pane he edits the code in, so a form beside it would be a second place for one thing (§2 rule 1) and a round-trip to keep right. *Renommer* renames the file, which is the ID.

- *[in force]* **An excerpt is anchored on its title's bookmark, not on its text, settled (v0.111).** The engineer asked to «sélectionner le titre du diagramme» by dragging over its text (C7), so that a new version of the print report two pages longer still gives the right diagram. On his own RFEM export the titles carry **no text at all** — every heading is vector paths, which pypdf and QtPdf read the same way — so the text route was closed by measurement, and the outline RFEM writes, one bookmark per heading with its page and its height, is what the anchor reads (§3.3undevicies). A click on the title stands in for the drag: it names the bookmark just above it. Chosen over OCR (a new dependency, results that differ between machines, and a misread «AUFLAS1» breaking the anchor in silence) and over matching the title's picture across pages (the watermark of a trial version crosses titles at different places on each page). ⚠️ Accepted price: a PDF with neither text nor bookmarks — anything printed through a PDF printer — cannot be followed at all, and its excerpts are refused until confirmed again after every new version; the tab and the «Nouveautés» page tell the engineer to export the report from its own program instead.
- *[in force]* **A title is compared without its chapter number, and told apart by the titles above it, settled (v0.111).** The engineer's own report renumbered the diagram of CC1 from 6.4 to 5.4 between two versions, so the leading number is left out (`annex.title_key`); a title used several times is narrowed by its path one parent at a time and, where the path is not enough, by its rank — his answer C8a, «un signet spécial». Chosen over the rank alone, which moves the day a load case is added in front, and over the whole path always, which would break on the root bookmark RFEM names after the project.
- *[in force]* **An excerpt that cannot be found stops the report, settled (v0.111).** His answer C9, the warn-then-refuse every other domain already follows: the export warns and records the reason, `#show-extract` panics with it. Chosen over printing the last place it was found, which prints a diagram that may not be the one the text speaks of — the silently wrong page this pipeline exists to stop.
- *[in force]* **The page is cropped by the export, and declares PDF 1.7, settled (v0.111).** Typst 0.15.1 draws a page of clipped image tiles wrongly away from the origin, so the export writes each excerpt as a one-page PDF whose MediaBox is the frame; and Typst refuses to set RFEM's PDF 2.0 in a report exported as 1.7, so the pages the export writes declare 1.7. Chosen over raising the report's own PDF target, which would have to be done in `woodcore-cli`, Woodcore's «Exporter PDF» and every project's Tinymist settings at once, for a feature that needs it only in the pages it writes itself. ⚠️ Accepted price: a page carrying a feature of PDF 2.0 that 1.7 does not know would be drawn as 1.7 draws it; the engineer's 29 pages draw identically either way.
- *[in force]* **An excerpt and its annex page are linked by where they land, settled (v0.111).** A label written twice is an error in Typst and an excerpt may be shown twice, so each end leaves a `metadata` and links to the location `query` finds. Chosen over one label per excerpt, which would stop a report that shows the same diagram twice, and over no link where no annex is printed — the query simply finds nothing, and the excerpt is a plain figure.
- *[in force]* **The annexes carry the pages of the excerpts the report shows, by default, settled (v0.111).** His answer E15: «uniquement les pages utilisées». Typst decides which pages, from what the report actually shows, where the export can only know which excerpts exist; the export writes every candidate page and Typst prints the ones it needs. Chosen over the export deciding, which would carry an excerpt's page into the annexes of a report that never shows it. `all` and `none` are the source's own choice in the tab.
- *[in force]* **«Annexes» sits in the Projet group, after the journal, and in the three documents, settled (v0.115), reversing v0.111's «Référence, above Formules».** The engineer's decision of 06.10.2026 when Gian's branch was ported: the annexes are what the project holds, not a reference, and each of the convention, the base and the report may show an excerpt (`DOCUMENT_TABS`). The two folders are in the tab's own list pane, «dans le deuxième volet», not in the side navigation (A1).
- *[in force]* **An excerpt has no levels, settled (v0.111).** His answer D13: a picture of another program's page is printed whole or not at all; `show-annexes` has none either, the annexes being complete by definition.
- *[in force]* **`show-annexes`' examples are shown and not rendered, settled (v0.111).** Every page of a source is a page of its own, and a documentation page is one picture; tests/test_annex.py compiles the annexes on probes of its own, which is where the command is compiled (the example project's report does not include them: a dozen structure tests rest on its fixed tree). The same exemption `show-check` carries, for a reason of its own.
- *[in force]* **The fire-protection plan is the position plan's tab a second time, on one shared base, settled (v0.115).** The engineer's requirement, in those words: *«extrêmement similaire»*, built on the same code base. `PlanTab` was cut into `calc/tools/drawing_tab.py`'s `DrawingTab` and the two tabs implement it through hooks. Chosen over copying `plan_tab.py` into a `fire_tab.py`, which is what Gian's branch did and which would have left two tabs to keep alike by hand through every later change (v0.114 alone changed the plan tab's every pane). ⚠️ Accepted price: WRITERS wraps methods **by name on the classes it lists**, so a method moved into a base silently loses the read-only guard unless the base is listed — `DrawingTab` is, and a test holds every writer `PlanTab` reaches guarded; and `replace_source`, the undo helper of the plan and the static models, must never become a writer (the annex tab's own methods carry other names for the same reason).
- *[in force]* **The documents are shared and a page's turn turns both kinds, settled (v0.115).** One import serves both tabs; ticking a page makes a plan of the tab's kind; rotating, layers and archiving act on both (`calc/domains/plan_pages.py`); unticking archives, never deletes. Chosen over a second document list for the fire tab, which would have meant importing the architect's plan twice and a page turned in one tab and not in the other. ⚠️ The turn is **checked on every source before the first file is written**, so a refusal leaves a page and its drawings as they were.
- *[in force]* **A fire plan follows a position plan or has a frame of its own, settled (v0.115).** The engineer's two rounds: the background **both** — directly on a page of an imported document, or following an existing position plan, with an own crop allowed; a page ticked in the fire tab that carries **exactly one** position plan makes a plan that follows it, any other an own frame, and following can also be chosen by hand. Chosen over a fire plan that is always a position plan's overlay (it could not be drawn on a floor the position plans do not cover) and over one that always carries a frame (a change of scale or background in «Plan de positions» would not reach it, two sources for one thing). The own crop on a following plan is v0.75's pattern for a value that follows and may be written over. Accepted price: a calibration drawn on a followed page is refused — the scale is the position plan's — and the status line says where to make it.
- *[in force]* **Archiving a position plan that fire plans follow detaches them, settled (v0.115).** The question names the plans and `fire.detach` copies the frame they followed into them, so nothing is lost and nothing dangles. Chosen over refusing the archive (the engineer could not tidy a floor that has a fire plan) and over archiving the fire plans with it (a drawing he did not ask to lose).
- *[in force]* **Schema 2.0, and a 1.0 source is read as it is, settled (v0.115).** `fire-source` 2.0 adds `background.document` — the same pattern as plan-source 1.2 — and a 1.0 file needs no rename, so it is **upgraded in memory on reading and written as 2.0 only when the plan is saved**; `fire` export 2.0 adds `legend_lines`. Gian has real projects with 1.0 sources, which is why a reader that refused or rewrote them would not do (the line §3.3sexies draws between reading and migrating); his two example plans are the fixture. Chosen over a migration script, which writes a second file on no action of the engineer's.
- *[in force]* **Gian's set of elements exactly, no limits checked, no automatic rooms, settled (v0.115).** Surfaces of three kinds, walls by VKF fire-resistance class, doors (swing or sliding; EI 30, E 30 or none), the escape routes' length, the emergency exit, the width, a legend: `calc/norms/vkf.py` and its JSON are kept. **No check of a VKF limit** — an area, a length and a width are measured, never compared —, and `fire_zones.py` (numpy) stays dropped as it was on his own branch since 05.10.2026: a proposal of rooms that needs correcting on every plan costs more than the outlines it saves.
- *[in force]* **The tab is the base de projet's and the report's, not the convention's, settled (v0.115).** The engineer's second round: `MODE_TABS["bp"]` and `["rs"]` carry `tab_fire`. A convention d'utilisation is an agreement and shows no fire plan; the three documents' other plan tab, «Plan de positions», is in all three.
- *[in force]* **The fire plan prints in three ways, and on a sheet its legend both on the plan and apart, settled (v0.115).** `#show-fire-plan` and `fire-plan-ids()`, a **viewport** on an export sheet (`sheet-fire-viewport`) and a Structure-tab object (`doc-fire-plan`); on a sheet the legend is **both possible** — `legend: true` on the plan, and `sheet-fire-legend` as an object of its own, movable. Chosen over one of the two: a sheet drawn for a client wants the legend where it stands, a sheet laid out by hand wants it beside the plan. `plan-figure` was generalised (a record's own per-language figure, `legend: false`) rather than a second figure routine written, and a legend line carries its own wording, so a fire line and an STE line share one `plan-legend`. ⚠️ **A fire plan has its own sheet state and its own commands**, `sheet-fire-viewport` beside `sheet-viewport` and not an argument of it: a position plan and a fire plan may share an id, and one command cannot know which it was given.
- *[in force]* **The drawing engines work alike wherever they can, settled (v0.116), as rule 16 of §2.** The engineer, at the end of his message: the fire plan must get every change made in the position plan, «il faut que les deux choses fonctionnent de manière similaire», and **the reference document must say that the drawing engines work alike when possible**. So the position plan and the fire-protection plan stay **one engine** (§2 rule 16): a feature of one is a feature of the other unless it is the domain's — plan types, sections, the load symbols, the load transfer level and the list of the positions belong to the position plan alone, a fire plan being a floor's concept and listing no positions —, and snapping and tracking live once, in `PlanCanvas` and `calc/domains/plan_tracking.py`, the fire canvas supplying only its own points. Chosen over copying each change into the second tab, which is how two tabs come to differ by what somebody forgot, and over merging the two into one plan type, which would put a fire plan's walls and routes into every position plan's source. Accepted price: a change to `PlanCanvas`, `DrawingTab` or `document_view.py` is a change to both tabs and is tested on both, and a method moved into the base silently loses the read-only guard unless the base is listed in WRITERS (§10quater).
- *[in force]* **Several frames on a page are several plans, the first one made by the tick, settled (v0.116).** The engineer's answer to the first question: the tick under a page creates the first plan, whole page; every further frame drawn with the crop tool on that page **creates a new plan** (P02, P03…); unticking archives every plan of the page, asking if one holds elements. Chosen over one plan holding several crops, which would have broken «a plan is a frame» — its crop, its scale, its storey, its name and its elements being what a position plan *is*. Accepted price: a page with five situations is five plans in the list, which is what the list and its folders are for. ⚠️ **The first frame crops the plan the tick made**, where a first frame making a second plan would have left an uncropped plan of the whole page beside it for nothing; a frame drawn on an unticked page makes its plan **and ticks the page**.
- *[in force]* **A document belongs to the tab that imported it, settled (v0.116).** The engineer, twice: «le plan de position ne doit absolument pas être mélangé avec le plan d'incendie et vice versa», and not in the left pane either. `plan-document` 1.1 carries an `owner`, `plan` or `fire`, written at import; a document **without one** is the fire tab's if the only plans drawn on it are fire plans of an own frame and the position tab's otherwise — the engineer's rule, «existing documents go to the position plan tab, except those that carry only fire plans» —, derived at reading by `plan_pages.document_owner` and **never written into a project's files on that account**. A fire plan may still **follow** a position plan, chosen in its background card, and turning a page then still turns the fire plan that follows; the muted frames of the other kind stay in the document view. Chosen over two document lists in each tab with a switch, which is the mixing again. Accepted price: importing the architect's plan for both kinds means importing it twice, once in each tab, and two documents are two files in `Woodcore-Datei/plans/documents/`.
- *[in force]* **A layer is switched in place and its consequences follow later, settled (v0.116).** The engineer found a layer very slow to switch and asked for «quasi instantané». Measured on his own 18-page, 74-layer set: a toggle was a full pypdf rewrite (1 to 1.6 s), a re-export of every plan on the document (about 2.6 s a page, stripping the hidden content) and a render of the canvas (1 to 1.6 s), **all on the main thread**. Now the PDF is updated incrementally (a few kB appended, both cross-reference forms, pixel-identical to the rewrite), the visible pages are rendered first at screen resolution with the old image kept, a combination seen before is shown from the cache with no render at all (about 0.2 s), and the export of the plans waits 1.2 s after the last toggle and runs in a thread, **finished before any other export, before leaving a plan and before the window closes**. Chosen over a faster rewrite, which stays a rewrite, and over dropping the export on each toggle, which would print a layer the window showed hidden. Accepted price, two of them: the window shows the new state in under a second and not instantly; and a window killed within 1.2 s of a toggle leaves the exported plans stale until the next export (§14). `flatten_page` is cached on disk by the file's hash (`FLATTEN_VERSION`), so the same combination is flattened once.
- *[in force]* **Tracking in the plan canvases dwells 350 ms, where the static models' tab dwells 220, settled (v0.116).** The engineer asked for the static models' behaviour in both plan canvases. A plan is drawn over a dense architect's sheet, where a pointer moving across the page rests for 220 ms over some corner or axis of the drawing in the ordinary course of aiming at something else, and every such rest would have acquired a point nobody meant; 350 ms is the measure at which acquiring is deliberate. Chosen over the same 220, which the engineer would have met as rays appearing and vanishing as he moved, and over a setting, which is one more thing to find. **Shift is unchanged** — H/V/45° from the last point, no tracking —, because an engineer who holds Shift has said which direction he wants; and with both snap buttons off nothing tracks, the buttons being the engineer's own switch. The arithmetic is a pure module (`plan_tracking.py`), as `statics.py`'s own, so it is tested without a window.
- *[in force]* **The crop and the calibration are cards of the right pane, and the right pane follows the selection, settled (v0.116).** The engineer: neither tool in the bar, and the right pane «davantage dépendante de ce que l'on sélectionne» — with nothing selected the plan, its background, the crop **above** the scale and the symbols' size, with an entry selected its own card and the symbols' size and nothing else. Chosen over keeping the crop in the bar and mirroring it in the pane, which is two ways to do one thing, and over hiding the plan's cards behind an expander, which leaves the selection's own card pushed down the pane. «Dessiner le cadre» and «Calibrer…» start the tool for one drag or two clicks and give the selection back. Accepted price: the two actions are one click further from the pointer, and the bar says nothing of them — the cards' tooltips do.
- *[in force]* **A plan has a type, and a load-path plan is a plan with six more symbols, settled (v0.116).** The engineer: «coupe en plan» shows the storey and the numbering character; «coupe en élévation» hides them and changes the tools to the storey line, the storey box and the load transfer level; «plan de descente de charges» takes every usual tool and is only another type, recognisable, with its storey and numbering character. The type is **`plan_type`, additive**, `plan-source` 1.3, **derived from the older `role` and `section_plan` where absent and refused where it disagrees**, so no source written before this version moved; the export stays 1.3 and writes it only where it is stated. Chosen over a new schema version that rewrites every plan, and over three source kinds in three folders, which would have made a plan that changes its mind a move between folders. ⚠️ Switching the type **removes what the new type does not carry**, in one step of «Annuler» and with the number said, over keeping an element nobody can see or draw: an elevation section with a storey line whose plan is turned back into a floor plan would carry a line in no tool's reach.
- *[in force]* **A load symbol is not a position, settled (v0.116, the implementer's decision).** The six STE load symbols are numbered `Pxxx` and `Lxxx` like positions, and the first design numbered them from `Positionen.xlsx`'s own list. They are **left out of every place a position is looked up** — the labels, the warnings about a number the sheet lacks, the rows the window copies for Excel, «Ajouter aux positions» — and exist on a load-path plan only: STE draws them as the loads a position takes **from above**, which is a load, and §8 says a position carries none, at any layer. They carry **no magnitude**, for the same reason, and the numbering is the plan's own (`plan.next_load_label`: `P` or `L`, the storey's character, two digits). Accepted price: a load's number is free text the engineer must keep unique himself, and the window's element list shows the load types in German (§14).
- *[in force]* **The load transfer level is a collection of a section, and a storey box carries STE's own designation, settled (v0.116).** The engineer: a red line with a vertical arrow pointing down at each end, a movable label «Niveau de reprise des charges (x0xx)» built from a chosen storey's numbering character; and, at the middle of a storey box, **x1xx** as STE has it — the numbering character gives the designation —, **not** the storey's name at the box's top left. `load_levels` is section-only and has no style factors, being a figure of the section and not an element of a drawing; the designation is «x» + the character + «xx» for a box and «x» + the character + «xx» in the label (**x0xx** for the ground floor) read from the STE pp. 12 and 14–15 renders. Because the label carries words, a section that has a level exports **its figure once per language**, the rule §4bis gives to every figure that writes the engine's words. Chosen over setting the words in Typst, which would make the label of a figure its own text. Accepted price: three SVG files for such a section.
- *[in force]* **Another plan shown over the open one is a display aid kept in the source, never printed, settled (v0.116).** The engineer's answer: everything is memorised in the open plan's source — the plan shown, its offset (a translation, both plans being calibrated), the opacity, the colour mode — and **it is never printed**. The field is named `superposition` and not `overlay`, because `data/plans.json`'s records already have an `overlay` (the symbols' SVG), and it is additive, `schema_version` unchanged, in the plan source and the fire source alike. Chosen over a window-only state, which the next opening forgets and which would make the alignment, two clicks of the engineer's own work, a thing to repeat. **The alignment is two clicks, a point of the plan shown then the matching point of the open plan**, and sets a translation: with both calibrated the scales are known, and a rotation would be a third thing to state. ⚠️ **Nothing of the plan shown can be selected**, so that a drag aimed at the open plan never moves the other one; its points are snap targets behind «Accrocher aux éléments», which is what the alignment and the drawing over it both want. How the colours read is the engineer's: a colour alone tints the plan, **grey alone leaves its elements their colours and the switch greys them too**. Accepted price: translation and scale only — a plan drawn rotated against the other does not overlay (§14) — and the plan shown is rendered at a fixed 2000 px.
- *[in force]* **A list of the positions is one table in three forms, settled (v0.116).** The engineer's answers: the positions shown by default are **those drawn on the plans of the sheet** (or of the grouped PDF), switchable to all the positions of the project or to one storey; **a movable sheet object** (added from the Options module), **a button «Créer une liste des positions»** in the export list that makes an A4 portrait sheet holding the list alone, and **a Typst command**, `#show-position-list`, for the report and the base de projet. The columns are **our own**, not STE's: ID, type, material, strength class, section type, dimension, units, moisture class, description; the table is **our `render-table`**, three rules and no grid, not STE's; and **the ID cell is coloured by the position's type**, the colour of the symbol the plan draws for that letter. One implementation (`lib/positions.typ`), so the three forms cannot come to list different things; the colours are `plan_svg`'s restated in Typst and **held equal by a test**, a second place the plan's colours live in being worth a guard and not worth a JSON file the two would read. ⚠️ **The section type is printed as a word** (`rec`, `cir`, `pla`, `clt`) where it is a code of ours and as its code where it is a steel family, SZS C5's own designation being what the engineer reads.
- *[in force]* **A bundle is a folder of the export list that makes one more PDF, settled (v0.116).** The engineer: «le dossier … c'est pas vraiment un dossier concret», to be called a PDF creation but looking like a folder, whose sheets make **one PDF with several pages**; his answer to the question that followed — each sheet keeps its own PDF **and** the bundle makes one extra, pages in the bundle's order, reorderable by drag, **named after the bundle**. Stored in `Plans/bundles.json` (`woodcore-sheet-bundles` 1.0), the sheets' `.typ` files not knowing which bundle they are in, so a sheet moved between bundles is one write. ⚠️ **Merged by pypdf from the sheets' own PDFs**, each compiled with its own `.final()` states, over compiling the bundle as one Typst document, which would have asked every sheet to be written for a shared state; pypdf is already pinned and already merges a plan's page. A sheet's id and a bundle's name never collide, a bundle that cannot be read is **never overwritten**, deleting a bundle moves its PDF to `Plans/archives` and archiving a sheet takes it out of its bundles. Accepted price: a big bundle is exported **sequentially**, one sheet after another, in a thread (§14).
- *[in force]* **A sheet is of one kind, settled (v0.116).** Position plans and fire plans never on one sheet, the engineer's rule on the sheets as on the lists; `sheet(kind: "position"|"fire")`, a sheet written before this version **read off its objects** (position by default, so no existing sheet changes), and the editor refusing an object of the other kind. The tab a sheet is made in decides its kind (`DrawingTab.SHEET_KIND`) and the kind combo of the first design is gone. Chosen over a sheet that may carry both with the legend of each, which is the mixing the engineer excluded.
- *[in force]* **A plan viewport has a title above it, one line, with a switch, settled (v0.116).** «Nom — description, étage (éch. 1:n)», the way STE heads each plan of a plan de positions, read from the plan's name, description and storey and from the scale the viewport is drawn at (`sheet.scale_short`); a switch in the viewport's module hides it, `title: none` in the call. The name reaches the sheet through `data/plans.json`'s new, additive `name`; the `test_plan_style` frozen hash changed by that field and nothing else. Chosen over a title taken from the plan's id, which says nothing, and over a title object of its own, which the engineer would then have to keep next to the plan it names.
- *[in force]* **The sheet editor's right pane follows the selection like the plan tabs', settled (v0.116).** The engineer's long dictation about the sheets reduces to one rule: **with nothing selected the sheet and its options; with an object selected only that object's module**. Options holds a switch for the title block and one for the legend — «aucune option pour le cartouche, c'est juste un bouton afficher ou non» — and, one under the other, **what can be added** (a plan view, a section, a text, the list of the positions), **each asking which**; adding selects nothing, so no module of the thing just added shows. The text box's choice among «title, remark, section, viewport, legend, title block» is gone, and the text's module is called «Titre» in French. A selected plan view's «position en évidence» is a **drop-down of the positions on the plan**, and the module of a title block, a legend or a section has only its own options. Chosen over keeping the sheet's modules beside the object's, which is what the engineer was looking at when he asked.
- *[in force]* **A position's type is stored once and shown in the language it is read in, settled (v0.116).** The engineer, who found the Positions tab's types all in German: translated into German, French or English **according to the language the document is written in**, while **the id keeps its STE letter** — a French column is `S001`, not `P001`. The type is stored as its STE key, in German, as it always was, **so no file changed**; the Positions tab shows it in the **project's** language (`config.typ`'s `doc-lang`, else the report's, else the window's), and the report, the lists and the legends in the language of the **compiled document** — two languages for one value because a window is read by its author and a report by its reader. A type typed freely, which is no STE key, **stays as typed** and a translated name typed back is read as its key. Chosen over storing the translated word, which would make a project change its data when its language changes. Accepted price, and a limit: a free type that equals a term of the glossary is translated too (§14).
- *[in force]* **Member checks are saved objects resolved by the export, named by ID in the report, and calculated in Typst alone, settled (v0.117) — the «Typst alone» was reversed for the window's results the same day, see the next bullet.** The engineer's request of 07.10.2026 and his answers. **Two navigation groups, one navigation entry per check** — «Sécurité structurale» (Traction, Compression, Flexion = bending with a normal force, Effort tranchant = shear and compression perpendicular to the grain, Torsion, Flambage, Déversement) and «Aptitude au service» (Déformations incl. fatigue, Vibrations) — **in the report alone**, chosen over one «Vérifications» tab with a combo, which would have hidden the structure the engineer thinks in. **A check is a saved object** (`Woodcore-Datei/checks/<id>.json`), not a stateless call builder: chosen over a builder (the commands tab's, the plan's «Commande Typst») because a check wants a name, a position it follows and a place in a folder, and because the same check printed in two sections should be one thing. **Three panes** — list | form | result —, the connectors' arrangement, with a live compiled preview of the draft. **The report knows a check by its ID, through the export** (one source of truth, `data/checks.json`), chosen over a self-contained call that copies every value into the section: a copied call is a second place for a strength class that the Positions tab already holds (§2 rule 1), and ages the day the position changes — the connectors' own argument (§13 v0.68, «Importer Excel is all it takes»). Accepted price: **the call only works once the check is saved and exported**, which the tab says under it. **The values the engineer may state without a position** are the strength class, the section (b/h or d), the moisture class and the impact factor η_t; **N is typed in kN only** in the form (the schema accepts N and MN). **Copy only, no insertion into a section** — the «Formules» tab's «Insérer» was not repeated. **One command for every future kind**, `#show-check-member`, rather than a command per kind; its options are the **check's level, the member line, the title and the unit** (the description too). **The calculation is in Typst only** — no Python arithmetic, the export resolves and `#verify-tension` calculates — so the page is the one a hand-written report prints and §1's untraced condition is unchanged. ⚠️ Reversed the same day for the window's own results (next bullet); the report's page is still Typst's alone. **Implementer decisions, not asked of the engineer:** the resolved values are **written out explicitly** to `#member` (so Typst computes with exactly what Python resolved); **the member's `var-domain` is the check's id**, so two checks on one position never collide and the published domain is `"<id>-verify-tension"`; **C16 at α = 0 is not calculable** (Tabelle 8 note 2), exported with the reason rather than refused by the report only; **a stated grade SIA 265 lacks stops the export, a position's unknown grade only makes the check not calculable** (a defect in a file Woodcore wrote against a gap in the project, the v0.33 rule); `draft_typst`'s `member_level=None` means no member line while `call_show_check_member` writes `member-level: none` for `False` (the tab maps both, §14); `TensionTab` has no `on_mode`; and `#verify-tension` sets N and α on one `var-line`. Accepted price: eight modules in the navigation that are placeholders, which the engineer asked for so that the structure of the chapter is visible before it is built.
- *[in force]* **The release of 0.116.0 was decided with the request, settled (v0.116).** The engineer's last point: commit, then `VERSION` 0.116.0, the hook builds the installer, `publish.py` and the push **«sans demander de nouveau»**. It is the one place where §13's rule that each publication is asked for (the GitLab token bullet) was answered in advance, and it is **this release's and no other's**: the next version asks again.
- *[in force]* **The window's results are an instant Python calculation, REVERSING «Typst alone», settled (v0.117, 07.10.2026, the same day).** The engineer, on trying the first build: «Pour la traction et la compression, finalement je souhaite avoir un calcul python instantané … pas de latence». A compile of the draft at every edit — half a second at best, several when the engine is cold — made the form feel slow. Chosen over making the compile faster (a floor of a few hundred milliseconds remains) and over a compile in the background with the last result kept (a result that lags what is typed is worse than a short wait). **`member_check.compute(record)`** computes the check in Python, reading every value of SIA 265 through `calc/norms`, and the right pane shows its results, its clause and its verdict at once; **the Typst preview stays, in a collapsed section that compiles only while open**, so the page the report will print can still be looked at. ⚠️ **The price, stated:** the arithmetic of 4.2.1 and 4.2.2 exists **twice**, in `lib/timber.typ` and in `member_check.compute` — the duplication §2 rule 1 forbids, accepted knowingly —, and it is **held equal by a test**: `tests/test_member_check.py` compiles 17 records at level 0 and reads Typst's registry back, every row equal within 1e-6, so that neither can change without the other. What the report prints is untouched: Typst's calculation, §1's untraced condition, no Python in the PDF. Each new kind of check now costs both implementations and a case in that test. **Compression decisions of the same day (the engineer's):** one angle α from 0 to 90° as for the tension; **at 0 the section alone with no buckling**, buckling being the future «Flambage» module — implemented as `stability: false` on `#verify-compression`, the default unchanged so no report written before moves; the resistance f_c,90,d **among five entries** of Tabelle 8/9 (Général, avec avant-bois, appui d'extrémité, the last two avec or sans conséquence — the bracketed value), the bracketed ones greyed for a grade that has none; the contact width following the section's width unless written over. Implementer's: the source schema stays 1.0 (the four fields are additive); a kind of check is a subclass of `MemberCheckTab`; the verdict has a theme token of its own, `ok`.

- *[in force]* **A saved bending is the section alone, its N signed, and (21)/(22) apply at an angle with the strengths at that angle, settled (v0.117, 08.10.2026).** The engineer: «la vérification à la flexion d'après la SIA265 §4.2.3 (sans déversement) … équation 21 si effort normal positif (traction), équation 22 si effort normal négatif (compression)», and to the questions of the spec (`docs/requirements/completed/2026-10-08-verification-flexion.md`): **«sans déversement» is an argument** `stability: false` of `#verify-bending` and `#verify-bending-normal`, as `#verify-compression` had since the day before — rather than leaving `a:` out, which prints «Kippen nicht nachgewiesen», and `l-k-y: 0`, which prints (37) with k_c = 1 and mixes the stability into η; **N is signed for a bending alone**, tension +, compression −, 0 for none — the Typst commands' convention — where a tension's and a compression's N stay magnitudes, so `resolve`, `validate_checks_business` and both schemas read N by `kind`; **at an angle the moment about z is closed**, (17) to (20) reading M_y alone, **and a normal force is allowed**: «Les équations 9 et 13 permettent de calculer le f_c ou f_t nécessaire pour la formule 21 ou 22». SIA 265 4.2.4 writes (21) and (22) along the grain only, so this is the engineer's reading and the page says so in a note: f_t,α,d of (9) or f_c,α,d of (13) (f_c,90,d «generell»), and k_mt,α·f_m,d or k_mc,α·f_m,d of (19), (20), **on the side where the normal stress adds to the bending stress**; the other side is checked by (17) or (18) alone, the normal force's relief not counted, and η is the larger (his choice among three: the interaction alone, or on both sides, were the others). The price: one more case Python and Typst each compute, held equal by `tests/test_member_check.py`, and a reading of the norm that a checker may not share — which is why the page states it rather than presenting it as the norm's. In passing, `verify-bending`'s side utilisations became `eta_m_t` and `eta_m_c`: stored as `eta_t` and `eta_c`, they overwrote in the check's domain the impact factor η_t it copies from its member.

- *[in force]* **A tab shows one task at a time, chosen in the top bar, settled (v0.117).** The engineer, 07.10.2026: «il y a pour l'instant trop d'informations dans les onglets. On se perd un peu». A switch left of the languages chooses a tab's view (`calc/tools/views.py`): the plans **Import · Dessin · Typst · Export** (his four positions, T11, correcting the «dessin, texte, document» of the sentence before), the checks **Calcul · Affichage** (T19), and two views likewise for the positions, the journal, the loads, the static models, the schemas, the connectors, the polar moments and the variables. *Affichage* is how the report prints it: the page compiled in the middle, at the right the command chosen among those that can show the object, its options and the call to copy at the end (T21, T24). Chosen over leaving the panes as they were with folding sections, which is what had made them crowded. **A segmented switch**, the language switch's own look, over a sliding control with notches, which reads four labels badly in three languages (the engineer's answer). **One mechanism**: a tab declares `VIEWS` and answers `set_view`, one `DisplayView` serves every *Affichage* (rule 16). **Prices accepted**: in a check, the Python result is no longer beside the call — **this reverses the order of the same day** (results, the call, the options and the preview folded, entry above), the reason of that order kept: nothing compiles while the engineer types in *Calcul*; and every panel is written by hand (below).
- *[in force]* **Every command of an *Affichage* has a panel written by hand, its defaults the signature's, settled (v0.117).** The engineer's answer to Q-001 of the spec: all the panels, by hand, now — over panels generated from the `///` blocks (fewer lines, a generated label less clear) and over a version without panels (the options postponed). A panel is a declaration (`display_options.SPECS`) drawn by one widget, never a widget per command (rule 16); it holds **no default** — a control starts at the signature's (`build_reference.signature_defaults`) and a call writes only what differs from it (`build_reference.call_command`, through which every `call_*` now goes). Two defaults had already drifted when this was built: `show-schema`'s `aids` (the window offered `aids: true`, the default, and could not write `false`) and a caption or a journal title written raw. Every named argument is offered or dismissed with a reason, the test holding it against the signature. A call is otherwise what it was, but **an option equal to its default is no longer written**, whatever the option (`show-journal(form: "table")`, `show-plan(zoom: 1)`, `show-check-member(member-level: 1)`, `show-schema(aids: true)` are their bare calls), which prints the same. In the variables tab a domain of the export offers `show-calc` and not `find`, which reads a domain of the report: a value of the export is listed by `show-calc` itself (the spec's D18, put to the engineer). **Three panels stay widgets of their own** (`display_options.OWN_PANELS`): `show-plan` and `show-fire-plan`, whose positions and scale bar depend on the plan's export record, and `show-check-member`, whose options also set the verdict's unit and the draft's page — a reason written down, as rule 16 asks; their values are held against the signature all the same. **`eq` is not offered for a variable** (the engineer, 07.10.2026: put off): the export carries a formula per calculation step, written in symbols with its left side, and turning it into the registry's keys is a converter of its own (docs/open-questions.md).
- *[in force]* **Leaving a tab, a view, a document or the window saves without a question, settled (v0.117), extending v0.114's rule for the plans to every editor.** The engineer, 07.10.2026: «il doit y avoir un enregistrement automatique sans demander la confirmation» — and, asked whether leaving Woodcore for another application counts, yes (the files are then what VS Code and the export read). One module, `calc/tools/autosave.py`, three contexts: the draft stays in memory (a tab, a view, the focus) — saved, or the dot kept and the reason in the status line, nothing asked; the draft is replaced (another object, «Nouveau», another document) or the window closes — saved, or the tab's own question. **What is never saved without the engineer**, by the architecture review's two blocking findings: a rename (an annex's PDF follows its ID; an ID half typed would rename files of the project), a draft never saved while the engineer only looks elsewhere — it is saved when it is replaced or the window closes —, and, on the focus alone, an object whose ID holds the cursor; a new draft never touched is let go of; the Python tab waits for the tab or the script to be left (a script half typed would run at the next export). Also decided with it, the review's defaults: an export saves every draft first; the heights of the plan tabs' left boxes and the fold of the pane last the session, as the splitters do, the view alone being kept in the user's settings. Chosen over a save on every keystroke, which would write half-typed IDs, and over asking as before, which is what the engineer asked to stop.
- *[in force]* **Woodcore starts on a screen of its own before anything heavy is imported, and loads at the start what the work will need, settled (v0.117).** The engineer, 07.10.2026: «Le fait que le lancement de Woodcore puisse prendre un certain temps ne me dérange pas. Ce qui me dérange est que Windows donne actuellement l'impression que Woodcore est bloqué», and, asked how far the start goes (Q-004 of spec `2026-10-07-performance-erreurs-plans-legendes`), **what is sure to serve** — the modules, the norms, every tab, the project's data and documents — on the start screen, the rest in the background with the activity chip. Chosen over everything before the window (10 to 60 s more, for tabs never opened) and over the minimum (every tab's first opening a wait). `calc/tools/woodcore_start.py`; the measurements, before and after, in `docs/architecture/performance.md`.
- *[in force]* **Every error, warning and information goes through one store and is shown at the top right, never in a message box; a decision that waits for its answer stays a dialog, settled (v0.117).** The engineer, 07.10.2026: «Les erreurs ne doivent plus être présentées sous forme de fenêtres popup dispersées dans l'application», and his answer to Q-002: the questions (Yes/No, a choice) stay dialogs, their nature being to wait for an answer. A refusal inside a modal dialog is said in a band of that dialog, the panel being behind it. The store is Qt-free (`calc/notifications.py`), so the domain and the command line report the same way; named `notifications` because `packaging/notices.py` is the third-party notices. Held by `tests/test_no_message_boxes.py`, a box added tomorrow named by its file and line, a decision marked `# dialog-ok: <why>`.
- *[in force]* **A notice is translated when it is shown, its technical text never, settled (v0.117).** «Il faut distinguer : Message utilisateur, traduit … Informations techniques : conserver les informations techniques originales». A notice carries its code and arguments (the form of `engine.Note`) and, for a converted box, the key of its sentence: the language switched, the panel reads it again in the new one. The same trouble reported again counts once more — its code, file, line, text and technical text alike.
- *[in force]* **The PDF views are not tiled; they render less, off the window's thread, in a better order, settled (v0.117), AMENDING D4 of the spec.** D4 (Q-001: QtPdf, no new dependency) planned a grid of bounded tiles. Measured on the engineer's architect's PDF, one PDFium render holds QtPdf's lock 0.2-0.5 s whatever its size (a 512-pixel tile 216 ms, a 2400 x 1500 clip 227 ms): a grid would pay that per tile. What was slow was elsewhere and is what changed: the export sheet rendered whole in its paint, the coarse renders of the pages round the view before the sharp one in view, and Qt's 10 MB pixmap cache, too small to keep a plan's symbols drawn once.
- *[in force]* **A column is a square turned by an angle; the export document has an id of its own that names its PDF; the position's type follows the window's language, settled (v0.117).** The engineer's answers at the approval: «un carré comme aujourd'hui, qu'il est possible de tourner (e.g. 30°)» — the plan's source goes to 1.4, since 1.3 refused an angle on a column; an id distinct from the name (bundles.json 1.1, the PDF Plans/<id>.pdf, the first plan's id where none is given — a 1.0 bundle's PDF is renamed after it at its next export); the window's language for the right pane's position list, the material staying the engineer's word.
- *[in force]* **The panes' sizes are kept by tab, by view and by the set of screens plugged in, AMENDING the v0.117 rule above that they last the session, settled (v0.117, 08.10.2026).** The engineer: «une sauvegarde d'affichage pour l'écran externe lorsqu'il est utilisé et une sauvegarde pour mon pc portable lorsqu'il est utilisé sans l'écran externe». In the user's settings (`layouts`), never the project; a set of screens by its makers, models, serial numbers and scalings, never `\\.\DISPLAYn`; one never seen takes the last used. The plan tabs' left boxes' heights still last the session.
- *[in force]* **The captions read «Tableau 1 — titre» and «Figure 1 — titre» in every document, settled (v0.117).** The engineer, 07.10.2026: «Tableau [numéro] --- (long tiret) [titre du tableau]». One rule, `caption-rules`, applied by `woodcore-document` — so a project's `config.typ` cannot set another separator or another word —, the words of the kinds the glossary's in the report's language. The windows' previews of the commands keep Typst's own (their preamble has no `woodcore-document`); see open-questions.
- *[in force]* **Retrait de RFEM et des lignes à mots-clés: the RFEM import and v0.100's «Formules» tab are retired whole and kept in `archive/`, settled (v0.117, 08.10.2026).** The engineer: «Tu peux supprimer les onglets RFEM ainsi que Formule. Mets-les dans un dossier archive, mais retire définitivement le code de Woodcore. Je ne les utiliserai plus, mais laisse-les dans les archives au cas où on en a besoin.» Asked how far (spec 2026-10-08-onglet-formule-domaines, Q-001), he chose **everything** RFEM rather than the tab alone: the tab, the reader of the table export (`calc/rfem_import/`), the `rfem` domain, `#rfem-max`, `#show-rfem-forces`, `#show-rfem-combination` and the three `rfem-*` data functions, `woodcore-cli import`, the static models generated at every export, and — the chain only RFEM fed (D-14) — `show-static-model(diagram:)` and its option in *Affichage*. **Where** (Q-002): `Woodcore/archive/<feature>/`, in the repository, every file at the path it had and moved with `git mv`, a README per feature listing what the removal changed in the files that stayed; out of the test run (`tests/run_tests.py` globs `tests/` alone, `archive/conftest.py` stops a bare `pytest`), of the installer (`woodcore.spec` names its folders) and of the site (`paths-ignore`). **A project that still names RFEM opens**: `gui.rfem_models` and `layout.rfem_export` are read, ignored and said at **every** export (`project.RETIRED_KEYS`, `Project.retired_keys`), and Woodcore never rewrites the file to take them out — the file is the engineer's; the schemas keep both keys, described as retired, so an older file goes on validating. The export removes `data/rfem.json` and `data/rfem/`, which nothing writes any more (`export_all._sweep_retired`): a file no writer sweeps is a copy that ages. ⚠️ **Accepted prices.** Woodcore no longer reads an RFEM export, and a section calling `#rfem-*` stops with Typst's «unknown variable» (neither Musterprojekt nor the example calls one). A layout in three documents states **six** keys and not seven, and a project made since 0.117 is refused by an older Woodcore naming `layout.rfem_export` — one way, without a new `schema_version`. The migration no longer moves `RFEM-Export/` up nor makes `gui.rfem_models` follow it, and its proof compares the export **byte for byte**: the one loosening it allowed, an RFEM path that moved (3 491 in Musterprojekt), went with the import. Musterprojekt's `data/statics.json` loses the 19 models RFEM generated at its next export, so `test_the_export_is_reproducible` is red once. A future FEM import would have to rebuild the diagram chain.
- *[in force]* **Every calculation written in the «Formule» tab is a domain of its own, one `.typ` file, printed by `#show-formula` at a level the domain sets and a command may override, settled (v0.117, 08.10.2026).** The engineer: «à chaque fois qu'il y ait des calculs, ils soient compris dans un domaine afin de pouvoir appeler ces domaines à d'autres endroits du rapport». **One file per domain, `<Typst root>/Formules/<id>.typ`** (his answer to Q-003 and, the review having shown that Typst reads nothing outside its root, to its follow-up): the file is the source of truth (principle 15) -- the tab writes it at every block validated, VS Code edits it and the tab reads it again --, and **no copy**: `#show-formula(name)` finds it through `data/formulas.json`, an index Woodcore writes at every save and export, and `include`s it from the root. A `Woodcore-Datei/formulas/` copied into `_woodcore/data/` at the export was the alternative, and it would have made a file edited in VS Code print only after «Import données» and the copy a trap. **Price**: the migration of a report alone moves `Formules/` up with the shared files; a name is one domain's, two files bearing it stop the export. **The file's format** (review ARCH-RISK-102): one tag `// woodcore:object formula-domain domain` around the `#var-domain(…)[…]`, read by `structure.pair_tags` and by `typst_objects`'s parser with a `positional` mode the ten commands of a document do not use; `canonical`, `hand_edited` and `unreadable` mean what they mean for a document's objects. **The level** (D-02, D-12): the domain's level is the default of its commands -- 1 hides the variables (`var`, `var-unit` at 0), 2 prints everything at 1, 3 everything at 3 -- and a command whose call states `level:` keeps it (his answer: «Chaque commande peut écraser ce level si l'argument est indiqué»). It is a state `formula-block` read in `line-level`, the one place the five commands ask their level: no signature changed. **The instant result is computed twice, REVERSING for this tab the v0.100 refusal of a second algebra** (his answer to Q-004, «Python instantané»; in the line of the member checks' own reversal above): `calc/units.py` is lib/formula.typ's unit algebra in Python, the units themselves written once in `lib/units.json`, which both read, and `tests/test_units.py` holds the two equal on 28 formulas and in what they refuse. The v0.100 reason -- one algebra cannot drift -- is answered by the one table and the test, not by having one implementation. **Variables taken from elsewhere** (his answer: «il doit être possible d'importer des variables. On switch alors dans l'onglet variables avec tous les autres onglets indisponibles»): «Importer…» opens the «Variables» tab in a choosing mode with the navigation off, and writes `#var(key, var-of(…))` or `#var(key, domain: …)`. ⚠️ **A key that reads as a unit takes `_1`** (`m` → `m_1`, `l` → `l_1`, the symbol shown unchanged): lib/formula.typ substitutes a whole word before it reads a literal's unit, so a variable `m` would be read inside `(3 "m")` further down and the formula could not be computed -- a defect of the engine's substitution, left as it is (the commands were out of this change's scope) and noted in docs/open-questions.md.

- *[in force]* **A number is one object on a plan, and a position deleted takes the numbers after it down everywhere, REVERSING for the plans v0.44's shared numbers, v0.45's «only the sheet's positions are drawn» and `propose_id`'s «a number deleted is never taken again», settled (issue #9, 08.10.2026).** The engineer: «chaque nouvel objet dessiné augmente le numéro de 1 … quand une position est supprimée, renuméroter en cascade … reportée partout : remplacement dans tous les documents Typst du projet», and his answers (spec `docs/requirements/completed/2026-10-08-plan-positions-cotes-numerotation-sections.md`, Q-004, Q-006 to Q-009): **drawing makes the position**, written by «Enregistrer» with the plan and not at the click (nothing to undo in positions.json); **a position still cited is refused**, unless «Copier les valeurs» keeps it as `<id>-old` (`positions-archive.json`) with its citations rewritten and an orange note wherever it is called; **every `.typ` of the documents and of `Plans/`** is rewritten, each occurrence listed and uncheckable. ⚠️ **Accepted prices**: a renumbering is no step of «Annuler» (a copy in the archives undoes it); a cascade rewrites the engineer's prose too (the list lets him refuse each); a position exists in the Positions tab only once its plan is saved; the schemas keep their shared numbers (v0.73, a piece cut). **Dimensions on a plan are a collection of the source, points and not references (`plan-source` 1.5), on a geometry shared with the static models and the schemas** (`calc/domains/dimension.py`, Q-010: «noyau commun», the same tool symbols in the three tabs); radius and diameter left out (a plan has no circle, Q-011); the ruler measures and keeps nothing (Q-002); the fire plan has the ruler and not the dimensions (Q-013). **The quantities are timber's volumes alone** (Q-005: a steel profile's area is in SZS C5 and in no file), by material and section with a total per material (Q-014), printed by two commands with a scope (Q-015); `lengths:` is a boolean as `axes:`, a sheet hiding what the plan draws.

- *[in force]* **A domain of the «Formules» tab is printed by `#include "/Formules/<id>.typ"`, its id is the file's name, and its name is the `title:` of `#var-domain`, REVERSING v0.117's `#show-formula(name, level:)`, its index by name and the domain's own level, settled (issue #15, 08.10.2026).** The engineer: «Remplacer `#show-formula` par `#include "/Formules/<id>.typ"`, puisque l'id du domaine est le nom de son fichier. Le domaine s'imprime alors au niveau du rapport : le `level:` propre au domaine … et le refus qui liste les domaines existants disparaissent», and «L'id du domaine est le nom de son fichier … pour qu'un renommage du domaine ne casse pas les appels»; asked, «Tout par l'id» -- `#var-domain("<id>")` and `#val(key, domain: "<id>")` --, the name an argument of `var-domain` (`title:`) and printed as a heading. **What it reverses**: the v0.117 entry above in three parts (printed by `#show-formula` at a level the domain sets; found through `data/formulas.json` by its name; the index keyed by name) and the completed spec's D-02, D-09, D-11, D-12 and D-15. **What it costs**: a whole domain can no longer be printed at one level in one stroke -- each command keeps its own `level:` or follows the report; `var-domain`, a command of every document, gained an argument (`title:`, optional, nothing printed without it); an id changed breaks the `#include` and `#val(domain:)` already written, which Woodcore lists and does not rewrite (his answer); `data/formulas.json` stays (2.0, by id) only for the «Variables» tab and the export's checks. `formula-block`, `block-level`, `line-level(kind:)` and their Python mirror went with `#show-formula` (spec docs/requirements/completed/2026-10-08-onglet-formules-editeur-dossiers-include.md, D-01, D-06, D-08, D-10, D-11).
- *[in force]* **The order of every list of the window is the engineer's, kept in `Woodcore-Datei/lists.json`, and the «Formules» tab's folders with it -- the one exception to «a folder is a field», settled (issue #15, 08.10.2026).** The engineer: «Réorganiser l'ordre des domaines par glisser-déposer ; l'ordre est gardé par Woodcore dans un fichier du projet, le même mécanisme pour tous les onglets du même noyau», and «Un seul noyau pour tous les onglets qui ont cet arbre». **One kernel**: `grouped_list.GroupedList.attach_order` and `calc/list_order.py`, attached by every editor tab, none copying it; a drop writes the order, then `moved` only for an entry whose folder changes, `reordered` otherwise -- an order rewrites no source and exports nothing. **Where**: asked, he chose the sources' folder (`Woodcore-Datei/`) over `_woodcore/`, which an export may rebuild. **The window's alone**: asked, «fenêtre seulement» -- the report prints by id, the file is no export input, so a malformed one is a warning and a refused drop, never a stopped export. **The folders of the «Formules» tab** are kept there too, by his choice («Fichier d'ordre»): a domain is a `.typ` that has no field for a folder, and a hidden group line would make the file the engineer edits diverge; every other tab keeps its folder in its source's `group` field (v0.69's decision stands for them), and `lists.json` refuses a `groups` for a tab not marked (`list_order.GROUPED_TABS`) -- one folder, one place. **What it costs**: a `.typ` copied into another project arrives at the root; a schema, a model or a connector renamed in a tab other than *Schémas* and *Formules* goes to the end of its list (the store's `rename` is wired there only); `source_archive.Actions` gained a backend (JSON by default) so the «Formules» tab archives through the same row (spec docs/requirements/completed/2026-10-08-onglet-formules-editeur-dossiers-include.md, D-04, D-07, D-09, D-14, D-16).

- *[in force]* **A broad `except` raises again or says why on its line, settled (v0.117, issue #28).** The rule «a missing input warns, a malformed file stops» was undone wherever an `except Exception` swallowed the error: no stop, no warning.
