The audit of 10.10.2026 counted 87 `except Exception` in `calc/` (98 with `except BaseException` and a bare `except:`), 16 of them followed by `pass`, `continue` or `return None`. **Each was classed** (spec docs/requirements/completed/2026-10-10-audit-issues-26-a-33.md, annex A): (a) the export's path or a file Woodcore wrote — the handler raises again, the error naming the file (an annex, a plan's background, a document of an export, a DXF), or undoes what it did and raises; (b) what is missing is said — a warning, a notice, a message in the tab, a line of a log; (c) a legitimate fallback of the window or a cache, justified on its line; (d) dead code, none found. **Where the exception is known it is named** instead (12: a file whose tags `typst_objects` cannot read is a `ValueError`, a section that does not read a `ValidationError`, no project open a `ProjectError`, a timeout a `SubprocessError`…), so a defect of another kind is no longer turned into silence. **Where it is not** — pypdf and ezdxf raise families of their own, a thread hands any error to the window, an undo must not hide the error it precedes — the handler keeps `except Exception` and carries **`# broad: <reason>`** on its `except` line, the marker `# noqa: BLE001 -- …` (a linter's, which this project does not run) said the new way. **`tests/test_broad_except_guard.py`** reads `calc/` with `ast` and refuses a broad handler that neither raises nor carries a non-empty reason — a comment as the first line of the body, a `return False` or `return {}`, a handler spread over lines are all seen. **What the window reads and does not read is said**: a detail schema that does not read was left out of the connectors' and polar moments' forms without a word (two identical loops); `schema.readable_sources` now gives the ones that read and each one that does not with its reason, and the tab says it in a warning notice naming the file, which offers to open it — the export still stops on it (`read_sources`). **What it costs**: a marker on 85 lines, which a reader of the code skips; a handler narrowed too far would now let an error through that it once hid, which is the point, and the suite is the net (spec D-09: the error classes keep their parents).
- *[in force]* **Every check's verdict prints the ratio of the action to the resistance against 1 -- never the utilisation η, never the stress against the resistance --, η staying the registry's value, REVERSING v0.88's «η ⩽ 1 at level 1, the norm's relation from level 2» and the window's «η ⩽ 1» of 07.10.2026, settled (issue #12, 09.10.2026).** The engineer: «Faire afficher par l'indication de vérification le rapport σ / résistance ≤ 1, et non l'utilisation η, ni la forme σ ≤ résistance» -- and, asked: the display alone changes, η stays what `#val("eta", …)` and the summary of the checks read (Q-002 A); level 1 prints the ratio's symbols and its value (D-009); in percent `σ/f = 41 %` and nothing after it (D-010: «il ne faut pas indiquer en plus <= 100%»). **One source**: the line is built from the formula of the utilisation each check already passed (`timber-verdict(lv, eta, eta-symbol, eta-formula, unit)`), the norm's relation it used to pass beside it removed with `eta-line`; the window reads the formula of the governing ratio from `member_check.compute`'s additive `ratio` and never builds it, a test holding its value equal to the report's η. **The engine**: `verify` is unchanged for its callers; `verify-core` (internal) adds the brief level 1, a left-hand side shown in percent and a line without its right-hand side. **What it costs**: the norm's own inequality `σ ⩽ f` is no longer printed anywhere; a reader who wants it reads the ratio's numbers at level 2 or 3; the 45 frozen pages of the `verify-*` examples are new (spec docs/requirements/completed/2026-10-09-issues-github-10-11-12-14-16-18.md, D-006, D-009, D-010).
- *[in force]* **The notices stand between the views' switch and the languages', REVERSING 07.10.2026's «left of the views and the languages», settled (issue #11, 08.10.2026).** The engineer: «Placer les panneaux d'erreurs à gauche du sélecteur de langues et à droite du sélecteur de modes» -- the «modes» being, asked, the views of the tab (D-005), the navigation's «Mode» drop-down staying where it is. A closed row shows up to three lines of its message at the row's own width (`notices_ui.ElidedLabel`, worked out again at every width), its text selectable without opening the row; «Copier» is a right click or Ctrl+C away, «Tout copier» copies every notice shown (`notifications.copy_all`, Qt-free, the context said once) (spec docs/requirements/completed/2026-10-09-issues-github-10-11-12-14-16-18.md, D-005).
- *[in force]* **Every tab's own left pane folds by an arrow at its foot and narrows to a floor every tab shares, built once (`chrome.FoldablePane`, `cards.PANE_FLOOR`, `cards.loosen`), never by the splitter's own collapse, settled (issue #10, 08.10.2026).** The engineer: «Permettre de fermer ce volet dans tous les onglets … redimensionner ce volet à volonté … le volet bloque à une largeur minimale encore trop large». **Why the arrow and not `setCollapsible`**: a pane dragged to nothing has no arrow to come back by, and the layout store would keep a fraction of nothing for the next start; the window puts the pane round every tab's first pane when it binds its layout (`woodcore_gui.fold_left_pane`), the plan tabs build theirs themselves, and the fold is a setting (`left_folded`, every screen alike, as `nav_folded`), applied after the dragged sizes are put back, a pane folded never writing its arrow's width as a layout. **Why loosen and not a minimum width**: a minimum set on the pane would win over its contents and cut its buttons at its right edge; instead a button's text is cut with an ellipsis (its whole text in its tooltip), a title wraps and a drop-down asks for a few letters -- measured, the floor went from 240-435 px to 120-168 px. **What it costs**: a pane's buttons may read «Nouv…» at its narrowest; a widget a tab adds to its pane after it is built is not loosened and holds the pane at its own width, which is a wider pane and never a cut one (spec docs/requirements/completed/2026-10-09-issues-github-10-11-12-14-16-18.md, D-002).
- *[in force]* **An annexes' excerpt is made three ways -- a frame drawn, a range of pages, a frame found in the page -- and stays one page and one frame; the frames found are read by pypdf, with no new dependency, settled (issue #16, 08.10.2026).** The engineer: «Extraire en dessinant un cadre … en choisissant un nombre de pages … en choisissant un cadre trouvé dans la page» -- and, asked: pypdf rather than PyMuPDF (D-007: its AGPL licence and 30 MB in the installer), a frame drawn always makes an excerpt, Shift and a drag redrawing the frame of the one selected (D-012), the smallest frame under the pointer first and Alt with the wheel or a right click for the one round it (D-013). **The schema stays 1.0**: every way gives an anchor and a frame on one page -- a frame on the bookmark above it, or on its page and confirmed where none stands above; a page whole on its page. **The source's pages are the plans' view of a document's pages** (`document_view.PageArea`, its strip under each page made optional and a per-page overlay added), not a third viewer. **What it costs**: a frame drawn in PDF content pypdf cannot follow -- a PDF printed as pictures, a form nested more than eight deep -- offers nothing to find; a frame drawn just above a title takes the title before it (spec docs/requirements/completed/2026-10-09-issues-github-10-11-12-14-16-18.md, D-007, D-012, D-013).
- *[in force]* **A position plan's construction aids are the editor's alone -- saved with the plan (source 1.6), drawn and snapped to in Woodcore, never in the report nor on a sheet -- and the fire-protection plan has the moves and the copies but no aid yet, settled (issue #18, 09.10.2026).** The engineer: «Ajouter dans l'onglet Plan de positions les outils déplacer, copier et ligne d'aide à la construction» -- and, asked: four kinds of aid (D-008), copies one after another until Escape (D-014), «Objet sélectionné» with «Dupliquer» and «Supprimer» at the foot of the left pane (D-016), the aids on the position plan alone for now (D-015, rule 16's written exception: the fire plan's source is `fire-source` 2.0 and its collections all carry ids and points, which an aid has not). **One geometry**: an aid is stored as an axis without bubbles, so `axis_segment` draws it and the snapping takes it as an axis, behind «Accrocher aux axes»; a move is `plan.translate_entry` for the tool and for every drag alike (`plan.shifted`), a copy `plan.copy_entry` (a copied axis named among the others by `insert_axis`, an element keeping its position number); the fire plan answers the same gestures with `fire.translate_entry` and `fire.copy_entry`. **What it costs**: a plan saved by 0.117 after this is version 1.6, which an older Woodcore refuses; turning a page now turns its dimensions too, which #9 had left where they were (spec docs/requirements/completed/2026-10-09-issues-github-10-11-12-14-16-18.md, D-004, D-008, D-014, D-015, D-016).

- *[in force]* **The dead load, the snow and the wind are computed in Woodcore from SIA 261, and the load workbooks are still read beside them, settled (issues #35-#37, 11.10.2026).** The engineer: «Calcul en Python (`calc/domains/`, sans Qt), l'Excel ne sert que de référence», and, asked, the two kinds of case side by side (D-04). This reverses, for the cases entered in Woodcore, «the snow load is calculated in Excel, not in Python» (§3.3bis) and its wind twin (§3.3quater): a case of `Woodcore-Datei/loads/{dead,snow,wind}/` is computed by `dead_load_source`, `snow_source` or `wind_source` from `calc/norms/`, and joins the sheets' under the same domain (`calculations` 1.5: `engine` and `source_file` per instance, `figures`). **What it costs**: two roads into one domain, held together by the refusal of an id both carry and by one arithmetic where both compute (`dead_load.instance_from_layers`); a project carries its loads in two places until the engineer gives up the workbook. Where the norm and a workbook disagree the norm wins and the gap is written (D-02): five cells of the wind tables, three directions, the facade glass of the material library, model 2's half load of the snow sheets, eta_w,S of the Stützen workbook (docs/domain/loads.md, docs/domain/member-checks.md). Spec docs/requirements/completed/2026-10-10-issues-github-35-a-39.md, D-02, D-04.
- *[in force]* **The curved roof is the third column of Figur 3, settled (issue #36, 11.10.2026).** The issue's «modèle avec l'hémisphère» read on a render of SIA 261:2020 p. 28: a semicircular roof loaded where its slope is under alpha_0 = 60°, model 1 a uniform 0.8, model 2 the triangles mu_3/2 and mu_3, mu_3 = min(0.2 + 0.09 alpha, 2.0) of Figur 2 -- and not Annexe C's Tableau 63, the wind on hemispheres (D-01).
- *[in force]* **The wind covers every building of Annexe C, settled (issue #37, 11.10.2026).** Tableaux 31 to 60b, 63 and 64 -- 37 tables, one file each in `calc/norms/`, transcribed from the text layer by a script that counts the columns, checked on a render, held against the workbook's tables --; the bridges, cylinders, walls, the covered walkway and the sections are no buildings (D-06). c_red is computed from Figur 9's vector paths, not read off a scroll bar. **What it costs**: 37 files of hand-checked coefficients, and a generic computation -- external pressure, net pressure with the openings or with a surface's own c_pi, global forces per reference area -- that does not apply a table's particular rules: a platform canopy's directly computed Q1 is named and not computed, a semi-cylindrical roof's «mou» and «rigide» rows are enveloped together (§14).
- *[in force]* **The materials of a build-up come from SIA 261, the Belastungsannahmen library and the project, settled (issue #35, 11.10.2026).** The engineer: «SIA 261 + Bibliothèque de l'Excel (de base dans woodcore). L'ingénieur peut ajouter des poids volumiques manuellement» (D-07). The workbook's library is read out once into `calc/libraries/` -- not `calc/norms/`, being no norm -- with each entry's source; the project's own weights are `Woodcore-Datei/materials.json`. A layer names its library, so a material both carry is never chosen between in silence.
- *[in force]* **One tab «Poids propre et surcharge», settled (issue #35, 11.10.2026).** It replaces the reserved *Poids propre* and *Surcharge* of 08.10.2026 (D-08); each layer is g_k or a_k at the engineer's choice.
- *[in force]* **Buckling and lateral torsional buckling are kinds of their own, and a lateral torsional buckling takes the whole normal force, settled (issue #38, 11.10.2026).** `buckling` is `#verify-compression` with its stability, `compression` staying the section alone; `lateral-torsional` takes N signed, (37) with a compression -- the engineer's «N complet, (37) compris» (D-09) --, its buckling lengths in the same fields as a buckling's and entered in one shared card. The lengths are the system lengths and their coefficients, `l_y`, `l_z`, `beta_y`, `beta_z`, l_k = beta · l (D-10). A shear has no N. 1.5 V/A and 4/3 V/A are mechanics, SIA 265 (23) stating tau_d <= f_v,d (read on SIA265-2021-DE p. 33): `member_check.SHEAR_FACTORS`, held equal to Typst's, never in `calc/norms/`.
- *[superseded in v0.118]* **A check's result is always printed at -1, settled (issue #39, 11.10.2026).** *Superseded by issue #58 (14.10.2026, «A check's verdict is a key of -1» above): the result is the verdict, a key like any other.* The engineer: «Le résultat d'une commande doit toujours être affiché … Sa case est toujours cochée, légèrement grisée, non décochable», in the window and in Typst (D-05): `lib/shown-variables.json` names each verify-* command's `results`, printed at 1 where `shown:` leaves them out, refused at 0. **It reverses D-12 of issue #20 for the member checks**; the connectors and the polar moment keep it, their keys being the export's.
- *[in force]* **A field that follows a variable keeps its number beside the link, settled (issue #42, 11.10.2026).** The engineer chose, of the two shapes put to him at the start of the implementation, the link **beside** the field: the number stays where it was and is the last value known, and the entry's `links` names the variable by the field's key (`{"N": {"domain", "instance", "var", "factor"?, "offset"?, "origin"?}}`), rather than the field becoming a `{link, factor, offset, unit, last}` object (spec `2026-10-11-variables-liees-champs-et-textes`, D-17; `docs/domain/variable-links.md` §3.3duovicies). The link is **live** (D-03), resolved at every export against the values that export has just built, the «Formules» domains computed in memory against its loads; a variable that cannot be read keeps the last value with a warning, never a stop (D-06), and that last value is written by the window when it saves, never by the export, which writes no source (D-14). Only the export's variables may be named, never a document's registry, a link being resolved by Python without a compile (D-02); units are compatible by their dimension in `calc/units.py`, a field in ° taking only °, a field without a unit only a variable without one, never rad (D-15); a load may name only another load or a «Formules» domain, the loads being computed first (D-11). A static model's load magnitude keeps its reference form of v0.79 with `last` and `origin` added. **What it costs**: every reader of a number stays as it was, but the window and the export must reconcile the number with its variable (`links.apply_links`, `links.refresh`), and a source holds a number that may be older than its variable until it is saved again.
- *[in force]* **A connector and a polar moment follow no joint, and a load only another load or a «Formules» domain, settled (issue #42, 11.10.2026).** What a consumer may name is one table, `links.allowed`, read by the «Variables» tab's choosing mode and by the export (spec `2026-10-11-variables-liees-champs-et-textes`, ARCH-005, D-11, D-18): the loads are computed first, so a load follows another load or a «Formules» domain, built after what it follows, a circle stopping the export and refused as it is chosen; a connector and a polar moment give variables of their own and are built before each other's exist, so they follow a load, a script or a «Formules» domain, the export running the scripts and the «Formules» index before them; a member check and a static model follow anything. **What it costs**: a joint cannot take a value from another joint -- a connector's thickness from a polar moment's, say -- without a «Formules» domain in between, which would need the joint computed first.
- *[in force]* **One text engine for every figure, and one text object for the four drawing engines, settled (issue #43, 11.10.2026).** A figure's text -- a label, a note, a text placed with the Text tool -- is measured and drawn by `calc/domains/svg_text.py`, the static models' engine moved verbatim (its Arial widths, its subscript cut and drop), and a text placed on a drawing is `calc/domains/figure_text.py`'s and `calc/tools/text_pane.py`'s in the schemas, the static models, the position plans and the fire-protection plans alike (spec `2026-10-11-variables-liees-champs-et-textes`, D-13, D-19, ARCH-003, ARCH-006). The schemas had their own engine, which the review took for a copy of the static models' and was not -- another width table («m» 0.7222 against 0.833 em) and another subscript --; the engineer chose to unify rather than draw a text one way in two engines and another in the other two. A symbol is read once, by `calc/typst_math.py`, for the window's HTML and for a figure's text. A text is a token string `{1}` with its variables beside it, never Typst. **What it costs**: the schemas' labels drawn before move slightly (letter widths, subscript position), which the frozen schema digests no longer pin -- `tests/test_figure_style.py` compares a schema's geometry without its `<text>` lines --; the plans keep their own text for their position numbers and lengths.
- *[in force]* **A member in the fire situation is checked with SIA 265 (51), R_d,fi = 1.8 · R_d, settled (issue #46, 12.10.2026).** The engineer chose (Q-001, D-04) SIA 265:2021 4.5.2.5 over the k_fi · f_k of EN 1995-1-2 that the reference workbook *Bemessung im Brandfall Knicken* applies: the Swiss norm and the edition of the library. `#member(fire:)` reduces the section by d_ef on each exposed face and multiplies the design values along the grain by 1.8 -- η_w and η_t kept, (51) naming R_d «bei Normaltemperatur», k_h and k_sys left out --, and f_c,0,k and E_0,05 stay as they are, so a buckling's resistance is 1.8 · k_c · f_c,0,d. Price accepted: the buckling in fire of a softwood comes out some 15 % below that workbook's. The AEAI directives hold no method of their own (15-15 §4.1, §5); the engineer: the Lignum 3.1 «contient en fait les normes VKF-AEAI» (D-05). Each facade area keeps its own c_red in the rows 0°, 90° and 180°; every other row -- 15° included, not being «less than 15°» (the engineer, Q-001) -- takes the larger of the two, printed as `cred_max`. The direction is read off the row's label (`sia261_wind.direction`), the rule (`c_red.directions`) is the norm file's, and a row naming no direction (Tableau 64's) keeps its area's own. This partly supersedes the issue-#37 description of c_red «per b·h and d·h» (docs/domain/loads.md §3.3quater). Price accepted: the comparison with the Belastungsannahmen workbook, which takes one c_red per area in every direction, holds only where the extremes stand in a reference row (Tableau 33 does). Spec `docs/requirements/completed/2026-10-12-audit-issues-47-48-49.md`, D-03, D-18.

The dowel's grade (`dowel.steel`, S235 to S460, those meeting G.9) fills f_u,k, S355 by default; the value stays writable, a value typed over it or following a variable clears the grade, and the report credits SIA 263 Tabelle 1 and the grade only where the value is the grade's. `calc/norms/sn-en-14592-2022-stabduebel-werkstoff.json` replaces the 2012 file. Without a plate stated the connector is calculated as before and warns. - *[in force]* **SZS C5's profile tables are transcribed from the book, the reference Hilfstabelle being the control, settled (issue #50, 12.10.2026).** The engineer chose (D-04) to re-read SZS C5 rather than take the workbook's table: 1038 profiles of fifteen families, transcribed once from the scanned edition (`ExternalFiles/000-Normes/SZS-C5-2021.pdf`, the 2018 tables) into `calc/norms/szs-c5-2018-profile-*.json` in the book's own columns and units, each value checked against the OCR, the section's mechanics and the workbook, every disagreement re-read on the page at 330–400 dpi. Where the book is inconsistent with itself (eight values: HD 320x74 I_z, IPN 550 i_z, ROR 610x60 W_pl, …) the value is corrected in `szs_c5.ANOMALIES` with the recomputed number and the reason, and a check that reads one says so (Note `steel_c5_anomaly`); where the reference extract differs from the book, the book wins and the fixture `tests/fixtures/steel-reference-profiles.json` lists the difference. *Price*: fifteen files nobody can regenerate from a machine-readable source — a misread digit is caught only by the control's tolerance (the printed rounding) or by mechanics, not by a second reading.
- *[in force]* **Steel checks are computed once in Python and printed by Typst, settled (issue #51, 12.10.2026).** The timber checks write their formula in Typst (`verify-*`), and Python computes the same check again for the window. For steel the engineer chose (D-09) one calculation: `calc/domains/steel_resistance.py` computes each check as rows — vars, steps with their formula, clause and level, the ratio, the answer — that `data/steel.json` holds, the window shows and `#show-check-steel` (lib/steel.typ, D-17) prints through `show-calc` without evaluating anything; the ratio is registered as `<id>-verify-steel-<kind>` so the summary lists it. *Price*: a steel check cannot be written by hand in a `.typ` with its own numbers (no `verify-steel-*` command); the formula printed is a string Python wrote, which a test compiles but no second calculation checks — the hand calculations of `tests/test_steel_check.py` take that role.

Nothing of it reaches the instance, the export's notices or the report (D-04: the tab only, chosen over a notice at every export). And «pour la neige, il ne faut afficher aucun graphique», answered «retirer du rapport»: the `snow-load` instance carries neither `figures` nor `_files`, so `show-calc` prints no sketch at any level and `figure-1` is no key of it; the sketch stays in the tab's «Calcul» pane through `snow_source.sketch(src)` (D-03) -- a drawing the tab asks the domain for, since `registry` writes every `_files` an instance hands it. *Price*: a report computed above 2000 m does not say that it is outside the norm -- the engineer states it where wanted; a report that printed the snow's sketch loses it at the next export. Spec `docs/requirements/completed/2026-10-14-neige-vent-affichage-altitude.md`.
